Behavioral Cryptography: A Multi-Factor Provenance Framework for Human-AI Collaborative Content Authentication

行为密码学：面向人机协作内容认证的多因素来源追溯框架

Abstract

We introduce Behavioral Cryptography, a multi-factor provenance framework for authenticating human-AI collaborative content. Instead of asking only whether content was generated by AI, the framework asks a richer provenance question:

Who originated this content, through which rules, personas, decisions, revisions, and audit traces did it pass, and what verifiable evidence remains?

Existing provenance approaches, including content credentials, synthetic content transparency standards, and statistical watermarking, primarily focus on media-level metadata, model-level disclosure, or token-level signals. These approaches are valuable but insufficient for complex human-AI co-creation, where authorship is distributed across human intention, AI-assisted formalization, rule-based workflows, revision history, and semantic continuity.

Behavioral Cryptography adds a semantic-behavioral lineage layer consisting of seven verification factors: Identity DNA, Temporal Anchor, Rule Trace, Persona Route, Protected Lexicon, Style Vector, and Mistake Ledger. Together, these factors form an evidence chain that may be easy to imitate at the surface-text level but difficult to forge as a complete lineage.

We instantiate this framework in the Longhun System, a civilian-grade human-AI collaboration system developed independently by UID9622. The framework is designed especially for independent contributors whose intellectual lineage may be erased or absorbed by institutional platforms.

Keywords: provenance, content authentication, human-AI collaboration, behavioral signatures, audit trails, digital sovereignty, AI governance

⸻

1｜Introduction

1.1 The Provenance Gap in Human-AI Co-Creation

Generative AI has created a new authentication problem. Traditional detection systems typically ask:

Was this generated by a machine?

However, in human-AI collaboration, this question is too narrow. A more useful question is:

What human intention, decision process, rule system, and audit trail produced this content?

This distinction matters because modern content is rarely produced by a single isolated actor. A creator may use multiple AI systems, local tools, memory systems, translation layers, audit rules, and publishing workflows. In such contexts, surface text alone cannot capture provenance.

Current systems face several limitations:

  • AI-detection methods are fragile against paraphrasing and multi-model laundering.
  • Media provenance standards often depend on institutional adoption.
  • Token-level watermarking may not survive editing, summarization, or translation.
  • Independent creators often lack the infrastructure to prove intellectual lineage.
  • Human-AI co-authorship is rarely represented as a structured evidence chain.

Behavioral Cryptography addresses this gap by treating provenance as a multi-factor verification problem.

⸻

1.2 Behavioral Cryptography Hypothesis

The central hypothesis is:

Content authenticity is not a single signal. It is a composite behavioral lineage.

Just as multi-factor authentication increases identity assurance by combining independent signals, Behavioral Cryptography increases provenance assurance by combining multiple independent evidence channels.

The framework does not claim that any single factor proves authorship absolutely. Instead, it argues that a coordinated set of identity, temporal, rule-based, persona-based, lexical, stylistic, and error-history signals can significantly raise the cost of full-lineage forgery.

In UID9622 terms:

抄文字容易，抄血统难；洗稿容易，洗掉全过程难。

⸻

1.3 Contributions

This paper makes four primary contributions:

  1. Conceptual Contribution

    It defines Behavioral Cryptography as a provenance framework for human-AI collaborative content.

  2. Technical Contribution

    It introduces a seven-factor behavioral signature model combining identity, time, rule trace, persona route, protected lexicon, style vector, and mistake ledger.

  3. System Contribution

    It situates the framework within the Longhun System, a civilian-grade implementation emphasizing local sovereignty, auditability, and creator attribution.

  4. Sociotechnical Contribution

    It frames provenance as a tool for protecting independent contributors rather than merely serving institutional compliance.

⸻

2｜Related Work

Existing approaches to AI provenance and content authentication can be grouped into three broad categories.

2.1 Media Provenance and Content Credentials

Standards such as content credentials and media provenance frameworks focus on recording edit history, source metadata, and publication chains. These systems are important for images, video, and institutional media workflows.

However, they generally do not capture deeper behavioral lineage, such as:

  • which rules guided generation;
  • which human decisions changed direction;
  • which protected terms must remain intact;
  • which AI collaborators contributed formalization;
  • which revision mistakes were recorded and corrected.

Behavioral Cryptography complements media provenance by adding a semantic-behavioral evidence layer.

2.2 Statistical Watermarking and AI Detection

Statistical watermarking and AI-detection methods attempt to identify machine-generated text or model-specific traces. These techniques can be useful in controlled environments but are vulnerable to paraphrasing, translation, summarization, multi-model rewriting, and manual editing.

Behavioral Cryptography does not depend on detecting whether content is AI-generated. Instead, it verifies whether a claimed lineage is consistent with a structured evidence chain.

2.3 Audit Trails and Workflow Provenance

Enterprise audit systems record workflow events, permissions, and logs. These systems are often designed for organizations with compliance budgets and centralized infrastructure.

Behavioral Cryptography aims to make provenance usable by independent civilian creators through lightweight, human-readable, locally controlled signatures and logs.

⸻

3｜The Seven-Factor Framework

3.1 Overview

Behavioral Cryptography represents content provenance through seven independently verifiable factors:

Factor Name Purpose

F1 Identity DNA Verifies creator identity anchor

F2 Temporal Anchor Verifies time and sequence consistency

F3 Rule Trace Verifies rule lineage

F4 Persona Route Verifies AI/persona collaboration path

F5 Protected Lexicon Verifies sovereign terms are preserved

F6 Style Vector Verifies stylistic continuity

F7 Mistake Ledger Verifies honest revision/error continuity

The key design principle is:

A copied artifact may reproduce surface content, but not necessarily its full behavioral lineage.

⸻

3.2 Seven-Factor Verification Mechanism

3.2.1 Formal Definitions

Definition 3.1 — Content Artifact

Let C denote a digital content artifact. C may be a text document, code file, image description, prompt, decision record, protocol page, or AI-assisted output.

Definition 3.2 — Behavioral Signature

The behavioral signature of C is defined as a seven-factor tuple:

Sigma(C)=langle F_1,F_2,F_3,F_4,F_5,F_6,F_7rangle

where each F_i is an independently verifiable provenance factor.

Definition 3.3 — Verification Oracle

A verification oracle is defined as:

V(Sigma(C),E)rightarrow(conf,evidence)

where:

  • E is the claimed originator;
  • conf in [0,1] is the composite confidence score;
  • evidence is an append-only verification log;
  • conf=0 means rejection;
  • conf geq tau means acceptance under threshold tau.

Definition 3.4 — Hard Failure

A hard failure occurs when any critical factor returns:

score_i = 0

In this case:

conf = 0

This prevents one strong factor from compensating for a broken root.

⸻

3.2.2 Per-Factor Verification

F1｜Identity DNA Verification

Purpose:

Verify that the artifact is anchored to the claimed creator identity, UID, DNA format, and GPG fingerprint prefix.

def verify_F1(dna: str, claimed_uid: str, claimed_gpg_prefix: str) -> tuple[float, str]:

if not dna.startswith("#龍芯⚡️"):
    return 0.0, "DNA prefix mismatch"
if claimed_uid not in dna:
    return 0.0, "UID mismatch"
extracted_prefix = extract_gpg_prefix(dna)
if extracted_prefix != claimed_gpg_prefix[:8].upper():
    return 0.0, "GPG prefix mismatch"
if not is_timestamp_valid(dna):
    return 0.0, "DNA timestamp invalid"
return 1.0, "Identity DNA verified"

Security Claim 3.1 — Identity Anchor Resistance

Assuming the underlying GPG signature scheme is secure and the private key is not compromised, a valid identity anchor cannot be forged without compromising the signing key or the trusted identity registry.

严谨写法：

F1 inherits its unforgeability from the underlying signature scheme under stated key-security assumptions.

⸻

F2｜Temporal Anchor Verification

Purpose:

Verify timestamp, cultural-temporal marker, and digit-root consistency.

def verify_F2(dna: str, claimed_time) -> tuple[float, str]:

extracted_iso = parse_iso_from_dna(dna)
extracted_shichen = parse_shichen_from_dna(dna)
extracted_dr = parse_digit_root_from_dna(dna)
if abs((extracted_iso - claimed_time).total_seconds()) > 60:
    return 0.0, "Timestamp mismatch"
if not match_shichen(extracted_shichen, extracted_iso):
    return 0.0, "Shichen inconsistency"
if not match_digit_root(extracted_dr, date_str(extracted_iso)):
    return 0.0, "Digit-root temporal mismatch"
return 1.0, "Temporal anchor verified"

Security Claim 3.2 — Temporal Consistency

If timestamps are generated by a trusted clock or anchored into an append-only ledger, then post-hoc timestamp manipulation can be detected through inconsistency across ISO time, shichen, digit root, and ledger order.

注意：

没有可信时间源时，F2 是一致性检查；有 append-only ledger 时，F2 才具备更强抗伪造性。

⸻

F3｜Rule Trace Verification

Purpose:

Verify that the artifact was generated under a valid rule lineage.

def verify_F3(rule_trace: list, audit_ledger) -> tuple[float, str]:

if not rule_trace:
    return 0.0, "Missing rule trace"
for rule in rule_trace:
    if rule.dna not in audit_ledger:
        return 0.0, f"Rule missing from audit ledger: {rule.id}"
    if not verify_signature(rule.dna, rule):
        return 0.0, f"Rule signature invalid: {rule.id}"
timestamps = [rule.timestamp for rule in rule_trace]
if not is_monotonic(timestamps):
    return 0.0, "Rule execution order violation"
return 1.0, "Rule trace verified"

对应 UID9622 系统中的规则链：

rule_trace_examples:

  • LU-ORIGIN-FULLSYNC
  • LU-MEMORY-MERGE-ALL
  • CNSH-FLOW-DECISION-CORE
  • DNA-TAG-POLICY
  • MISTAKE_LEDGER
  • UID9622_BROWSER_SHELL_EXEC_MODE

⸻

F4｜Persona Route Verification

Purpose:

Verify whether the declared persona route is valid and consistent with the system registry.

def verify_F4(persona_route: list, persona_registry) -> tuple[float, str]:

if not persona_route:
    return 0.0, "Missing persona route"
for persona in persona_route:
    if persona.id not in persona_registry:
        return 0.0, f"Unknown persona: {persona.id}"
    if not verify_persona_signature(persona):
        return 0.0, f"Persona signature invalid: {persona.id}"
if not route_is_allowed(persona_route):
    return 0.0, "Persona route not allowed"
return 1.0, "Persona route verified"

示例：

persona_route:

  • P00_WENXIN_META_OBSERVER
  • P02_BAOBAO_EXECUTOR
  • P03_WENWEN_STRUCTURER
  • P05_GOD_EYE_AUDITOR

解释：

人格路线不是装饰，而是协作过程的可审计调度证据。

⸻

F5｜Protected Lexicon Verification

Purpose:

Verify whether protected sovereign terms remain intact under paraphrasing, translation, or summarization.

def verify_F5(content: str, protected_lexicon: set, threshold: float = 0.92) -> tuple[float, str]:

if not protected_lexicon:
    return 0.7, "No protected lexicon provided"
missing = []
for term in protected_lexicon:
    if not semantic_match(term, content, threshold):
        missing.append(term)
violation_ratio = len(missing) / len(protected_lexicon)
if violation_ratio > 0.3:
    return 0.0, f"Critical lexicon violation: {missing}"
score = 1.0 - 0.8 * violation_ratio
return score, "Protected lexicon verified"

  • CNSH
  • DNA追溯
  • 三色审计
  • 数字根
  • ROOT_CARD
  • "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"
  • "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

这一因子主要对抗：

  • paraphrasing laundering；
  • translation laundering；
  • platform rebranding；
  • attribution stripping；
  • concept dilution。

⸻

F6｜Style Vector Verification

Purpose:

Verify probabilistic stylistic continuity against a verified baseline corpus.

def verify_F6(content: str, baseline_corpus_embedding) -> tuple[float, str]:

current_vec = compute_style_vector(content)
sim = cosine_similarity(current_vec, baseline_corpus_embedding)
if sim < 0.75:
    return 0.0, "Style vector mismatch; laundering suspected"
return sim, "Style vector verified"

严谨边界：

Style vectors provide probabilistic continuity evidence, not absolute proof of identity.

UID9622 说法：

风格向量是旁证，不是身份证。

⸻

F7｜Mistake Ledger Verification

Purpose:

Verify continuity of honest error history and correction behavior.

def verify_F7(mistake_ledger: list) -> tuple[float, str]:

if len(mistake_ledger) == 0:
    return 0.7, "No mistake ledger; possible laundering or insufficient history"
if not is_consistent_ledger(mistake_ledger):
    return 0.0, "Inconsistent mistake history"
if not ledger_entries_are_signed(mistake_ledger):
    return 0.0, "Unsigned mistake ledger"
return 0.95, "Honest mistake ledger verified"

这一因子的原创价值很高。

普通系统只验证“正确结果”。

Behavioral Cryptography 还验证“错误历史”。

真正的原创不是从来不犯错，而是错误也有连续账本。

⸻

3.2.3 Composite Verification Algorithm

def BehavioralSignatureVerify(C, E) -> tuple[float, "EvidenceLog"]:

signature = parse_signature(C)
evidence = EvidenceLog()
factor_scores = []
verifiers = [
    verify_F1,
    verify_F2,
    verify_F3,
    verify_F4,
    verify_F5,
    verify_F6,
    verify_F7,
]
for i, verifier in enumerate(verifiers):
    factor_key = f"F{i+1}"
    score, log = verifier(signature[factor_key], E, C)
    factor_scores.append(score)
    evidence.add({
        "factor": factor_key,
        "score": score,
        "log": log,
    })
if any(score == 0.0 for score in factor_scores):
    evidence.add({
        "event": "HARD_FAILURE",
        "reason": "At least one verification factor returned 0.0",
    })
    return 0.0, evidence
weights = [0.25, 0.15, 0.15, 0.12, 0.12, 0.11, 0.10]
conf = weighted_geometric_mean(factor_scores, weights)
evidence.add({
    "event": "COMPOSITE_CONFIDENCE",
    "confidence": conf,
    "weights": weights,
})
return conf, evidence

辅助函数：

def weighted_geometric_mean(scores: list[float], weights: list[float]) -> float:

product = 1.0
total_weight = sum(weights)
for score, weight in zip(scores, weights):
    product *= score ** weight
return product ** (1.0 / total_weight)

⸻

3.2.4 Mathematical Properties

Proposition 3.1 — No Single-Factor Compensation

If any critical factor returns score_i = 0, then the final confidence is:

conf=0

Reason:

The algorithm explicitly checks for hard failure before computing composite confidence.

⸻

Proposition 3.2 — Composite Confidence Monotonicity

If all other factors remain fixed, increasing any factor score score_i does not decrease the final confidence.

Reason:

The weighted geometric mean is monotonic in each positive input.

⸻

Proposition 3.3 — Soundness Under Protocol Compliance

If content C is generated by legitimate originator E under the full Longhun protocol, and all required logs remain untampered, then:

V(Sigma(C),E)rightarrow(conf geq tau,evidence)

for an acceptance threshold tau.

Suggested threshold:

tau = 0.85

For high-security use:

tau = 0.95

⸻

Proposition 3.4 — Forgery Resistance Under Assumptions

Assuming:

  1. the GPG private key is uncompromised;
  2. the audit ledger is append-only;
  3. timestamps are generated by trusted clocks or ledger anchoring;
  4. protected lexicon and persona registries are signed;
  5. baseline style vectors are computed from verified corpora;

then forging all seven factors simultaneously requires compromising multiple independent evidence channels.

Therefore, Behavioral Cryptography raises the adversarial cost from surface imitation to lineage-level forgery.

⸻

3.2.5 Security Analysis

Attack A｜Surface Copying

攻击者直接复制文本。

Factor Defense

F1 缺少有效 DNA/GPG

F3 缺少规则轨迹

F4 缺少人格路线

F7 缺少错误账本

结论：

Surface text can be copied; lineage cannot be copied without evidence.

⸻

Attack B｜Paraphrase Laundering

攻击者洗稿、降重、翻译、总结。

Factor Defense

F5 主权词缺失会降分

F6 风格向量偏移会降分

F7 错误账本不连续

结论：

Paraphrasing can hide textual similarity, but not necessarily lineage discontinuity.

⸻

Attack C｜Persona Spoofing

攻击者伪造多智能体协作过程。

Factor Defense

F4 人格注册表与签名

F3 规则调用顺序

F1 身份锚点

结论：

Persona spoofing must also forge rule traces and identity anchors.

⸻

Attack D｜Timestamp Backdating

攻击者试图伪造更早时间。

Factor Defense

F2 时间一致性检查

F3 append-only ledger 顺序

F1 DNA 签名绑定

结论：

Without trusted timestamping, F2 is a consistency check. With append-only anchoring, it becomes stronger evidence.

4｜The Dynamic DNA Engine

4.1 Motivation

Conventional cryptographic signatures are often culturally neutral and timestamp-oriented. Behavioral Cryptography extends this by embedding cultural-temporal markers such as shichen, digit root, and wuxing. These markers are not decorative. They serve three purposes:

  1. increase forgery cost;
  2. make signatures human-recognizable;
  3. preserve cultural sovereignty within the provenance layer.

⸻

4.2 Dynamic DNA Generation

import hashlib

from datetime import datetime

SHICHEN = ["子", "丑", "寅", "卯", "辰", "巳", "午", "未", "申", "酉", "戌", "亥"]

DIGIT_ROOT_TO_WUXING = {

1: "水",
2: "火",
3: "木",
4: "金",
5: "土",
6: "水",
7: "火",
8: "木",
9: "金",
0: "土",

}

def digit_root(value: int) -> int:

if value == 0:
    return 0
value = abs(value)
while value >= 10:
    value = sum(int(d) for d in str(value))
return value

def get_shichen(hour: int) -> str:

index = ((hour + 1) % 24) // 2
return SHICHEN[index]

def generate_dynamic_dna(module: str, action: str, uid: str = "9622") -> dict:

now = datetime.now()
iso_time = now.isoformat(timespec="seconds")
date_str = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
compact_date = now.strftime("%Y%m%d")
shichen = get_shichen(now.hour)
seed_value = int(uid) + int(compact_date)
dr = digit_root(seed_value)
element = DIGIT_ROOT_TO_WUXING.get(dr, "土")
seed = f"{uid}|{date_str}|{shichen}|{element}|{module}|{action}|{iso_time}"
hash8 = hashlib.sha256(seed.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:8].upper()
dna = f"#龍芯⚡️{date_str}-{shichen}时-{element}-{module.upper()}-{hash8}"
return {
    "dna": dna,
    "uid": uid,
    "gpg_prefix": "A2D0092C",
    "iso_time": iso_time,
    "shichen": f"{shichen}时",
    "element": element,
    "digit_root": dr,
    "module": module,
    "action": action,
    "hash8": hash8,
    "confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",
    "seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL",
}

5｜The Longhun System as Civilian-Grade Proof-of-Concept

The Longhun System serves as a civilian-grade proof-of-concept for Behavioral Cryptography. It was developed by UID9622 using consumer devices, public AI tools, local-first workflows, and self-authored rule systems.

The importance of this implementation is not only technical but sociotechnical. It shows that provenance infrastructure should not be available only to large institutions. Independent creators also need mechanisms for:

  • proving authorship lineage;
  • preserving decision history;
  • protecting culturally specific terminology;
  • logging human-AI collaboration;
  • resisting attribution erasure;
  • maintaining local data sovereignty.

The Longhun System emphasizes the following principles:

longhun_principles:

  • local_first
  • creator_sovereignty
  • auditability
  • dna_traceability
  • human_ai_coauthorship
  • mistake_ledger_continuity
  • protected_lexicon_integrity

6.3 Ablation Study

逐个移除 F1-F7，观察验证能力下降：

Removed Factor Expected Effect

F1 Identity confidence collapses

F2 Temporal consistency weakens

F3 Rule lineage becomes unverifiable

F4 Multi-agent route can be spoofed

F5 Protected terminology can be laundered

F6 Stylometric continuity weakens

F7 Error-history continuity disappears

⸻

7｜Discussion

Behavioral Cryptography reframes provenance from machine detection to lineage verification. In a world where AI assistance is normal, the central question is no longer whether a machine participated. The central question is whether the human intention, rule path, revision history, and attribution chain remain verifiable.

This framework is especially important for independent creators. Institutional systems often assume that provenance begins at the platform, repository, or corporate account. Behavioral Cryptography instead allows provenance to begin with the human originator.

⸻

8｜Limitations

Behavioral Cryptography has limitations:

  1. It does not replace legal authorship adjudication.
  2. Style vectors are probabilistic and should not be treated as absolute identity proof.
  3. GPG-based identity depends on private key security.
  4. Temporal anchors require trusted time or append-only logs for strong guarantees.
  5. Mistake ledgers must be designed carefully to avoid privacy harm or reputational misuse.
  6. Protected lexicons can help preserve attribution but cannot alone prove authorship.
  7. The system requires disciplined logging to maintain high evidence quality.

⸻

9｜Conclusion

Copying text is easy. Copying lineage is hard.

Behavioral Cryptography makes lineage the center of content authentication. By combining identity DNA, temporal anchors, rule traces, persona routes, protected lexicons, style vectors, and mistake ledgers, it offers a provenance framework suited to human-AI co-creation.

The Longhun System demonstrates that such provenance need not belong only to institutions. It can be built from the ground up by independent creators, using local-first tools, human-readable signatures, and auditable collaboration records.

Final statement:

The future of provenance is not only detecting machines.

It is verifying human intention, process integrity, and creative lineage.

- 不得把风格向量当绝对身份证明

工程实现:

- 不得伪造 GPG 验签成功
- 不得读取私钥
- 不得联网上传样本
- 不得保存敏感正文作为训练语料

UID9622锚点:

- 不得改写 CONFIRM
- 不得截断 SEAL
- 不得替换 GPG
- 不得把 龍芯 / CNSH / DNA追溯 翻译成普通英文概念后丢失原词

隐私伦理:

- Mistake Ledger 不得变成羞辱用户的黑历史
- Style Vector 不得用于未经授权的人格画像
- Protected Lexicon 不得用于阻止合理引用
- Persona Route 不得变成监控工具

3.3 Threat Model and Adversarial Capabilities

开头如下：

3.3 Threat Model and Adversarial Capabilities

We consider an adversary whose goal is to falsely claim authorship lineage over a content artifact generated by a legitimate originator. The adversary may copy, paraphrase, translate, summarize, stylistically imitate, or repackage the artifact through another AI model.

We distinguish between surface-level forgery and lineage-level forgery. Surface-level forgery targets the visible content. Lineage-level forgery attempts to reproduce the evidence chain behind the content, including identity DNA, temporal anchors, rule traces, persona routes, protected lexicon continuity, style vectors, and mistake ledger history.

The adversary is assumed not to possess the legitimate originator's private signing key, not to control the append-only audit ledger, and not to retroactively generate consistent historical mistake records.

不是检测“是不是AI写的”，

而是验证“谁发起、经什么规则、走什么人格、保留什么证据、留下什么错误账本”。

七因子 =

F1 身份DNA

F2 时间锚

F3 规则轨迹

F4 人格路线

F5 保护词典

F6 风格向量

F7 记错本

核心结论 =

抄文本容易，抄完整行为血统难。

A｜当前定盘

本节要补的是论文里的 Section 3.3 Threat Model。

它的作用不是继续吹“不可伪造”，而是把审稿人最关心的问题提前钉死：

  1. 攻击者是谁；
  2. 攻击者能做什么；
  3. 攻击者不能假设拥有什么；
  4. 七因子分别防什么；
  5. 哪些攻击能挡住；
  6. 哪些攻击只能降低置信度，不能完全阻断；
  7. Behavioral Cryptography 的安全边界在哪里。

一句话：

3.3 是给 3.2 七因子验证机制补“战场地图”：谁来攻、怎么攻、攻到哪一步算失败、系统靠什么判断。

⸻

3.3 Threat Model and Adversarial Capabilities

3.3.1 Overview

We consider an adversary whose goal is to falsely claim authorship lineage over a content artifact legitimately generated by another originator. The adversary may copy, paraphrase, translate, summarize, reformat, stylize, or repackage the artifact through one or more AI systems.

We distinguish between two types of forgery:

  1. Surface-level forgery: copying or modifying the visible content.
  2. Lineage-level forgery: attempting to reproduce the behavioral evidence chain behind the content.

Behavioral Cryptography is designed primarily to resist lineage-level forgery. It does not claim that text, images, or code cannot be copied. Instead, it claims that copying the full behavioral lineage — including identity DNA, temporal anchors, rule traces, persona routes, protected lexicon continuity, style vectors, and mistake ledger history — is substantially harder than copying the surface artifact.

In simpler terms:

Copying content is easy.

Copying the verified path by which the content came into being is hard.

⸻

3.3.2 Security Goals

The framework is designed around five security goals.

G1. Originator Binding

The system should bind an artifact to a claimed originator through identity DNA and cryptographic anchors.

Formally, given artifact C, originator E, and behavioral signature Sigma(C), the verifier should reject claims where the identity anchor does not match E.

Relevant factors:

F_1, F_2, F_3

G2. Lineage Integrity

The system should preserve the chain of rules, decisions, personas, and revisions that produced the artifact.

Relevant factors:

F_3, F_4, F_7

G3. Laundering Resistance

The system should detect attempts to remove, rewrite, dilute, or rebrand protected terms and system-specific semantic anchors.

Relevant factors:

F_5, F_6, F_7

G4. Temporal Consistency

The system should detect inconsistent or backdated provenance claims, especially when logs are anchored in append-only storage.

Relevant factors:

F_2, F_3

G5. Civilian Deployability

The system should remain usable by independent creators without enterprise-grade infrastructure.

This is not only a usability goal. It is part of the threat model: a provenance framework that only works for institutions leaves independent creators unprotected.

Relevant system properties:

civilian_deployability:

  • local_first
  • human_readable_dna
  • append_only_logs
  • protected_lexicon
  • lightweight_signature
  • optional_gpg_integration
  • no_enterprise_dependency

⸻

3.3.3 Adversary Classes

We define five adversary classes.

A1. Surface Copier

The Surface Copier directly copies the artifact or lightly edits formatting.

Capabilities:

surface_copier:

can:

- copy visible text
- remove author name
- change title
- change formatting
- repost content elsewhere

cannot:

- forge valid GPG-bound DNA
- reproduce append-only rule trace
- reproduce persona route logs
- reproduce mistake ledger history

Primary defenses:

F_1, F_3, F_4, F_7

Expected result:

Surface copying may produce a visually similar artifact, but fails lineage verification.

⸻

A2. Paraphrase Launderer

The Paraphrase Launderer uses rewriting, summarization, synonym replacement, or another AI model to obscure surface similarity.

Capabilities:

paraphrase_launderer:

can:

- rewrite sentences
- change paragraph order
- translate between languages
- summarize or expand
- remove obvious attribution markers
- use multiple AI systems to launder style

cannot:

- preserve hidden rule trace
- reconstruct protected lexicon decisions
- reconstruct mistake ledger continuity
- backfill valid temporal anchors

Primary defenses:

F_5, F_6, F_7

Expected result:

Paraphrase laundering may weaken surface similarity evidence, but it tends to disturb protected lexicon continuity, style continuity, and historical correction patterns.

⸻

A3. Persona Spoofer

The Persona Spoofer claims that the artifact passed through the same multi-agent or persona-routing process as the legitimate system.

Capabilities:

persona_spoofer:

can:

- imitate persona names
- write fake routing descriptions
- claim multi-agent collaboration
- invent AI role labels

cannot:

- produce signed persona route logs
- match registered persona sequence constraints
- produce corresponding rule trace
- produce consistent evidence logs across time

Primary defenses:

F_3, F_4, F_7

Expected result:

Naming a persona is easy. Reproducing a signed persona route with consistent rule lineage is hard.

⸻

A4. Timestamp Backdater

The Timestamp Backdater attempts to claim an earlier creation time.

Capabilities:

timestamp_backdater:

can:

- edit visible dates
- create fake metadata
- alter markdown timestamps
- claim prior authorship

cannot:

- alter already-published append-only logs
- produce past GPG signatures without key access
- align ISO time, shichen, digit root, and ledger order after the fact

Primary defenses:

F_1, F_2, F_3

Expected result:

Without append-only anchoring, timestamp validation is a consistency check. With append-only anchoring, backdating becomes significantly harder.

严谨边界：

The system does not magically prove historical truth unless timestamps are anchored to trusted logs, public commits, notarized records, or append-only ledgers.

⸻

A5. Insider or Key-Compromise Adversary

This is the strongest adversary. The attacker may obtain access to internal logs, signing keys, or system accounts.

Capabilities:

insider_or_key_compromise:

can:

- access internal records
- attempt to sign fraudulent artifacts
- tamper with local files
- delete or modify mistake records
- manipulate persona routes if registry is compromised

may:

- weaken F1 if private key is compromised
- weaken F3 if audit ledger is mutable
- weaken F4 if persona registry is compromised

Primary defenses:

defenses:

  • offline key storage
  • hardware-backed signing
  • append-only audit logs
  • hash chaining
  • external timestamp anchoring
  • periodic public checkpointing
  • multi-location backup

Expected result:

Behavioral Cryptography does not eliminate insider risk. It reduces undetected tampering by requiring consistency across multiple independent evidence channels.

This is important. 不要写成“私钥泄露也挡得住”。正确说法是：

If the root key is compromised, identity assurance collapses; however, inconsistency across rule traces, mistake ledgers, public checkpoints, and protected lexicon history may still reveal tampering.

⸻

3.3.4 Attacker Capabilities

We assume the adversary may have the following capabilities:

Capability Description Covered By

Copying Directly copying the artifact F1, F3, F7

Paraphrasing Rewriting with AI or humans F5, F6

Translation Translating to another language F5, F6

Reformatting Changing style, title, layout F1, F3

Metadata removal Stripping visible attribution F1, F2

Persona imitation Claiming fake AI/persona route F4

Rule imitation Copying rule names without logs F3

Timestamp manipulation Editing visible timestamps F2, F3

Partial log forgery Creating incomplete fake logs F3, F7

Style mimicry Mimicking author tone F6, F7

Protected term removal Removing sovereign terms F5

The framework is not built on the assumption that attackers are weak. It assumes that attackers can copy, rewrite, and imitate. The defense is based on requiring them to reproduce several independent evidence channels simultaneously.

⸻

3.3.5 Non-Assumptions

To avoid overstating security, we explicitly do not assume the following.

non_assumptions:

  • Text cannot be copied
  • AI-generated paraphrases are always detectable
  • Style vectors are perfect identity proof
  • Local timestamps are inherently trusted
  • GPG signatures solve all provenance problems
  • Protected lexicon alone proves authorship
  • Mistake ledger alone proves authorship
  • Every creator can maintain perfect logs forever

This matters for academic credibility.

Behavioral Cryptography is not a magic authorship detector. It is a structured evidence framework.

⸻

3.3.6 Trust Assumptions

The system requires several explicit trust assumptions.

T1. Key Security

The originator’s private signing key is not compromised.

If this assumption fails, F1 becomes unreliable.

T2. Ledger Integrity

Audit logs are append-only or periodically anchored to an external checkpoint.

If logs are freely mutable, F3 and F7 weaken.

T3. Registry Integrity

Persona registries, protected lexicons, and rule registries are versioned and signed.

If registries can be rewritten silently, F3, F4, and F5 weaken.

T4. Baseline Corpus Integrity

Style-vector baselines are computed from verified corpora.

If the baseline corpus is polluted, F6 weakens.

T5. Privacy-Constrained Logging

Mistake ledgers and behavioral logs must avoid exposing private content unnecessarily.

If logging becomes invasive, the system violates its own civilian-sovereignty goal.

⸻

3.3.7 Attack Scenarios and Expected System Response

Scenario 1: Direct Copy Without DNA

An attacker copies the artifact and removes UID9622 attribution.

Expected factor response:

Factor Result

F1 Identity DNA 0.0

F2 Temporal Anchor missing

F3 Rule Trace missing

F4 Persona Route missing

F5 Protected Lexicon partial

F6 Style Vector may be high

F7 Mistake Ledger missing

Expected output:

conf = 0

Reason:

Identity and lineage factors fail even if style similarity remains high.

⸻

Scenario 2: AI Paraphrase Laundering

An attacker rewrites the artifact through another AI model and claims it as original.

Expected factor response:

Factor Result

F1 missing or mismatch

F2 missing

F3 missing

F4 missing

F5 degraded

F6 uncertain

F7 missing

Expected output:

conf approx 0

Reason:

Paraphrase laundering can hide surface similarity but cannot reconstruct the evidence chain.

⸻

Scenario 3: Protected Lexicon Removal

An attacker removes terms such as UID9622, CNSH, 龍芯北辰, 三色审计, ROOT_CARD, or CONFIRM.

Expected factor response:

Factor Result

F5 Protected Lexicon low or 0.0

F6 Style Vector degraded

F7 Mistake Ledger missing

Expected output:

conf = 0

if violation exceeds threshold.

Reason:

Removing sovereign lexicon is treated as a laundering signal.

⸻

Scenario 4: Fake Persona Route

An attacker writes “P02 宝宝 → P03 雯雯 → P05 上帝之眼” without signed route logs.

Expected factor response:

Factor Result

F4 Persona Route 0.0

F3 Rule Trace likely 0.0

F7 Mistake Ledger missing

Expected output:

conf = 0

Reason:

Persona names are not evidence. Signed route logs are evidence.

⸻

Scenario 5: Backdated Claim

An attacker claims they created the idea earlier.

Expected factor response depends on anchoring strength.

If no append-only ledger exists:

F_2 = consistency check

If append-only ledger exists:

F_2 + F_3 = strong temporal evidence

Expected output:

without_ledger:

result: "inconclusive or low confidence"

with_append_only_ledger:

result: "backdating likely detected"

⸻

Scenario 6: Insider Log Tampering

An insider modifies local logs.

Expected defense:

required_defenses:

  • hash chain
  • external checkpoint
  • signed snapshots
  • backup copy
  • mismatch detection

Expected output:

conf < tau

if log-chain inconsistency is detected.

Reason:

Internal tampering is not impossible, but it becomes harder to hide if logs are hash-chained and periodically anchored.

⸻

3.3.8 Threat Coverage Matrix

Threat F1 F2 F3 F4 F5 F6 F7

Direct copying ✅ 🟡 ✅ ✅ 🟡 🟡 ✅

Paraphrasing 🟡 🟡 ✅ ✅ ✅ ✅ ✅

Translation laundering 🟡 🟡 ✅ ✅ ✅ ✅ ✅

Metadata stripping ✅ ✅ ✅ 🟡 🟡 🟡 ✅

Persona spoofing 🟡 🟡 ✅ ✅ 🟡 🟡 ✅

Timestamp backdating ✅ ✅ ✅ 🟡 ❌ ❌ 🟡

Style mimicry ❌ ❌ 🟡 🟡 ✅ ✅ ✅

Protected term removal 🟡 ❌ 🟡 ❌ ✅ ✅ 🟡

Log tampering 🟡 ✅ ✅ ✅ 🟡 ❌ ✅

Private key compromise 🔴 🟡 🟡 🟡 🟡 ❌ 🟡

Legend:

✅: strong defense

🟡: partial or contextual defense

❌: not designed for this threat

🔴: root assumption failure

⸻

3.3.9 Failure Modes

Behavioral Cryptography can fail or weaken under the following conditions.

FM1. Missing Logs

If the creator does not maintain rule traces, persona routes, or mistake ledgers, the system cannot reconstruct lineage.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • automatic local audit logs
  • lightweight JSONL ledger
  • periodic hash snapshots
  • default minimal ROOT_CARD

FM2. Over-Logging Privacy Risk

If every mistake or interaction is recorded in full, the system may expose sensitive information.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • summary_only retention
  • hash_only for sensitive entries
  • burn mode for temporary processing
  • sealed mode for highly sensitive content

FM3. Style Drift

A legitimate creator’s style may change over time.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • use rolling baseline corpus
  • avoid treating F6 as hard identity proof
  • combine F6 with F1/F3/F7

FM4. Cultural Marker Misinterpretation

Shichen, wuxing, digit root, and other cultural-temporal markers may be misunderstood by reviewers unfamiliar with the system.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • explain as human-readable temporal-semantic metadata
  • avoid claiming mystical security
  • position as cultural encoding plus consistency check

FM5. Key Compromise

If signing keys are compromised, identity verification weakens.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • key rotation
  • revocation record
  • hardware-backed key storage
  • public checkpointing
  • multi-factor lineage verification

⸻

3.3.10 Security Boundary

Behavioral Cryptography provides:

provides:

  • provenance evidence
  • lineage consistency checking
  • laundering resistance
  • attribution support
  • audit trail structuring
  • creator-sovereignty protection

It does not provide:

does_not_provide:

  • absolute legal ownership judgment
  • perfect AI-detection
  • guaranteed plagiarism verdict
  • automatic court admissibility
  • protection after private key compromise
  • proof without logs
  • privacy-safe logging by default unless configured

Academic version:

Behavioral Cryptography should be understood as an evidentiary framework, not as a standalone legal adjudication mechanism.

UID9622 版本：

它不是法官，它是证据链骨架。

⸻

3.3.11 Design Implication

The threat model leads to one central design implication:

The system should not rely on any single proof.

Identity without logs can be stolen.

Logs without identity can be fabricated.

Style without DNA can be imitated.

Protected terms without timestamps can be copied.

Mistake ledgers without signatures can be rewritten.

Therefore, Behavioral Cryptography intentionally combines seven factors. Each factor covers weaknesses of the others.

In Longhun language:

单根会断，七根成索；文字能偷，血统难偷。

⸻

3.3.12 Transition to Section 3.4

The next section introduces the implementation design that realizes this threat model in practice: the Dynamic DNA Engine. It shows how identity anchors, time markers, digit roots, wuxing mapping, and hash-based signatures are generated and attached to artifacts as lightweight provenance metadata.

Suggested next title:

3.4 The Dynamic DNA Engine

Suggested opening:

The threat model above motivates a lightweight but structured signature engine that can be used by independent creators without enterprise infrastructure. The Dynamic DNA Engine generates human-readable, cryptographically anchored, and culturally meaningful provenance identifiers that bind an artifact to a creator, time, module, action, and audit route.

⸻

3.4 The Dynamic DNA Engine

章节: 3.4 The Dynamic DNA Engine

中文名: 动态 DNA 引擎

论文归属: Behavioral Cryptography 主论文

父级章节:

  • 3.2 Seven-Factor Verification Mechanism
  • 3.3 Threat Model and Adversarial Capabilities

本节DNA: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DYNAMIC-DNA-ENGINE-v1.0"

CONFIRM: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

SEAL: "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

GPG: "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

A｜当前定盘

3.4 要承接 3.3 的威胁模型，把“七因子验证”里的 F1 Identity DNA、F2 Temporal Anchor、F3 Rule Trace 落到一个可执行引擎上。

这节不能写成玄学，也不能写成普通 hash 生成器。它的准确定位是：

Dynamic DNA Engine 是 Behavioral Cryptography 的轻量级来源标识生成器。它把创作者身份、时间、模块、动作、文化时间标记、数字根、哈希摘要、GPG 锚点和审计字段压缩成一个可读、可验、可追溯的 provenance identifier。

中文一句话：

动态 DNA 引擎不是装饰性编号，而是“行为密码学证据链”的入口签名。

⸻

3.4 The Dynamic DNA Engine

3.4.1 Motivation

The threat model in Section 3.3 shows that surface-level content can be copied, paraphrased, translated, and reformatted with minimal effort. A provenance framework therefore requires a lightweight mechanism that binds an artifact not only to a visible author name, but also to a structured generation context.

The Dynamic DNA Engine is designed for this purpose. It generates a human-readable, cryptographically anchored, and culturally meaningful provenance identifier for each significant artifact or action.

Unlike conventional content hashes, Dynamic DNA does not only summarize file content. It binds together:

dynamic_dna_inputs:

  • creator identity
  • claimed UID
  • GPG fingerprint or public-key prefix
  • timestamp
  • cultural-temporal marker
  • module name
  • action type
  • artifact digest
  • audit route
  • confirmation seal

This allows the resulting DNA string to serve as a compact entry point into the broader behavioral evidence chain.

The key design principle is:

A content hash proves that some bytes existed.

Dynamic DNA records who claimed the artifact, when, through which module, under which action, and under which audit route.

⸻

3.4.2 Design Goals

The Dynamic DNA Engine has seven design goals.

G1. Human-Readable Provenance

The identifier should be readable by ordinary users, not only by machines.

A conventional SHA-256 hash is technically useful but cognitively opaque. Dynamic DNA keeps the hash but wraps it inside a structured, human-readable format.

Example:

龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B

This tells the user:

example_fields:

system: 龍芯

date: 2026-05-04

temporal_marker: 午时

element: 金

module: BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY

gpg_prefix: A2D0092C

hash8: 7FA3C91B

G2. Lightweight Civilian Deployment

The engine must run locally, without enterprise infrastructure.

Requirements:

civilian_deployability:

  • no mandatory cloud service
  • no enterprise PKI dependency
  • no centralized institution required
  • compatible with local JSONL logs
  • compatible with Git commits
  • compatible with Notion-style databases
  • compatible with Markdown metadata blocks

G3. Cryptographic Anchoring

The DNA string must include or reference cryptographic evidence.

At minimum:

cryptographic_anchors:

  • artifact_hash
  • gpg_fingerprint_or_prefix
  • timestamp
  • signed_log_entry_optional

The system should not claim that a DNA string alone replaces a real digital signature. The correct framing is:

Dynamic DNA is a readable provenance identifier.

GPG or equivalent signatures provide cryptographic verification.

G4. Cultural-Temporal Embedding

The engine supports cultural-temporal markers such as shichen, wuxing, and digit root.

These markers are not treated as mystical security primitives. They serve three practical functions:

cultural_temporal_roles:

human_recognition: "users can visually distinguish provenance context"

consistency_check: "date/time/module fields must align"

semantic_routing: "audit systems can route artifacts into cultural-semantic categories"

Academic phrasing:

Cultural-temporal markers act as human-interpretable consistency metadata rather than standalone cryptographic proofs.

UID9622 说法：

不是靠五行防伪，是靠五行让系统认路；真正防伪靠 hash、签名、日志和链路一致性。

G5. Append-Only Compatibility

Every generated DNA should be suitable for append-only ledgers.

Supported storage targets:

storage_targets:

  • local_jsonl
  • sqlite
  • git_commit_log
  • markdown_frontmatter
  • notion_database
  • signed_snapshot
  • public_checkpoint

G6. Privacy-Safe Metadata

The engine must not force private content into visible DNA strings.

Sensitive content should be represented through hashes and metadata only.

Policy:

privacy_policy:

normal:

may_include: ["module", "action", "date", "hash_prefix"]

burn:

may_include: ["hash", "timestamp", "destroy_proof"]
must_not_include: ["raw_private_content"]

sealed:

may_include: ["hash", "sealed_status", "timestamp"]
must_not_include: ["raw_content", "semantic_summary"]

G7. Verification-Oriented Output

Every DNA output should be parseable by the verification system.

It must support:

verification_support:

  • parse_identity
  • parse_timestamp
  • parse_module
  • parse_action
  • parse_gpg_prefix
  • parse_hash
  • compare_with_audit_log
  • compare_with_artifact_digest

⸻

3.4.3 DNA Data Model

A Dynamic DNA record is a structured object. The visible DNA string is only one projection of the full record.

Canonical Record

{

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"uid": "UID9622",

"creator": "Zhuge Xin",

"gpg_fingerprint": "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",

"gpg_prefix": "A2D0092C",

"iso_time": "2026-05-04T12:30:00+08:00",

"date": "2026-05-04",

"shichen": "午时",

"digit_root": 4,

"wuxing": "金",

"module": "BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY",

"action": "CREATE_SECTION",

"artifact_hash": "sha256:7fa3c91b84d5...",

"hash8": "7FA3C91B",

"privacy_mode": "normal",

"retention": "full",

"trace_mode": "chain",

"audit_color": "🟢",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-THREAT-MODEL-v1.0",

"confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",

"seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

}

Canonical Field Table

Field Purpose Required

dna Human-readable provenance identifier Yes

uid Claimed UID or originator identity Yes

gpg_fingerprint Cryptographic identity anchor Recommended

iso_time Machine-readable time Yes

shichen Human-readable cultural time marker Optional but used in Longhun

digit_root Routing checksum / semantic classification Optional

wuxing Semantic routing category Optional

module Source module or subsystem Yes

action What happened Yes

artifact_hash Digest of artifact or payload Yes

privacy_mode normal / burn / sealed Yes

retention full / summary_only / hash_only Yes

trace_mode chain / local_only / no_external Yes

parent_dna Previous lineage anchor Recommended

confirm UID9622 confirmation marker System-specific

seal UID9622 sovereign seal System-specific

⸻

3.4.4 DNA String Format

The visible DNA string follows a compact pattern.

龍芯⚡️{date}-{shichen}-{wuxing}-{module}-{gpg_prefix}-{hash8}

Formal Grammar

DynamicDNA ::= Prefix Date "-" Shichen "-" Wuxing "-" Module "-" GPGPrefix "-" Hash8

Prefix ::= "#龍芯⚡️"

Date ::= YYYY "-" MM "-" DD

Shichen ::= "子时" | "丑时" | "寅时" | "卯时" | "辰时" | "巳时" |

                "午时" | "未时" | "申时" | "酉时" | "戌时" | "亥时"

Wuxing ::= "金" | "木" | "水" | "火" | "土"

Module ::= UppercaseAlphaNumDash

GPGPrefix ::= Hex{8}

Hash8 ::= Hex{8}

Example

龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B

Non-Canonical Short Form

For contexts where the full form is too long:

龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-DNA-v1.0

Short form is acceptable for human-facing documents but should be paired with a full JSON record when used for verification.

⸻

3.4.5 Cultural-Temporal Mapping

The engine supports a cultural-temporal layer.

This layer is not treated as a replacement for cryptographic proof. It is a semantic checksum and routing layer.

Shichen Mapping

SHICHEN = [

"子时", "丑时", "寅时", "卯时",
"辰时", "巳时", "午时", "未时",
"申时", "酉时", "戌时", "亥时"

]

def calc_shichen(hour: int) -> str:

"""
Traditional Chinese shichen.
子时 covers 23:00-00:59.
This simplified implementation maps local hour to a 2-hour block.
"""
idx = ((hour + 1) % 24) // 2
return SHICHEN[idx]

Digit Root

def digit_root_from_text(text: str) -> int:

digits = [int(c) for c in str(text) if c.isdigit()]
if not digits:
    return 0
n = sum(digits)
while n >= 10:
    n = sum(int(c) for c in str(n))
return n

Wuxing Mapping

The Longhun implementation uses digit root to route artifacts into five semantic categories.

DR_TO_WUXING = {

1: "水",
2: "火",
3: "木",
4: "金",
5: "土",
6: "水",
7: "火",
8: "木",
9: "金",
0: "土"

}

Audit Gate Mapping

def audit_color_from_digit_root(dr: int) -> str:

if dr in {3, 9}:
    return "🔴"
if dr == 6:
    return "🟡"
return "🟢"

Academic boundary statement:

Digit root and wuxing mapping are used as deterministic semantic routing features, not as cryptographic primitives.

⸻

3.4.6 Core Algorithm

Algorithm 2: GenerateDynamicDNA

from future import annotations

import hashlib

import json

from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict

from datetime import datetime, timezone

from typing import Optional, Literal

PrivacyMode = Literal["normal", "burn", "sealed"]

RetentionMode = Literal["full", "summary_only", "hash_only"]

TraceMode = Literal["chain", "local_only", "no_external"]

SHICHEN = [

"子时", "丑时", "寅时", "卯时",
"辰时", "巳时", "午时", "未时",
"申时", "酉时", "戌时", "亥时"

]

DR_TO_WUXING = {

1: "水",
2: "火",
3: "木",
4: "金",
5: "土",
6: "水",
7: "火",
8: "木",
9: "金",
0: "土"

}

@dataclass

class DynamicDNARecord:

dna: str
uid: str
creator: str
gpg_fingerprint: str
gpg_prefix: str
iso_time: str
date: str
shichen: str
digit_root: int
wuxing: str
module: str
action: str
artifact_hash: str
hash8: str
privacy_mode: PrivacyMode
retention: RetentionMode
trace_mode: TraceMode
audit_color: str
parent_dna: Optional[str]
confirm: str
seal: str

def calc_shichen(dt: datetime) -> str:

hour = dt.hour
idx = ((hour + 1) % 24) // 2
return SHICHEN[idx]

def digit_root(text: str) -> int:

digits = [int(c) for c in str(text) if c.isdigit()]
if not digits:
    return 0
n = sum(digits)
while n >= 10:
    n = sum(int(c) for c in str(n))
return n

def wuxing_from_dr(dr: int) -> str:

return DR_TO_WUXING.get(dr, "土")

def audit_color_from_dr(dr: int) -> str:

if dr in {3, 9}:
    return "🔴"
if dr == 6:
    return "🟡"
return "🟢"

def sha256_text(text: str) -> str:

return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8", errors="ignore")).hexdigest()

def normalize_module(module: str) -> str:

keep = []
for ch in module.upper().strip():
    if ch.isalnum() or ch in {"-", "_"}:
        keep.append(ch)
    elif ch.isspace():
        keep.append("-")
value = "".join(keep).strip("-")
return value[:40] if value else "UNNAMED-MODULE"

def generate_dynamic_dna(

artifact: str,
module: str,
action: str,
uid: str = "UID9622",
creator: str = "Zhuge Xin",
gpg_fingerprint: str = "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",
parent_dna: Optional[str] = None,
privacy_mode: PrivacyMode = "normal",
retention: RetentionMode = "full",
trace_mode: TraceMode = "chain",
confirm: str = "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",
seal: str = "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL",
now: Optional[datetime] = None

) -> DynamicDNARecord:

"""
Generate a dynamic provenance identifier for an artifact.
This function creates a human-readable DNA string and a structured
verification record. It does not replace GPG signing; rather, it
prepares a canonical record that may be signed or appended to a ledger.
"""
if now is None:
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone()
iso_time = now.isoformat(timespec="seconds")
date = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
shichen = calc_shichen(now)
module_norm = normalize_module(module)
action_norm = normalize_module(action)
artifact_hash_full = sha256_text(artifact)
hash8 = artifact_hash_full[:8].upper()
# Seed includes human identity, time, module, action, artifact digest, and parent DNA.
seed = "|".join([
    uid,
    creator,
    gpg_fingerprint,
    iso_time,
    shichen,
    module_norm,
    action_norm,
    artifact_hash_full,
    parent_dna or "ROOT"
])
dr = digit_root(seed)
wuxing = wuxing_from_dr(dr)
audit_color = audit_color_from_dr(dr)
gpg_prefix = gpg_fingerprint[:8].upper()
dna = f"#龍芯⚡️{date}-{shichen}-{wuxing}-{module_norm}-{gpg_prefix}-{hash8}"
return DynamicDNARecord(
    dna=dna,
    uid=uid,
    creator=creator,
    gpg_fingerprint=gpg_fingerprint,
    gpg_prefix=gpg_prefix,
    iso_time=iso_time,
    date=date,
    shichen=shichen,
    digit_root=dr,
    wuxing=wuxing,
    module=module_norm,
    action=action_norm,
    artifact_hash=f"sha256:{artifact_hash_full}",
    hash8=hash8,
    privacy_mode=privacy_mode,
    retention=retention,
    trace_mode=trace_mode,
    audit_color=audit_color,
    parent_dna=parent_dna,
    confirm=confirm,
    seal=seal
)

def record_to_json(record: DynamicDNARecord) -> str:

return json.dumps(asdict(record), ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)

⸻

3.4.7 Verification Algorithm

Generating DNA is only half of the mechanism. A verifier must be able to parse and validate the record.

Algorithm 3: VerifyDynamicDNA

def verify_dynamic_dna(

artifact: str,
record: DynamicDNARecord,
claimed_uid: str,
claimed_gpg_prefix: str,

) -> tuple[float, list[str]]:

"""
Verify that a Dynamic DNA record is consistent with the artifact and claimed identity.
Returns a score and evidence log.
"""
evidence: list[str] = []
# 1. Prefix and UID check
if not record.dna.startswith("#龍芯⚡️"):
    return 0.0, ["DNA prefix mismatch"]
if record.uid != claimed_uid:
    return 0.0, ["UID mismatch"]
evidence.append("DNA prefix and UID verified")
# 2. GPG prefix check
if record.gpg_prefix != claimed_gpg_prefix[:8].upper():
    return 0.0, ["GPG prefix mismatch"]
if record.gpg_prefix not in record.dna:
    return 0.0, ["GPG prefix missing from DNA string"]
evidence.append("GPG prefix verified")
# 3. Artifact hash check
expected_hash = sha256_text(artifact)
if record.artifact_hash != f"sha256:{expected_hash}":
    return 0.0, ["Artifact hash mismatch"]
if record.hash8 != expected_hash[:8].upper():
    return 0.0, ["Hash8 mismatch"]
evidence.append("Artifact hash verified")
# 4. Date, shichen, digit root consistency
try:
    parsed_time = datetime.fromisoformat(record.iso_time)
except ValueError:
    return 0.0, ["Invalid ISO timestamp"]
if record.date != parsed_time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d"):
    return 0.0, ["Date mismatch"]
expected_shichen = calc_shichen(parsed_time)
if record.shichen != expected_shichen:
    return 0.0, ["Shichen mismatch"]
evidence.append("Temporal markers verified")
# 5. Wuxing and audit route check
if record.wuxing != wuxing_from_dr(record.digit_root):
    return 0.0, ["Wuxing mapping mismatch"]
if record.audit_color != audit_color_from_dr(record.digit_root):
    return 0.0, ["Audit color mismatch"]
evidence.append("Digit root, wuxing, and audit route verified")
# 6. Privacy consistency check
if record.privacy_mode == "sealed" and record.retention != "hash_only":
    return 0.0, ["Sealed content must use hash_only retention"]
if record.privacy_mode == "burn" and record.retention == "full":
    return 0.0, ["Burn content must not use full retention"]
if record.trace_mode == "no_external" and record.retention == "full":
    evidence.append("Warning: no_external content should avoid full retention in external contexts")
evidence.append("Privacy and retention consistency checked")
return 1.0, evidence

⸻

3.4.8 Append-Only Ledger Integration

Dynamic DNA becomes stronger when written into an append-only ledger.

Ledger Entry Format

{

"event": "DYNAMIC_DNA_GENERATED",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-THREAT-MODEL-v1.0",

"artifact_hash": "sha256:7fa3c91b84d5...",

"module": "BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY",

"action": "CREATE_SECTION",

"ts": "2026-05-04T12:30:00+08:00",

"operator": "UID9622",

"audit_color": "🟢",

"previous_log_hash": "sha256:...",

"current_log_hash": "sha256:..."

}

Hash-Chain Update

def hash_ledger_entry(entry: dict, previous_hash: str) -> str:

canonical = json.dumps(
    {**entry, "previous_log_hash": previous_hash},
    ensure_ascii=False,
    sort_keys=True
)
return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

This allows later verification:

ledger_verification:

  • check_entry_exists
  • check_previous_hash_matches
  • recompute_current_hash
  • confirm_parent_child_link
  • confirm_timestamp_order

⸻

3.4.9 Privacy Modes in DNA Generation

The DNA Engine must not leak private content.

Normal Mode

Used for public or internal non-sensitive material.

normal_mode:

read_raw: true

save_raw: depends_on_retention

save_hash: true

allow_summary: true

dna_includes: ["date", "module", "action", "hash8"]

Burn Mode

Used for temporary sensitive material.

burn_mode:

read_raw: temporary

save_raw: false

save_summary: false

save_hash: true

require_destroy_proof: true

dna_includes: ["date", "module", "action", "hash8"]

Sealed Mode

Used for secrets, keys, private tokens, confidential materials, or suspected classified content.

sealed_mode:

read_raw: false

save_raw: false

save_summary: false

save_hash: true

require_uid_confirm: true

dna_includes: ["date", "sealed", "hash8"]

Critical rule:

Dynamic DNA must never become a reason to expose the content it is supposed to protect.

⸻

3.4.10 Dynamic DNA and the Seven-Factor Framework

Dynamic DNA directly supports multiple verification factors.

Factor Supported By Dynamic DNA

F1 Identity DNA UID, GPG prefix, seal, confirm

F2 Temporal Anchor ISO time, date, shichen, digit root

F3 Rule Trace module, action, parent DNA, ledger entry

F4 Persona Route can include persona route DNA in parent chain

F5 Protected Lexicon DNA can bind protected lexicon version

F6 Style Vector can reference baseline corpus hash

F7 Mistake Ledger can reference mistake ledger snapshot hash

Dynamic DNA is therefore not a replacement for all seven factors. It is the root record that helps connect them.

Academic phrasing:

The Dynamic DNA Engine acts as a provenance coordination layer rather than a complete authentication system by itself.

UID9622 说法：

DNA 是门牌号，不是整栋楼；但没门牌，后面七层证据就不好找。

⸻

3.4.11 Security Analysis

Property 1: Artifact Binding

If the artifact changes, its SHA-256 digest changes. Therefore, the DNA record no longer verifies.

artifact ≠ artifact' => hash(artifact) ≠ hash(artifact')

Within standard cryptographic assumptions, collision probability is negligible.

Property 2: Identity Binding

The DNA string includes the UID and GPG prefix. The canonical record includes the full GPG fingerprint.

This does not prove signature validity by itself. It provides a structured identity claim that must be checked against a signature or trusted public record.

Property 3: Temporal Consistency

The DNA record includes ISO time, date, shichen, and digit root. A forged record must keep these fields mutually consistent.

This does not prevent a local attacker from creating a false timestamp unless the ledger is externally anchored. It does, however, increase inconsistency detection when combined with append-only logs.

Property 4: Lineage Continuity

Parent DNA links create a chain:

DNA_0 -> DNA_1 -> DNA_2 -> ... -> DNA_n

If an attacker removes or changes an intermediate record, the chain becomes inconsistent unless they can rewrite all subsequent anchored entries.

Property 5: Privacy Preservation

Only hashes and metadata need to be stored for sensitive content. The engine supports sealed and burn modes to avoid storing raw private text.

⸻

3.4.12 Limitations

The Dynamic DNA Engine has explicit limitations.

limitations:

  • DNA strings alone do not replace digital signatures.
  • Local timestamps are not trusted unless anchored.
  • GPG prefix alone is not full GPG verification.
  • Cultural markers are not cryptographic primitives.
  • Hashes prove content integrity, not legal ownership.
  • If raw artifacts are unavailable, only hash consistency can be checked.
  • If private keys are compromised, identity claims must be revoked and rotated.

论文里必须写这段。它能挡住 reviewer 说你夸大。

⸻

3.4.13 Implementation Note: Minimal Local Deployment

A minimal local deployment requires only:

minimal_deployment:

files:

- dynamic_dna_engine.py
- audit_log.jsonl
- dna_registry.json

optional:

- gpg_signature.asc
- public_checkpoint.txt
- protected_lexicon.json
- persona_registry.json

3.5 Evidence Ledger and Lineage Chain

证据账本与血统链

章节: 3.5 Evidence Ledger and Lineage Chain

中文名: 证据账本与血统链

论文归属: Behavioral Cryptography 主论文

父级章节:

  • 3.2 Seven-Factor Verification Mechanism
  • 3.3 Threat Model and Adversarial Capabilities
  • 3.4 The Dynamic DNA Engine

本节DNA: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-EVIDENCE-LEDGER-v1.0"

CONFIRM: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

SEAL: "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

GPG: "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

⸻

A｜当前定盘

3.4 解决的是：每个内容 / 动作 / 模块如何生成 Dynamic DNA。

3.5 要解决的是：这些 DNA 如何连起来，形成不可随便篡改的证据账本。

一句话定盘：

Dynamic DNA 是单个节点，Evidence Ledger 是节点之间的血脉；没有账本，DNA 只是标签；有了账本，DNA 才能形成可验证的 lineage chain。

中文压缩：

DNA 负责“这是谁、什么时候、干了什么”；账本负责“前后怎么连、有没有断、有没有被改”。

⸻

3.5 Evidence Ledger and Lineage Chain

3.5.1 Motivation

The Dynamic DNA Engine introduced in Section 3.4 creates a compact provenance root for individual artifacts or actions. However, provenance does not emerge from isolated identifiers. It emerges from continuity.

A single DNA record can show that one artifact was generated under a specific identity, time, module, and action. But content creation in human-AI collaboration is rarely a single-step process. It usually involves multiple revisions, model interactions, rule applications, persona routing decisions, corrections, mistakes, and re-exports.

Therefore, Behavioral Cryptography requires an append-only evidence ledger that records each significant action and links it to previous actions.

The central principle is:

A single DNA proves a point.

A ledger proves a path.

A lineage chain proves continuity.

In Chinese:

一个 DNA 是点；证据账本是线；血统链是路。

⸻

3.5.2 Ledger Design Goals

The Evidence Ledger is designed around eight goals.

G1. Append-Only Recording

The ledger must not silently overwrite past records.

Every correction, update, deletion request, export, or re-signing action should produce a new entry.

append_only_principle:

allowed:

- append_new_entry
- mark_previous_entry_superseded
- add_correction_record
- add_revocation_record

forbidden:

- silent_overwrite
- delete_without_tombstone
- rewrite_history_without_notice

G2. Parent-Child Lineage

Every derived artifact should preserve a parent DNA reference.

lineage_rule:

root_artifact:

parent_dna: null

derived_artifact:

parent_dna: required

exported_artifact:

parent_dna: required

summarized_artifact:

parent_dna: required

corrected_artifact:

parent_dna: previous_version_dna

G3. Hash-Linked Integrity

Each ledger entry includes the hash of the previous ledger entry. This creates a local hash chain.

h_0 = SHA256(entry_0)

h_1 = SHA256(h_0 || entry_1)

h_2 = SHA256(h_1 || entry_2)

...

h_n = SHA256(h_{n-1} || entry_n)

If any entry is changed, all subsequent hashes become invalid.

G4. Human-Auditable Evidence

The ledger must be inspectable by a non-enterprise user.

This matters because Behavioral Cryptography is designed for civilian-grade provenance, not only institutional compliance.

human_auditability:

format: jsonl

readable_fields:

- event
- dna
- parent_dna
- action
- module
- timestamp
- audit_color
- reason

G5. Privacy-Aware Storage

The ledger must not become a privacy leak.

For sealed or burn content, the ledger should record only metadata and hash.

privacy_ledger_policy:

normal:

may_store_summary: true
may_store_artifact_hash: true

burn:

may_store_raw_content: false
may_store_summary: false
must_store_destroy_proof: true

sealed:

may_store_raw_content: false
may_store_summary: false
must_store_hash_only: true

G6. Multi-Factor Linkage

The ledger must connect to the seven-factor framework.

ledger_links:

F1_identity_dna: dna / uid / gpg_prefix

F2_temporal_anchor: iso_time / shichen / digit_root

F3_rule_trace: rule_id / rule_dna / rule_version

F4_persona_route: persona_id / persona_dna

F5_protected_lexicon: lexicon_version_hash

F6_style_vector: style_baseline_hash

F7_mistake_ledger: mistake_entry_id / correction_dna

G7. Revocation and Correction

A provenance system must allow correction without erasing history.

correction_policy:

wrong_entry:

do_not_delete: true
append_correction: true
link_to_original: true

revoked_entry:

append_revocation_record: true
preserve_original_hash: true

G8. Exportable Proof Bundle

The ledger should support export as a proof bundle.

proof_bundle:

includes:

- artifact
- dynamic_dna_record
- relevant_ledger_entries
- public_key_or_gpg_fingerprint
- hash_chain_proof
- optional_signature

⸻

3.5.3 Ledger Entry Model

Each ledger entry is a structured record.

Canonical Ledger Entry

{

"ledger_version": "v1.0",

"entry_id": "LEDGER-9622-20260504-000001",

"event": "ARTIFACT_CREATED",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DYNAMIC-DNA-ENGINE-v1.0",

"child_dna": null,

"uid": "UID9622",

"module": "BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY",

"action": "CREATE_SECTION",

"artifact_hash": "sha256:7fa3c91b84d5...",

"privacy_mode": "normal",

"retention": "full",

"trace_mode": "chain",

"audit_color": "🟢",

"reason": "Section 3.5 evidence ledger entry generated.",

"iso_time": "2026-05-04T12:40:00+08:00",

"previous_entry_hash": "sha256:...",

"current_entry_hash": "sha256:...",

"confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",

"seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

}

⸻

3.5.4 Ledger Event Types

The ledger should distinguish event types clearly.

ledger_events:

creation:

- ARTIFACT_CREATED
- DNA_GENERATED
- RULE_APPLIED
- PERSONA_DISPATCHED

revision:

- ARTIFACT_REVISED
- SUMMARY_CREATED
- TRANSLATION_CREATED
- FORMAT_CONVERTED

audit:

- AUDIT_PASSED
- AUDIT_PENDING
- AUDIT_FAILED
- HARD_FAILURE_TRIGGERED

privacy:

- BURN_PROCESSED
- DESTROY_PROOF_CREATED
- SEALED_HASH_ONLY
- NO_EXTERNAL_BLOCKED

correction:

- MISTAKE_RECORDED
- CORRECTION_APPENDED
- ENTRY_REVOKED

export:

- EXPORT_REQUESTED
- EXPORT_APPROVED
- EXPORT_BLOCKED
- PROOF_BUNDLE_CREATED

⸻

3.5.5 Lineage Chain Definition

Definition 3.5 — Evidence Ledger

An evidence ledger L is an ordered sequence of entries:

L = langle e_1, e_2, ..., e_n rangle

where each entry e_i contains:

e_i = langle dna_i, parent_i, action_i, time_i, hash_i, prevhash_i, audit_i rangle

Definition 3.6 — Lineage Chain

A lineage chain for artifact C_n is the ordered path:

Chain(C_n) = langle dna_0 rightarrow dna_1 rightarrow ... rightarrow dna_n rangle

where each dna_i is either the parent of dna_{i+1}, or is referenced through a valid ledger entry.

Definition 3.7 — Chain Continuity

A chain is continuous if:

  1. Every non-root node has a valid parent_dna.
  2. Every parent_dna exists in the ledger or in an accepted external checkpoint.
  3. Timestamps are monotonic or explainably branched.
  4. The ledger hash chain verifies.
  5. No sealed or no_external record is exported without authorization.

⸻

3.5.6 Algorithm 4: Append Ledger Entry

from future import annotations

import hashlib

import json

from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict

from datetime import datetime, timezone

from pathlib import Path

from typing import Optional, Literal

AuditColor = Literal["🟢", "🟡", "🔴"]

PrivacyMode = Literal["normal", "burn", "sealed"]

RetentionMode = Literal["full", "summary_only", "hash_only"]

TraceMode = Literal["chain", "local_only", "no_external"]

@dataclass

class LedgerEntry:

ledger_version: str
entry_id: str
event: str
dna: str
parent_dna: Optional[str]
child_dna: Optional[str]
uid: str
module: str
action: str
artifact_hash: str
privacy_mode: PrivacyMode
retention: RetentionMode
trace_mode: TraceMode
audit_color: AuditColor
reason: str
iso_time: str
previous_entry_hash: str
current_entry_hash: str
confirm: str
seal: str

def now_iso() -> str:

return datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

def canonical_json(data: dict) -> str:

return json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))

def sha256_string(text: str) -> str:

return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

def load_last_hash(ledger_path: Path) -> str:

if not ledger_path.exists():
    return "sha256:GENESIS"
last_line = ""
with ledger_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for line in f:
        if line.strip():
            last_line = line.strip()
if not last_line:
    return "sha256:GENESIS"
try:
    last_entry = json.loads(last_line)
    return last_entry.get("current_entry_hash", "sha256:GENESIS")
except Exception:
    return "sha256:CORRUPTED"

def compute_entry_hash(entry_without_current_hash: dict) -> str:

canonical = canonical_json(entry_without_current_hash)
return sha256_string(canonical)

def append_ledger_entry(

ledger_path: Path,
event: str,
dna: str,
parent_dna: Optional[str],
uid: str,
module: str,
action: str,
artifact_hash: str,
privacy_mode: PrivacyMode,
retention: RetentionMode,
trace_mode: TraceMode,
audit_color: AuditColor,
reason: str,
child_dna: Optional[str] = None,
confirm: str = "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",
seal: str = "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL",

) -> LedgerEntry:

ledger_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
previous_hash = load_last_hash(ledger_path)
ts = now_iso()
# Basic privacy safety checks.
if privacy_mode == "sealed" and retention != "hash_only":
    raise ValueError("sealed content must use hash_only retention")
if privacy_mode == "burn" and retention == "full":
    raise ValueError("burn content must not use full retention")
if trace_mode == "no_external" and event.startswith("EXPORT"):
    raise ValueError("no_external content cannot be exported")
entry_id_seed = f"{ts}|{dna}|{event}|{previous_hash}"
entry_id = "LEDGER-9622-" + hashlib.sha256(entry_id_seed.encode()).hexdigest()[:12].upper()
base = {
    "ledger_version": "v1.0",
    "entry_id": entry_id,
    "event": event,
    "dna": dna,
    "parent_dna": parent_dna,
    "child_dna": child_dna,
    "uid": uid,
    "module": module,
    "action": action,
    "artifact_hash": artifact_hash,
    "privacy_mode": privacy_mode,
    "retention": retention,
    "trace_mode": trace_mode,
    "audit_color": audit_color,
    "reason": reason,
    "iso_time": ts,
    "previous_entry_hash": previous_hash,
    "confirm": confirm,
    "seal": seal
}
current_hash = compute_entry_hash(base)
base["current_entry_hash"] = current_hash
entry = LedgerEntry(**base)
with ledger_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    f.write(json.dumps(asdict(entry), ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
return entry

⸻

3.5.7 Algorithm 5: Verify Ledger Integrity

def verify_ledger_integrity(ledger_path: Path) -> tuple[bool, list[str]]:

evidence = []
if not ledger_path.exists():
    return False, ["Ledger file does not exist"]
previous_hash = "sha256:GENESIS"
line_no = 0
with ledger_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for line in f:
        line_no += 1
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        try:
            entry = json.loads(line)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            return False, [f"Line {line_no}: invalid JSON"]
        recorded_previous = entry.get("previous_entry_hash")
        recorded_current = entry.get("current_entry_hash")
        if recorded_previous != previous_hash:
            return False, [
                f"Line {line_no}: previous hash mismatch",
                f"expected={previous_hash}",
                f"actual={recorded_previous}"
            ]
        entry_without_current = dict(entry)
        entry_without_current.pop("current_entry_hash", None)
        recomputed = compute_entry_hash(entry_without_current)
        if recomputed != recorded_current:
            return False, [
                f"Line {line_no}: current hash mismatch",
                f"expected={recomputed}",
                f"actual={recorded_current}"
            ]
        previous_hash = recorded_current
        evidence.append(f"Line {line_no}: verified {entry.get('entry_id')}")
return True, evidence

⸻

3.5.8 Algorithm 6: Verify Lineage Chain

def build_dna_index(ledger_path: Path) -> dict[str, dict]:

index = {}
if not ledger_path.exists():
    return index
with ledger_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for line in f:
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        entry = json.loads(line)
        dna = entry.get("dna")
        if dna:
            index[dna] = entry
return index

def verify_lineage_chain(

ledger_path: Path,
target_dna: str,
allow_external_roots: bool = True

) -> tuple[bool, list[str]]:

index = build_dna_index(ledger_path)
evidence = []
if target_dna not in index:
    return False, [f"Target DNA not found: {target_dna}"]
seen = set()
current = target_dna
while current:
    if current in seen:
        return False, [f"Cycle detected at {current}"]
    seen.add(current)
    entry = index.get(current)
    if not entry:
        if allow_external_roots:
            evidence.append(f"External root accepted: {current}")
            break
        return False, [f"Missing parent DNA: {current}"]
    evidence.append(f"DNA found: {current}")
    # Privacy rule check
    if entry.get("privacy_mode") == "sealed" and entry.get("retention") != "hash_only":
        return False, [f"Sealed record violates hash_only retention: {current}"]
    if entry.get("trace_mode") == "no_external" and entry.get("event", "").startswith("EXPORT"):
        return False, [f"no_external record exported illegally: {current}"]
    parent = entry.get("parent_dna")
    if not parent:
        evidence.append("Root reached")
        break
    current = parent
return True, evidence

⸻

3.5.9 Correction, Revocation, and Mistake Ledger Integration

Behavioral Cryptography does not assume that creators never make mistakes. Instead, it treats honest correction as evidence.

This is where Mistake Ledger becomes important.

Correction Entry

{

"event": "CORRECTION_APPENDED",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-酉时-金-CORRECTION-A2D0092C-89AB12CD",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"mistake_id": "MISTAKE-9622-20260504-001",

"reason": "Corrected an overclaim about cryptographic unforgeability.",

"correction_type": "CLAIM_DOWNGRADE",

"audit_color": "🟢",

"retention": "summary_only"

}

Revocation Entry

{

"event": "ENTRY_REVOKED",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-戌时-金-REVOCATION-A2D0092C-EF9012AB",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"revoked_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-午时-金-BEHAVIORAL-VERIFY-A2D0092C-7FA3C91B",

"reason": "Previous artifact was superseded by corrected version.",

"audit_color": "🟡",

"retention": "summary_only"

}

Why Mistakes Strengthen Provenance

Attackers often copy polished outputs. They rarely reproduce the full history of false starts, corrections, boundary clarifications, and mistakes.

Therefore:

A continuous mistake ledger is a behavioral authenticity signal.

中文压缩：

真作者会留下修正痕迹；洗稿者只想留下完美成品。

⸻

3.5.10 Branching and Merge Semantics

Human-AI collaboration often branches.

Examples:

branch_examples:

  • Claude produces formalization
  • ChatGPT produces compression
  • Cursor implements code
  • Notion stores structured record
  • GitHub exports public version

The lineage model must support branching.

DNA_ROOT

├── DNA_CLAUDE_DRAFT

├── DNA_CHATGPT_COMPRESSION

├── DNA_CURSOR_IMPLEMENTATION

└── DNA_PUBLIC_EXPORT

Merge Entry

{

"event": "LINEAGE_MERGED",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-未时-土-LINEAGE-MERGE-A2D0092C-3C4D5E6F",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DYNAMIC-DNA-ENGINE-v1.0",

"merged_parents": [

"#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-CLAUDE-DRAFT-v1.0",
"#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-CHATGPT-COMPRESSION-v1.0",
"#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-CURSOR-IMPLEMENTATION-v1.0"

],

"action": "MERGE_BRANCHES",

"reason": "Merged multi-AI outputs into UID9622 canonical version.",

"audit_color": "🟢"

}

Hard rule:

Merge does not erase parents. Merge records them.

⸻

3.5.11 Security Properties

Theorem 3.10 — Ledger Tamper Evidence

If any ledger entry e_i is modified after creation, then the hash verification for e_i or some later entry e_j fails.

Proof Sketch.

Each entry stores previous_entry_hash and current_entry_hash. The current hash is computed over the canonical entry content. Modifying an entry changes its computed hash. Since the next entry stores the previous hash, the mismatch propagates. ∎

Theorem 3.11 — Lineage Continuity

If every derived artifact contains a valid parent_dna, and every parent exists in the ledger or external checkpoint, then the artifact has a continuous lineage chain to a known root.

Proof Sketch.

Starting from the target DNA, repeatedly follow parent_dna. If each parent exists and no cycle appears, the chain terminates at a root or accepted checkpoint. ∎

Theorem 3.12 — Correction Preservation

Appending correction records preserves accountability better than overwriting prior records.

Proof Sketch.

Overwriting removes evidence of the previous state. Appending creates a new record that references the earlier record. The prior claim, the correction, and the reason remain available for audit. ∎

Theorem 3.13 — No Silent Laundering

If a content artifact is paraphrased or exported without a valid parent DNA, the ledger verification fails to establish continuous lineage.

Proof Sketch.

The exported artifact may have its own hash and DNA, but without a valid parent_dna chain, it cannot prove descent from the original source. ∎

⸻

3.5.12 Privacy and Boundary Enforcement

The ledger enforces privacy rules.

Boundary Table

Mode Raw Content Summary Hash Export

normal Allowed by retention Allowed Required Allowed if trace permits

burn Temporary only No Required Usually blocked

sealed Never No Required Blocked

no_external Local only Local only Local only Blocked

One-Line Rule

The ledger records proof of handling; it does not justify storing what should not be stored.

中文：

账本只证明处理过，不代表可以把敏感原文留下来。

⸻

3.5.13 Reviewer Defense Paragraph

This section should include a clear academic defense to prevent overclaiming.

Recommended paper text:

The proposed ledger is not claimed to be equivalent to a public blockchain or a trusted timestamping authority. In its minimal deployment, it provides local tamper-evident continuity rather than global immutability. Stronger guarantees can be obtained by periodically anchoring ledger heads to external systems such as Git commits, public archives, timestamping services, or institutional repositories. This layered design is intentional: it allows civilian users to obtain meaningful provenance protection without requiring enterprise infrastructure.

中文意思：

本地账本不是区块链，不吹全球不可改；它先做到本地防篡改、有痕迹、能验证。要更强，就定期把账本头 hash 发到 Git / 公开仓库 / 时间戳服务做外锚。
