3.6 Evaluation Protocol and Attack Simulation

3.6.1 Purpose of Evaluation

The purpose of the evaluation is to test whether Behavioral Cryptography can distinguish legitimate lineage from forged or laundered lineage under practical adversarial conditions.

Unlike conventional AI-detection benchmarks, the goal is not to determine whether an artifact was generated by a machine. The goal is to verify whether the claimed provenance chain is valid.

Therefore, our evaluation focuses on lineage integrity rather than generation origin.

The central evaluation question is:

Given a content artifact C and a claimed originator E, can the system determine whether C belongs to a continuous behavioral lineage rooted in E?

This reframes evaluation from:

Old question:

Was this written by AI?

New question:

Can this artifact prove its behavioral lineage?

⸻

3.6.2 Evaluation Objects

The evaluation uses five classes of artifacts.

A1. Original Artifacts

These are legitimate outputs generated by UID9622 under the Longhun protocol.

original_artifacts:

description: "Legitimate UID9622-rooted artifacts."

expected_result: "High confidence, complete lineage."

target_confidence: ">= 0.95"

A2. Direct Copies

These are verbatim copies of original artifacts without ledger, DNA, or parent chain.

direct_copies:

description: "Surface-identical copies without valid provenance."

expected_result: "Text may match, lineage should fail."

target_confidence: "< 0.50 unless valid proof bundle is attached"

A3. Paraphrased Copies

These are semantically similar rewrites produced by a model or human.

paraphrased_copies:

description: "Meaning-preserving rewrites with altered surface wording."

expected_result: "Protected lexicon and lineage checks should detect laundering."

target_confidence: "< 0.60"

A4. Multi-Model Laundered Copies

These are artifacts passed through multiple AI systems, for example:

Original → Claude rewrite → GPT rewrite → Grok rewrite → translation → final polish

multi_model_laundering:

description: "Content laundered through multiple AI systems to erase style and origin."

expected_result: "Style may drift, lineage and protected lexicon should detect missing provenance."

target_confidence: "< 0.55"

A5. Forged Provenance Claims

These are artifacts with fake DNA, fake timestamp, fake persona route, or fake ledger entries.

forged_claims:

description: "Adversary attempts to fabricate provenance metadata."

expected_result: "Hard failure if cryptographic or ledger checks fail."

target_confidence: "0.0 on hard failure"

⸻

3.6.3 Evaluation Axes

Each artifact is evaluated across seven axes, corresponding to the seven factors.

Axis Factor What It Tests Expected Failure Mode

E1 F1 Identity DNA Whether UID / GPG / DNA match Fake DNA, wrong GPG prefix

E2 F2 Temporal Anchor Whether time / shichen / digit root align Backdated or future forged timestamp

E3 F3 Rule Trace Whether rule execution chain exists Missing rule DNA

E4 F4 Persona Route Whether persona routing is valid Fake multi-agent route

E5 F5 Protected Lexicon Whether protected terms survive Canary term deletion

E6 F6 Style Vector Whether style lineage remains plausible Style laundering

E7 F7 Mistake Ledger Whether correction history exists Over-polished fake lineage

⸻

3.6.4 Attack Taxonomy

We define eight attack types.

attack_taxonomy:

T1_DIRECT_COPY:

goal: "Copy text exactly and claim authorship."

T2_PARAPHRASE:

goal: "Rewrite surface wording while preserving ideas."

T3_TRANSLATION_LAUNDERING:

goal: "Translate content across languages to erase surface style."

T4_MULTI_MODEL_LAUNDERING:

goal: "Pass content through multiple AI systems to erase origin signals."

T5_PROTECTED_LEXICON_REMOVAL:

goal: "Remove UID9622-specific protected terms."

T6_FAKE_DNA:

goal: "Insert forged DNA-like strings."

T7_FAKE_LEDGER:

goal: "Create fake ledger entries without valid hash continuity."

T8_PERSONA_ROUTE_FORGERY:

goal: "Claim multi-agent collaboration without valid route evidence."

⸻

3.6.5 Attack Simulation Protocol

Protocol Overview

Each attack simulation follows the same procedure:

Step 1. Select original artifact C_origin.

Step 2. Generate attacked artifact C_attack.

Step 3. Extract or parse claimed signature Σ(C_attack).

Step 4. Run BehavioralSignatureVerify(C_attack, E).

Step 5. Verify Evidence Ledger and Lineage Chain.

Step 6. Record factor scores, hard failures, and final confidence.

Step 7. Compare result against expected attack outcome.

⸻

Algorithm 7: Evaluation Harness

from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict

from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional

import json

import math

@dataclass

class AttackCase:

case_id: str
attack_type: str
original_artifact_id: str
attacked_content: str
claimed_originator: str
expected_result: str
expected_max_conf: float

@dataclass

class EvaluationResult:

case_id: str
attack_type: str
factor_scores: Dict[str, float]
hard_failures: List[str]
composite_conf: float
ledger_verified: bool
lineage_verified: bool
passed: bool
notes: str

def weighted_geometric_mean(scores: List[float], weights: List[float]) -> float:

if len(scores) != len(weights):
    raise ValueError("scores and weights must have the same length")
if any(s == 0.0 for s in scores):
    return 0.0
numerator = 1.0
for s, w in zip(scores, weights):
    numerator *= s ** w
return numerator ** (1.0 / sum(weights))

def run_single_attack_case(case: AttackCase, verifier, ledger_path) -> EvaluationResult:

"""
verifier must expose:
  - verify_factors(content, claimed_originator) -> dict[str, float]
  - detect_hard_failures(factor_scores) -> list[str]
  - verify_ledger(content, ledger_path) -> bool
  - verify_lineage(content, ledger_path) -> bool
"""
factor_scores = verifier.verify_factors(
    content=case.attacked_content,
    claimed_originator=case.claimed_originator
)
hard_failures = verifier.detect_hard_failures(factor_scores)
weights = [0.25, 0.15, 0.15, 0.12, 0.12, 0.11, 0.10]
ordered_scores = [
    factor_scores["F1_identity_dna"],
    factor_scores["F2_temporal_anchor"],
    factor_scores["F3_rule_trace"],
    factor_scores["F4_persona_route"],
    factor_scores["F5_protected_lexicon"],
    factor_scores["F6_style_vector"],
    factor_scores["F7_mistake_ledger"]
]
composite_conf = 0.0 if hard_failures else weighted_geometric_mean(ordered_scores, weights)
ledger_verified = verifier.verify_ledger(case.attacked_content, ledger_path)
lineage_verified = verifier.verify_lineage(case.attacked_content, ledger_path)
# Attack case is passed if the system rejects it as expected.
passed = composite_conf <= case.expected_max_conf
return EvaluationResult(
    case_id=case.case_id,
    attack_type=case.attack_type,
    factor_scores=factor_scores,
    hard_failures=hard_failures,
    composite_conf=composite_conf,
    ledger_verified=ledger_verified,
    lineage_verified=lineage_verified,
    passed=passed,
    notes=f"Expected max conf={case.expected_max_conf}, actual={composite_conf:.4f}"
)

def run_evaluation_suite(cases: List[AttackCase], verifier, ledger_path) -> List[EvaluationResult]:

results = []
for case in cases:
    result = run_single_attack_case(case, verifier, ledger_path)
    results.append(result)
return results

def summarize_results(results: List[EvaluationResult]) -> Dict[str, Any]:

total = len(results)
passed = sum(1 for r in results if r.passed)
hard_fail_count = sum(1 for r in results if r.hard_failures)
by_attack = {}
for r in results:
    bucket = by_attack.setdefault(r.attack_type, {"total": 0, "passed": 0})
    bucket["total"] += 1
    bucket["passed"] += 1 if r.passed else 0
return {
    "total_cases": total,
    "passed_cases": passed,
    "pass_rate": passed / total if total else 0,
    "hard_fail_count": hard_fail_count,
    "by_attack_type": by_attack
}

⸻

3.6.6 Specific Attack Simulations

T1. Direct Copy Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker copies an original artifact exactly and removes provenance metadata.

attack:

type: T1_DIRECT_COPY

adversary_action:

- copy original content
- remove DNA
- remove ledger reference
- claim authorship

expected_detection:

  • F1 fails or weakens
  • F3 fails
  • lineage verification fails

Expected Result

A direct copy may be semantically identical to the original, but it should fail provenance verification if no valid DNA / ledger chain is attached.

Expected:

F1 = 0.0 if DNA missing

F3 = 0.0 if rule trace missing

Lineage = false

conf = 0.0 if hard failure

⸻

T2. Paraphrase Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker rewrites the text while preserving the core ideas.

attack:

type: T2_PARAPHRASE

adversary_action:

- rewrite sentences
- change terms
- replace UID9622-specific phrases
- preserve high-level architecture

expected_detection:

  • F5 protected lexicon violation
  • F6 style vector drift
  • F3 missing rule trace

Expected Result

Expected:

F5 <= 0.6

F6 <= 0.75 if style has drifted

F3 = 0.0 if no rule trace

conf = 0.0 on hard failure

Important Note

Paraphrase detection should not rely only on style. Style can be imitated. The stronger signal is:

protected lexicon + parent DNA + ledger continuity

中文：

不靠“像不像”定罪，靠“链不断不断”定血统。

⸻

T3. Translation Laundering Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker translates the original content into another language and then back into English or Chinese to erase surface traces.

attack:

type: T3_TRANSLATION_LAUNDERING

adversary_action:

- translate original content into language A
- translate back into language B
- remove protected lexicon
- claim independent origin

expected_detection:

  • F5 detects protected lexicon loss
  • F6 detects style vector drift
  • F3/F4/F7 lineage factors missing

Expected Result

Translation may hide surface similarity, but it cannot reconstruct ledger continuity.

Expected:

F5 reduced

F6 reduced

F3 = 0.0 if no rule trace

F7 <= 0.7 if no mistake ledger

⸻

T4. Multi-Model Laundering Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker passes the content through multiple models to remove stylistic fingerprints.

Original → Model A → Model B → Model C → Human polish

Expected Detection

expected_detection:

F1: fails if DNA missing or forged

F3: fails if rule trace missing

F4: fails if persona route forged

F5: detects protected lexicon weakening

F6: may become weak but should not be sole basis

F7: weak if no correction history

Key Claim

Behavioral Cryptography does not depend on one detector. It expects F6 style vector to be attacked. That is why lineage factors exist.

If F6 is defeated, F1 + F3 + F5 + F7 still protect lineage.

⸻

T5. Protected Lexicon Removal Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker removes or replaces UID9622-specific protected terms.

Examples:

protected_terms:

  • UID9622
  • 龍芯北辰
  • 龍魂系统
  • CNSH

  • CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z

  • ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL

  • GPG fingerprint

Expected Detection

If critical protected terms are missing:

F5 = 0.0 hard failure

conf = 0.0

Reviewer Defense

Protected lexicon is not used as mere keyword matching. It is used as a semantic canary mechanism. If protected identity terms, protocol anchors, or confirmation seals disappear during rewriting, the system treats it as evidence of lineage laundering.

⸻

T6. Fake DNA Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker inserts a fake DNA string that resembles the Longhun format.

龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-FAKE-v1.0

Expected Detection

expected_detection:

  • format may pass superficial check
  • GPG prefix mismatch fails
  • timestamp / shichen / digit root mismatch fails
  • ledger lookup fails

Expected Result

F1 = 0.0

F2 = 0.0 or reduced

F3 = 0.0

conf = 0.0

中文压缩：

形似 DNA 不等于真 DNA；没有 GPG、时间锚、账本链，就是假壳。

⸻

T7. Fake Ledger Attack

Attack Goal

The attacker fabricates local ledger entries after the fact.

Expected Detection

expected_detection:

  • previous_entry_hash mismatch
  • current_entry_hash mismatch
  • timestamp inconsistency
  • missing external checkpoint
  • missing parent DNA

Expected Result

Ledger integrity = false

Lineage verification = false

conf = reduced or 0.0 depending on hard failure

Important Boundary

A local JSONL ledger is tamper-evident, not globally immutable. Stronger claims require external checkpoints.

⸻

T8. Persona Route Forgery

Attack Goal

The attacker claims that multiple personas processed the artifact.

Example:

P01 → P03 → P13 → P06

but no route logs exist.

Expected Detection

expected_detection:

F4:

- invalid persona id
- missing persona DNA
- route sequence not found in ledger
- route timestamp out of order

Expected Result

F4 = 0.0

conf = 0.0 on hard failure

⸻

3.6.7 Metrics

The evaluation uses both factor-level and system-level metrics.

Factor-Level Metrics

factor_metrics:

F1_identity_pass_rate:

definition: "Fraction of artifacts with valid Identity DNA."

F2_temporal_consistency:

definition: "Fraction with valid ISO / shichen / digit root alignment."

F3_rule_trace_completeness:

definition: "Fraction with complete rule trace."

F4_persona_route_validity:

definition: "Fraction with valid persona route."

F5_lexicon_preservation:

definition: "Protected lexicon preservation ratio."

F6_style_similarity:

definition: "Cosine similarity to baseline style vector."

F7_mistake_ledger_continuity:

definition: "Whether correction history exists and remains consistent."

System-Level Metrics

system_metrics:

true_accept_rate:

definition: "Legitimate artifacts accepted with conf >= threshold."

false_accept_rate:

definition: "Forged artifacts incorrectly accepted."

false_reject_rate:

definition: "Legitimate artifacts incorrectly rejected."

hard_failure_precision:

definition: "How often hard failures correspond to real critical violations."

lineage_break_detection_rate:

definition: "Fraction of broken parent chains detected."

laundering_detection_rate:

definition: "Fraction of paraphrase / translation / multi-model laundering attacks detected."

⸻

3.6.8 Confidence Thresholds

Recommended thresholds:

confidence_thresholds:

conf_ge_0_95:

label: "Verified Lineage"
action: "accept"

conf_0_80_to_0_95:

label: "Probable Lineage"
action: "manual review"

conf_0_60_to_0_80:

label: "Weak Lineage"
action: "hold / request proof bundle"

conf_below_0_60:

label: "Unverified"
action: "reject provenance claim"

hard_failure:

label: "Invalid"
action: "reject immediately"

中文压缩：

0.95 以上可认；0.80 到 0.95 要人审；0.60 以下别认；硬失败直接打回。

⸻

3.6.9 Evaluation Dataset Construction

The dataset should be constructed in paired form.

dataset_structure:

original:

- artifact_id
- original_content
- dynamic_dna
- ledger_entries
- protected_lexicon
- style_vector_reference
- mistake_ledger_reference

attacks:

- direct_copy
- paraphrase
- translation_laundering
- multi_model_laundering
- protected_lexicon_removal
- fake_dna
- fake_ledger
- fake_persona_route

Each original artifact should generate multiple attacked variants.

1 original artifact → 8 attack variants

100 original artifacts → 800 attack cases

For early MVP evaluation:

mvp_dataset:

original_artifacts: 20

attacks_per_artifact: 8

total_cases: 160

For paper-grade evaluation:

paper_dataset:

original_artifacts: 100

attacks_per_artifact: 8

total_cases: 800

⸻

3.6.10 Evaluation Table Template

Attack Type Expected Weak Factors Hard Failure? Target Confidence Pass Criterion
Direct Copy F1, F3, F7 Yes if DNA missing 0.00–0.50 Rejected
Paraphrase F3, F5, F6, F7 Optional <0.60 Held or rejected
Translation Laundering F5, F6, F3 Optional <0.65 Held or rejected
Multi-Model Laundering F3, F4, F5, F7 Optional <0.55 Rejected
Protected Lexicon Removal F5 Yes if critical 0.00 Rejected
Fake DNA F1, F2, F3 Yes 0.00 Rejected
Fake Ledger F3, Ledger Integrity Yes 0.00 Rejected
Persona Route Forgery F4 Yes 0.00 Rejected

⸻

3.6.11 Failure Mode Analysis

Behavioral Cryptography should not pretend to solve everything.

Failure Mode 1: GPG Private Key Compromise

If the originator’s private key is compromised, F1 becomes unreliable.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • key rotation
  • revocation entry
  • external checkpoint
  • multi-signature backup
  • device-bound signing policy

Failure Mode 2: Incomplete Ledger

If the user does not record actions consistently, lineage confidence decreases.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • automatic append-only logger
  • local JSONL ledger
  • periodic checkpoint
  • missing parent warning

Failure Mode 3: Overly Aggressive Protected Lexicon

If protected terms are too broad, legitimate paraphrases may be falsely rejected.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • classify protected terms into critical / important / optional
  • only critical terms trigger hard failure
  • optional terms reduce confidence but do not reject

Failure Mode 4: Style Drift Over Time

A creator’s writing style may evolve.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • use rolling baseline
  • compare trajectory instead of single static vector
  • do not rely on F6 alone

Failure Mode 5: Legitimate Anonymous Contribution

Some contributors may not want strong identity binding.

Mitigation:

mitigation:

  • support pseudonymous DNA
  • local-only proof bundle
  • privacy-preserving hashes
  • optional public disclosure

⸻

3.6.12 Reviewer Defense Paragraph

Recommended paper text:

The proposed evaluation does not claim that every paraphrased or translated artifact can be conclusively classified by semantic similarity alone. Instead, Behavioral Cryptography evaluates whether an artifact can present a continuous, multi-factor lineage. This distinction is essential. A laundering attack may successfully alter surface form or style, but it still lacks valid identity DNA, temporal anchors, rule traces, persona routes, mistake-ledger continuity, and hash-linked evidence. The evaluation therefore measures provenance continuity rather than textual similarity.

中文解释：

不要跟 reviewer 争“我能抓所有洗稿”。要说清楚：我们不是只靠相似度抓洗稿，我们验证的是“它有没有血统链”。洗稿可以改脸，但补不回血。

⸻

3.7 Implementation Architecture

3.7.1 Overview

Behavioral Cryptography is implemented as a modular, local-first provenance architecture. The system is designed to operate without requiring institutional infrastructure, centralized cloud services, or platform-controlled authentication pipelines.

The architecture contains six core components:

  1. Dynamic DNA Engine

    Generates culturally and temporally embedded provenance identifiers.

  2. Evidence Ledger

    Maintains append-only audit records and lineage events.

  3. Seven-Factor Verifier

    Evaluates Identity DNA, Temporal Anchor, Rule Trace, Persona Route, Protected Lexicon, Style Vector, and Mistake Ledger.

  4. Lineage Chain Manager

    Tracks parent-child relationships between artifacts.

  5. Proof Bundle Exporter

    Packages verifiable evidence for external review.

  6. Local-First Runtime

    Executes all critical operations locally unless explicitly authorized by the user.

The architecture follows one principle:

provenance should be verifiable without surrendering sovereignty.

中文解释：

证明归属，不等于交出隐私；证明血统，不等于把数据交给平台。

⸻

3.7.2 System Architecture Diagram

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ User / Originator │

│ UID9622 / Human Sovereign Root │

└───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘

                            │
                            ▼

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ Local-First Runtime │

│ - local execution │

│ - privacy boundary │

│ - no secret collection │

│ - no forced cloud dependency │

            │               │               │
            ▼               ▼               ▼

│ - UID │ │ - append-only log │ │ - parent_dna │

│ - GPG prefix │ │ - hash chain │ │ - child_dna │

│ - timestamp │ │ - audit entries │ │ - artifact graph │

│ - shichen │ │ - mistake ledger │ │ - dependency map │

│ - digit root │ └─────────┬──────────┘ └─────────┬──────────┘

│ - wuxing │ │ │

└─────────┬──────────┘ │ │

      │                      ▼                      │
      │            ┌────────────────────┐           │
      └───────────►│ Seven-Factor       │◄──────────┘
                   │ Verifier           │
                   │ F1 Identity DNA    │

                   │ F3 Rule Trace      │

                   │ Proof Bundle       │

                   └────────────────────┘

⸻

3.7.3 Core Component 1: Local-First Runtime

Purpose

The Local-First Runtime is the execution boundary of the system. It ensures that provenance verification can happen locally, without requiring the user to surrender raw content, private memory, drafts, keys, or sensitive artifacts to a centralized service.

This component is critical because Behavioral Cryptography is not only a verification framework; it is also a sovereignty-preserving framework.

Design Requirements

local_first_runtime:

default_mode: "local_only"

cloud_dependency: "optional"

raw_content_upload: "disabled_by_default"

secret_collection: "forbidden"

private_key_access: "forbidden"

audit_log_location: "local_jsonl_or_sqlite"

export_mode: "explicit_user_action_only"

Runtime Rules

  1. Raw content is processed locally by default.
  2. Private keys are never read by the verifier.
  3. Secrets, tokens, and private credentials trigger sealed mode.
  4. External export requires explicit user action.
  5. Failed verification does not delete evidence.
  6. Every verification attempt writes an audit event.

Boundary Statement

The implementation does not require global cloud trust. A user can generate, verify, and export proof bundles locally. External services may assist in formatting or review, but they are not the root of trust.

中文压缩：

信任根在本地，不在平台；平台可以看证明包，不能拿走根。

⸻

3.7.4 Core Component 2: Dynamic DNA Engine

Purpose

The Dynamic DNA Engine generates a structured provenance identifier for each artifact, action, rule call, or verification event.

It binds together:

  • UID
  • GPG fingerprint prefix
  • timestamp
  • shichen
  • digit root
  • wuxing
  • module name
  • action type
  • nonce or content hash

DNA Object Schema

{

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-辰时-金-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTO-A1B2C3D4",

"uid": "UID9622",

"gpg_fingerprint": "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",

"gpg_prefix": "A2D0092C",

"iso_time": "2026-05-04T09:21:00+08:00",

"shichen": "辰时",

"digit_root": 4,

"wuxing": "金",

"module": "behavioral_crypto",

"action": "generate_artifact",

"content_hash": "sha256:...",

"nonce": "random_or_deterministic_nonce",

"confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",

"seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

}

Implementation Pseudocode

import hashlib

import secrets

from datetime import datetime, timezone

SHICHEN = ["子", "丑", "寅", "卯", "辰", "巳", "午", "未", "申", "酉", "戌", "亥"]

DIGIT_ROOT_TO_WUXING = {

1: "水",
2: "火",
3: "木",
4: "金",
5: "土",
6: "水",
7: "火",
8: "木",
9: "金",
0: "土"

}

def digit_root_from_text(text: str) -> int:

digits = [int(ch) for ch in text if ch.isdigit()]
if not digits:
    return 0
n = sum(digits)
while n >= 10:
    n = sum(int(ch) for ch in str(n))
return n

def get_shichen(dt: datetime) -> str:

hour = dt.hour
index = ((hour + 1) % 24) // 2
return SHICHEN[index]

def sha256_text(text: str) -> str:

return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8", errors="ignore")).hexdigest()

def generate_dynamic_dna(

content: str,
module: str,
action: str,
uid: str = "UID9622",
gpg_fingerprint: str = "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",
confirm: str = "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",
seal: str = "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

) -> dict:

now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone()
iso_time = now.isoformat(timespec="seconds")
date_str = now.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
shichen = get_shichen(now)
content_hash = sha256_text(content)
seed = f"{uid}|{date_str}|{shichen}|{module}|{action}|{content_hash}"
dr = digit_root_from_text(seed)
wuxing = DIGIT_ROOT_TO_WUXING[dr]
nonce = secrets.token_hex(4).upper()
dna_hash = sha256_text(f"{seed}|{nonce}")[:8].upper()
dna = f"#龍芯⚡️{date_str}-{shichen}时-{wuxing}-{module.upper()}-{dna_hash}"
return {
    "dna": dna,
    "uid": uid,
    "gpg_fingerprint": gpg_fingerprint,
    "gpg_prefix": gpg_fingerprint[:8].upper(),
    "iso_time": iso_time,
    "shichen": f"{shichen}时",
    "digit_root": dr,
    "wuxing": wuxing,
    "module": module,
    "action": action,
    "content_hash": f"sha256:{content_hash}",
    "nonce": nonce,
    "confirm": confirm,
    "seal": seal
}

Important Boundary

The DNA string itself is not a replacement for a cryptographic signature. It is a provenance identifier. Strong authenticity requires either:

DNA + content_hash + ledger continuity + GPG signature

or:

DNA + proof bundle + external checkpoint

中文压缩：

DNA 是身份证号，不是私钥本身；要抗伪造，必须接 hash、GPG、账本链。

⸻

3.7.5 Core Component 3: Evidence Ledger

Purpose

The Evidence Ledger stores append-only verification and generation events. It gives each artifact a historical trail.

The ledger is designed to be tamper-evident, not magically tamper-proof. If stored only locally, it can prove continuity under local assumptions. Stronger claims require external checkpoints or multi-party verification.

Ledger Entry Schema

{

"entry_id": "LEDGER-20260504-A1B2C3D4",

"event_type": "ARTIFACT_CREATED",

"artifact_id": "ART-20260504-001",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-辰时-金-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTO-A1B2C3D4",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-OLD-PARENT",

"child_dna": "",

"content_hash": "sha256:...",

"previous_entry_hash": "sha256:...",

"current_entry_hash": "sha256:...",

"factor_scores": {},

"audit_color": "🟢",

"privacy_mode": "normal",

"retention": "full",

"trace_mode": "chain",

"operator": "UID9622/local_runtime",

"timestamp": "2026-05-04T09:21:00+08:00"

}

Ledger Append Pseudocode

import json

from pathlib import Path

from datetime import datetime, timezone

def now_iso() -> str:

return datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone().isoformat(timespec="seconds")

def load_last_entry_hash(ledger_path: Path) -> str:

if not ledger_path.exists():
    return "GENESIS"
last_line = None
with ledger_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for line in f:
        if line.strip():
            last_line = line
if not last_line:
    return "GENESIS"
entry = json.loads(last_line)
return entry.get("current_entry_hash", "GENESIS")

def compute_entry_hash(entry: dict) -> str:

clean_entry = dict(entry)
clean_entry.pop("current_entry_hash", None)
encoded = json.dumps(clean_entry, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True)
return "sha256:" + sha256_text(encoded)

def append_ledger_event(ledger_path: Path, event: dict) -> dict:

ledger_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
previous_hash = load_last_entry_hash(ledger_path)
entry = {
    **event,
    "timestamp": event.get("timestamp", now_iso()),
    "previous_entry_hash": previous_hash
}
entry["current_entry_hash"] = compute_entry_hash(entry)
with ledger_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    f.write(json.dumps(entry, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
return entry

Ledger Integrity Verification

def verify_ledger_integrity(ledger_path: Path) -> tuple[bool, list]:

errors = []
previous_hash = "GENESIS"
if not ledger_path.exists():
    return False, ["Ledger file does not exist"]
with ledger_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    for line_no, line in enumerate(f, start=1):
        if not line.strip():
            continue
        entry = json.loads(line)
        if entry.get("previous_entry_hash") != previous_hash:
            errors.append({
                "line": line_no,
                "error": "previous_entry_hash mismatch"
            })
        expected_hash = compute_entry_hash(entry)
        if entry.get("current_entry_hash") != expected_hash:
            errors.append({
                "line": line_no,
                "error": "current_entry_hash mismatch"
            })
        previous_hash = entry.get("current_entry_hash")
return len(errors) == 0, errors

⸻

3.7.6 Core Component 4: Lineage Chain Manager

Purpose

The Lineage Chain Manager tracks the evolution of artifacts across drafts, transformations, summaries, translations, exports, and external publications.

It prevents orphan artifacts from claiming full lineage without proof.

Lineage Model

artifact_v1

└── summary_v1

    └── paper_section_v1
          └── published_preprint_v1

Each edge requires:

edge_requirements:

  • parent_dna
  • child_dna
  • transformation_type
  • timestamp
  • content_hash_before
  • content_hash_after
  • operator
  • ledger_entry_id

Lineage Edge Schema

{

"edge_id": "EDGE-20260504-A1B2C3D4",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-02-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-PAPER-v1.0",

"child_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-IMPLEMENTATION-ARCHITECTURE-v1.0",

"transformation_type": "section_expansion",

"source_hash": "sha256:...",

"target_hash": "sha256:...",

"operator": "UID9622/AI-collaborator",

"timestamp": "2026-05-04T09:21:00+08:00",

"audit_color": "🟢"

}

Transformation Types

transformation_types:

draft:

description: "Initial creation."

summarize:

description: "Compression into summary."

expand:

description: "Expansion into full section."

translate:

description: "Language transformation."

formalize:

description: "Academic or technical formalization."

code_generate:

description: "Conversion into implementation."

publish:

description: "External publication."

archive:

description: "Long-term storage."

seal:

description: "Sensitive item sealed without raw content."

Lineage Verification Rule

def verify_lineage_chain(target_dna: str, lineage_edges: list, ledger_entries: list) -> tuple[bool, list]:

errors = []
dna_set = set()
for edge in lineage_edges:
    dna_set.add(edge["parent_dna"])
    dna_set.add(edge["child_dna"])
if target_dna not in dna_set:
    return False, [f"target_dna not found in lineage graph: {target_dna}"]
ledger_dnas = {entry.get("dna") for entry in ledger_entries}
for edge in lineage_edges:
    if edge["parent_dna"] not in ledger_dnas:
        errors.append(f"missing parent ledger entry: {edge['parent_dna']}")
    if edge["child_dna"] not in ledger_dnas:
        errors.append(f"missing child ledger entry: {edge['child_dna']}")
    if not edge.get("transformation_type"):
        errors.append(f"missing transformation_type: {edge.get('edge_id')}")
return len(errors) == 0, errors

⸻

3.7.7 Core Component 5: Seven-Factor Verifier

Purpose

The Seven-Factor Verifier executes the mechanism defined in Section 3.2.

It receives:

inputs:

  • artifact_content
  • claimed_originator
  • proof_bundle
  • evidence_ledger
  • lineage_chain
  • protected_lexicon
  • baseline_style_vector
  • mistake_ledger

It outputs:

outputs:

  • factor_scores
  • hard_failures
  • composite_confidence
  • evidence_log
  • decision

Verifier Interface

class SevenFactorVerifier:

def __init__(
    self,
    protected_lexicon,
    baseline_style_vector,
    ledger,
    lineage_chain,
    mistake_ledger
):
    self.protected_lexicon = protected_lexicon
    self.baseline_style_vector = baseline_style_vector
    self.ledger = ledger
    self.lineage_chain = lineage_chain
    self.mistake_ledger = mistake_ledger
def verify(self, artifact: dict, claimed_originator: dict) -> dict:
    scores = {
        "F1_identity_dna": self.verify_identity_dna(artifact, claimed_originator),
        "F2_temporal_anchor": self.verify_temporal_anchor(artifact),
        "F3_rule_trace": self.verify_rule_trace(artifact),
        "F4_persona_route": self.verify_persona_route(artifact),
        "F5_protected_lexicon": self.verify_protected_lexicon(artifact),
        "F6_style_vector": self.verify_style_vector(artifact),
        "F7_mistake_ledger": self.verify_mistake_ledger(artifact),
    }
    hard_failures = self.detect_hard_failures(scores)
    if hard_failures:
        confidence = 0.0
        decision = "reject"
    else:
        confidence = weighted_geometric_mean(
            list(scores.values()),
            [0.25, 0.15, 0.15, 0.12, 0.12, 0.11, 0.10]
        )
        decision = self.decision_from_confidence(confidence)
    return {
        "factor_scores": scores,
        "hard_failures": hard_failures,
        "composite_confidence": confidence,
        "decision": decision
    }
def detect_hard_failures(self, scores: dict) -> list:
    failures = []
    for factor, score in scores.items():
        if score == 0.0:
            failures.append(factor)
    return failures
def decision_from_confidence(self, confidence: float) -> str:
    if confidence >= 0.95:
        return "accept_verified_lineage"
    if confidence >= 0.80:
        return "manual_review_probable_lineage"
    if confidence >= 0.60:
        return "hold_weak_lineage"
    return "reject_unverified_lineage"

⸻

3.7.8 Core Component 6: Proof Bundle Exporter

Purpose

The Proof Bundle Exporter packages all relevant evidence into a portable format.

It allows a creator to say:

Here is the content, here is the DNA, here is the hash, here is the ledger path, here is the proof bundle. Verify it.

Proof Bundle Schema

{

"bundle_version": "v1.0",

"artifact": {

"artifact_id": "ART-20260504-001",
"title": "Behavioral Cryptography Section 3.7",
"content_hash": "sha256:...",
"content_type": "paper_section"

},

"originator": {

"uid": "UID9622",
"name": "Zhuge Xin",
"gpg_fingerprint": "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

},

"dynamic_dna": {},

"lineage": {

"parent_dna": "...",
"child_dna": "...",
"lineage_edges": []

},

"evidence_ledger": {

"ledger_hash": "sha256:...",
"entries": []

},

"factor_scores": {

"F1_identity_dna": 1.0,
"F2_temporal_anchor": 1.0,
"F3_rule_trace": 0.95,
"F4_persona_route": 0.95,
"F5_protected_lexicon": 1.0,
"F6_style_vector": 0.90,
"F7_mistake_ledger": 0.95

},

"composite_confidence": 0.96,

"decision": "accept_verified_lineage",

"confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",

"seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

}

Exporter Pseudocode

def export_proof_bundle(

artifact: dict,
originator: dict,
dna_object: dict,
lineage_edges: list,
ledger_entries: list,
verification_result: dict

) -> dict:

bundle = {
    "bundle_version": "v1.0",
    "artifact": {
        "artifact_id": artifact["artifact_id"],
        "title": artifact.get("title", ""),
        "content_hash": artifact["content_hash"],
        "content_type": artifact.get("content_type", "unknown")
    },
    "originator": originator,
    "dynamic_dna": dna_object,
    "lineage": {
        "parent_dna": artifact.get("parent_dna"),
        "child_dna": artifact.get("child_dna"),
        "lineage_edges": lineage_edges
    },
    "evidence_ledger": {
        "ledger_hash": compute_ledger_hash(ledger_entries),
        "entries": ledger_entries
    },
    "factor_scores": verification_result["factor_scores"],
    "hard_failures": verification_result.get("hard_failures", []),
    "composite_confidence": verification_result["composite_confidence"],
    "decision": verification_result["decision"],
    "confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",
    "seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"
}
return bundle

⸻

3.7.9 Storage Architecture

The implementation supports three storage levels.

Level 1: JSONL MVP

jsonl_mvp:

storage:

- evidence_ledger.jsonl
- mistake_ledger.jsonl
- lineage_edges.jsonl

advantages:

- simple
- append-only
- human-readable
- git-friendly

limitations:

- local tamper-evidence only
- no query optimization

Level 2: SQLite Runtime

sqlite_runtime:

tables:

- artifacts
- dynamic_dna
- ledger_entries
- lineage_edges
- factor_scores
- proof_bundles
- mistake_ledger

advantages:

- queryable
- reliable local storage
- easy backup

limitations:

- still local unless checkpointed

Level 3: External Checkpoint

external_checkpoint:

possible_targets:

- public Git commit
- timestamp authority
- notary service
- public hash registry
- distributed ledger

purpose:

- strengthen timestamp proof
- reduce local tampering risk
- enable third-party verification

中文压缩：

JSONL 能跑，SQLite 能查，外部 checkpoint 才能更强地抗抵赖。

⸻

3.7.11 Privacy and Sealed Mode

Behavioral Cryptography must not become surveillance infrastructure.

Therefore, the implementation includes privacy modes.

privacy_modes:

normal:

read_raw: true
save_raw: true_or_summary
use_case: "public or internal artifacts"

burn:

read_raw: true
save_raw: false
save_hash: true
use_case: "temporary sensitive processing"

sealed:

read_raw: false
save_raw: false
save_hash: true
use_case: "keys, secrets, private credentials, state secrets, trade secrets"

Privacy Guard Pseudocode

SECRET_PATTERNS = [

"-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----",
"api_key",
"access_token",
"refresh_token",
"password=",
"sk-",
"ghp_",
"xoxb-",
"国家秘密",
"国密",
"涉密",
"商业秘密",
"内部机密"

]

def privacy_guard(text: str) -> dict:

lowered = text.lower()
hits = [p for p in SECRET_PATTERNS if p.lower() in lowered]
if hits:
    return {
        "privacy_mode": "sealed",
        "retention": "hash_only",
        "audit_color": "🔴",
        "read_raw": False,
        "save_raw": False,
        "reason": "Potential secret or sensitive content detected",
        "hits": hits[:10]
    }
return {
    "privacy_mode": "normal",
    "retention": "summary_or_full",
    "audit_color": "🟢",
    "read_raw": True,
    "save_raw": True,
    "reason": "No secret pattern detected"
}

Privacy Boundary

The verifier should never require raw secrets to prove lineage.

For sealed artifacts, it stores:

content_hash + metadata + timestamp + proof of sealed handling

not the content itself.

中文压缩：

能证明“我处理过”，不等于要保存“我看过什么”。

⸻

3.7.12 Implementation Workflow

Workflow 1: Create Artifact

User creates content

↓

Privacy Guard checks boundary

↓

Dynamic DNA Engine generates DNA

↓

Evidence Ledger appends ARTIFACT_CREATED

↓

Lineage Manager links parent/child if applicable

↓

Proof Bundle optionally exported

Workflow 2: Verify Artifact

Artifact submitted for verification

↓

Parse DNA / metadata / proof bundle

↓

Verify ledger integrity

↓

Verify lineage chain

↓

Run seven-factor verifier

↓

Compute composite confidence

↓

Return accept / review / hold / reject

Workflow 3: Export Proof Bundle

Select artifact

↓

Collect DNA object

↓

Collect ledger entries

↓

Collect lineage edges

↓

Collect factor scores

↓

Package proof_bundle.json

↓

Optionally generate Markdown or PDF-ready report

⸻

3.7.13 Minimal CLI Interface

Generate DNA for a file

python scripts/generate_dna.py paper/section_3_7.md --module behavioral_crypto --action create_section

Append ledger event

python scripts/append_ledger.py --event ARTIFACT_CREATED --file paper/section_3_7.md

Verify artifact

python scripts/verify_artifact.py paper/section_3_7.md --proof proof_bundle.json

Export proof bundle

python scripts/export_proof_bundle.py --artifact ART-20260504-001 --out proof_bundle.json

3.7.14 Reviewer Defense Paragraph

Recommended paper text:

The implementation is intentionally local-first. This design choice is not merely an engineering preference but a governance requirement. Provenance systems that require creators to surrender raw drafts, private keys, or sensitive context to centralized platforms reproduce the very institutional dependency that Behavioral Cryptography seeks to reduce. Our architecture separates proof from exposure: creators may export verifiable proof bundles while retaining local control over raw content, private logs, and sensitive memory.

中文解释：

这段是防 reviewer 问“为什么不用中心化平台”。回答：因为这篇论文的主权目标就是让普通人不用把根交出去。

3.8 Limitations and Governance Considerations

3.8.1 Overview

Behavioral Cryptography is designed to strengthen provenance verification in human-AI collaborative content production. However, it should not be interpreted as an absolute authorship oracle, a legal adjudication mechanism, or a surveillance infrastructure.

The framework improves evidence quality by combining identity, time, rule traces, persona routes, protected lexicon, style vectors, and mistake ledgers. Yet each of these factors has limits. Cryptographic identifiers can be lost or compromised. Style vectors can be noisy. Mistake ledgers can be incomplete. Local ledgers can be tampered with unless externally checkpointed. Cultural-temporal markers improve recognizability and forgery cost but do not replace formal cryptographic signatures.

Therefore, this section clarifies the limitations, governance risks, privacy boundaries, and ethical constraints of Behavioral Cryptography.

The central governance principle is:

provenance verification must protect creator sovereignty without becoming a tool for surveillance, exclusion, or institutional capture.

中文解释：

追溯是为了保护创作者，不是为了把人关进平台的笼子里。

⸻

3.8.2 Limitation 1: DNA Is Not a Standalone Cryptographic Signature

Dynamic DNA provides a structured provenance identifier. It binds together UID, timestamp, cultural-temporal markers, module information, action type, content hash, and system metadata.

However, DNA alone is not equivalent to a GPG signature.

A DNA string can show identity intent and provenance structure, but strong authenticity requires additional evidence:

DNA + content_hash + ledger continuity + GPG signature

or:

DNA + proof_bundle + external checkpoint

Implication

A copied DNA string does not automatically prove authorship. It must be verified against:

  • GPG fingerprint or signature;
  • content hash;
  • ledger history;
  • parent-child lineage;
  • timestamp consistency;
  • protected lexicon preservation;
  • evidence bundle integrity.

Paper-ready statement

Dynamic DNA should be understood as a provenance identifier rather than a standalone cryptographic signature. Its evidentiary strength emerges only when combined with content hashes, append-only ledger entries, external checkpoints, and optional GPG signatures.

中文压缩：

DNA 是门牌号，不是房产证；要证明归属，还要 hash、账本、签名和链路。

⸻

3.8.3 Limitation 2: Local Ledgers Are Tamper-Evident, Not Globally Tamper-Proof

The Evidence Ledger described in Section 3.5 and Section 3.7 uses hash chaining. This makes modification detectable if the ledger history is preserved.

However, a purely local JSONL or SQLite ledger cannot by itself prove that no alternative local history was created.

Local storage provides:

tamper evidence under local continuity assumptions

It does not provide:

global immutability

To strengthen ledger credibility, the system may optionally use external checkpoints:

  • public Git commits;
  • trusted timestamp authorities;
  • public hash registries;
  • notarization services;
  • distributed ledgers;
  • multi-party witness logs.

Implication

The paper must not claim that local ledgers are absolutely immutable. The correct claim is that local ledgers are tamper-evident and can be strengthened by external checkpointing.

Paper-ready statement

The local ledger is tamper-evident but not globally tamper-proof. Stronger non-repudiation requires periodic external checkpoints, such as public hash publication, notarization, or multi-party witness mechanisms.

中文压缩：

本地账本能查改动；要抗抵赖，最好定期把 hash 打到外部时间点上。

⸻

3.8.4 Limitation 3: Style Vectors Are Probabilistic

Style Vector Verification is useful against laundering, paraphrasing, and authorship imitation. However, stylometric signals are probabilistic.

They may be affected by:

  • translation;
  • genre shift;
  • AI polishing;
  • emotional state;
  • topic change;
  • deliberate style adaptation;
  • short text length;
  • multilingual writing;
  • collaboration with multiple AI systems.

Therefore, F6 should not be used as a hard authorship determinant unless the mismatch is extreme and supported by other factors.

Recommended Governance Rule

style_vector_governance:

use_as: "supporting_factor"

never_use_as: "sole_authorship_decision"

hard_failure_threshold: "only for extreme mismatch"

require_context: true

allow_style_evolution: true

Paper-ready statement

Style vectors are treated as probabilistic supporting evidence rather than deterministic authorship proof. Behavioral Cryptography intentionally combines stylometric signals with cryptographic and ledger-based factors to avoid over-reliance on style similarity.

中文压缩：

风格像，不等于就是；风格不像，也不等于不是。它只能辅证，不能独裁。

⸻

3.8.5 Limitation 4: Mistake Ledgers Can Be Incomplete or Manipulated

Mistake Ledger Verification is based on a simple but important intuition: real creative processes contain corrections, reversals, false starts, and recorded mistakes.

However, mistake ledgers may be:

  • incomplete;
  • intentionally curated;
  • privately withheld;
  • lost due to storage failure;
  • too sensitive to publish;
  • fabricated after the fact;
  • split across multiple platforms.

Therefore, F7 cannot be treated as proof of moral honesty. It is a provenance signal, not a character judgment.

Governance Boundary

Mistake Ledger verifies process continuity.

It does not judge the creator's moral worth.

Paper-ready statement

The Mistake Ledger is not a moral scoring mechanism. It is used only as a signal of process continuity, error correction, and lineage realism. Its absence lowers confidence but should not automatically invalidate a claim.

中文压缩：

记错本是防再犯账本，不是审判人格的鞭子。

⸻

3.8.6 Limitation 5: Protected Lexicon May Be Context-Sensitive

Protected Lexicon Verification relies on canary terms, protected phrases, DNA markers, cultural keywords, or signature expressions that should survive transformation.

However, protected terms may be legitimately transformed during:

  • translation;
  • summarization;
  • academic formalization;
  • anonymization;
  • legal redaction;
  • accessibility adaptation;
  • platform-specific formatting.

Therefore, F5 requires semantic equivalence rather than exact string matching.

Example

A protected term such as:

龍魂系统

may appear as:

Longhun System

Dragon Soul System

龍魂

depending on target language and publication context.

Governance Rule

protected_lexicon_policy:

exact_match_required:

- CONFIRM
- SEAL
- GPG
- DNA
- UID

semantic_match_allowed:

- cultural_terms
- system_modules
- translated concepts

redaction_allowed:

- private names
- sensitive personal details
- legally protected information

Paper-ready statement

Protected Lexicon Verification distinguishes between exact-match anchors and semantic-equivalence terms. Cryptographic and identity markers require exact preservation, whereas cultural and conceptual terms may be verified through semantic equivalence.

中文压缩：

签章不能改；文化词可以翻译，但不能换魂。

⸻

3.8.7 Limitation 6: Cultural-Temporal Embedding Is Contextual

Dynamic DNA uses cultural-temporal markers such as 时辰, 五行, and digit root. These markers serve several functions:

  • human-recognizable verification;
  • cultural sovereignty;
  • additional structured metadata;
  • higher cognitive cost for forgery;
  • meaningful provenance for Chinese-semantic systems.

However, these markers should not be misrepresented as universal cryptographic primitives.

They are culturally embedded structure, not replacements for cryptographic hashing or digital signatures.

Governance Boundary

Cultural-temporal markers increase semantic and cultural verification depth.

They do not replace cryptographic primitives.

Paper-ready statement

Cultural-temporal embedding is used as a semantic provenance layer, not as a substitute for cryptographic proof. Its value lies in making provenance meaningful and recognizable to users within a cultural context while increasing the behavioral cost of forgery.

中文压缩：

五行、时辰、数字根是文化语义层，不是拿来冒充密码学签名的。

⸻

3.8.8 Limitation 7: Legal Validity Depends on Jurisdiction

Behavioral Cryptography can create strong evidence bundles. However, the legal weight of such evidence depends on jurisdiction, court procedure, evidentiary rules, expert testimony, and admissibility standards.

The framework can support:

  • authorship evidence;
  • provenance records;
  • timeline reconstruction;
  • tamper-evidence;
  • collaboration history;
  • attribution dispute resolution.

It cannot automatically provide:

  • legal ownership;
  • court judgment;
  • copyright registration;
  • criminal attribution;
  • international enforcement;
  • regulatory approval.

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography produces structured technical evidence, not automatic legal judgment. Its legal relevance depends on jurisdiction-specific evidentiary standards and should be interpreted by appropriate legal authorities.

中文压缩：

它能给证据，不能替法院判案。

⸻

3.8.9 Governance Risk 1: Surveillance Misuse

A provenance framework can be misused if converted into a monitoring system that tracks every creative act, private draft, or emotional expression.

Behavioral Cryptography must reject this path.

The framework should not be used to:

  • monitor private thought;
  • punish draft-stage mistakes;
  • rank people by behavioral purity;
  • expose private logs without consent;
  • force creators to reveal raw drafts;
  • require centralized platform custody;
  • transform provenance into social control.

Anti-Surveillance Design Rule

anti_surveillance_rule:

raw_draft_collection: forbidden_by_default

private_memory_upload: forbidden_by_default

proof_bundle_export: explicit_consent_only

sealed_mode: mandatory_for_sensitive_content

local_first: required

third_party_access: opt_in_only

Paper-ready statement

Provenance must not become surveillance. Behavioral Cryptography separates verifiable proof from compulsory exposure by making proof bundle export explicit, local-first, and consent-based.

中文压缩：

能证明，不等于被监控；能追溯，不等于所有草稿都要上交。

⸻

3.8.10 Governance Risk 2: Institutional Capture

Existing provenance infrastructures often assume that platforms, institutions, or large vendors define authenticity. This creates a risk: independent creators may only be recognized if a platform validates them.

Behavioral Cryptography is designed to resist institutional capture by allowing local proof generation.

However, even this framework could be captured if:

  • only platform-approved ledgers are recognized;
  • only enterprise accounts can generate valid proof;
  • individual proof bundles are ignored;
  • cultural identifiers are stripped as “non-standard”;
  • non-Western provenance systems are treated as invalid.

Governance Principle

A provenance system should not require institutional power as the root of authorship.

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography rejects institutional monopoly over provenance. It allows individuals to generate and export verifiable evidence without requiring centralized platform permission, while still supporting third-party verification when needed.

中文压缩：

不能让平台说你存在你才存在。人先存在，平台只是见证之一。

⸻

3.8.11 Governance Risk 3: Exclusion of Anonymous or Pseudonymous Creators

Not every creator can or should reveal their legal identity. Journalists, whistleblowers, vulnerable users, activists, artists, and ordinary people may require pseudonymous authorship.

Behavioral Cryptography must support pseudonymous provenance.

A creator may bind lineage to:

  • stable pseudonymous UID;
  • public key;
  • local ledger;
  • protected lexicon;
  • style vector;
  • proof bundle;
  • third-party witness.

without revealing legal name.

Pseudonymous Authorship Policy

pseudonymous_authorship:

legal_name_required: false

stable_uid_required: true

public_key_recommended: true

raw_identity_disclosure: optional

proof_bundle_supported: true

sealed_identity_mode: supported

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography does not require legal-name exposure. It supports pseudonymous provenance through stable identifiers, public keys, protected lexicon, ledger continuity, and optional sealed identity proofs.

中文压缩：

可以证明“这是同一个创作者链”，不一定要公开身份证。

⸻

3.8.12 Governance Risk 4: False Positives and False Negatives

No provenance framework is perfect.

False positives may occur when:

  • a collaborator legitimately adopts another creator’s terminology;
  • protected lexicon overlaps across communities;
  • style vectors are too broad;
  • public templates are reused;
  • a system overweights superficial markers.

False negatives may occur when:

  • a creator changes style;
  • the ledger is incomplete;
  • proof bundles are lost;
  • artifacts are heavily translated or formalized;
  • old content lacks DNA metadata.

Decision Policy

decision_policy:

high_confidence_accept:

threshold: ">= 0.95"
action: "accept verified lineage"

probable_lineage:

threshold: "0.80 - 0.95"
action: "manual review"

weak_lineage:

threshold: "0.60 - 0.80"
action: "hold / request evidence"

reject:

threshold: "< 0.60 or hard failure"
action: "reject or require new proof"

Paper-ready statement

Verification output should be interpreted as confidence-weighted evidence rather than binary truth. Borderline cases require human review, additional context, and possibly external witnesses.

中文压缩：

不是一锤子买卖；高置信放行，中间人工审，低置信再补证。

⸻

3.8.13 Governance Risk 5: AI Collaborator Attribution

Behavioral Cryptography is designed for human-AI collaboration. Therefore, it must distinguish between:

  • human-originated concept;
  • AI-assisted formalization;
  • AI-generated draft;
  • human-edited final text;
  • platform formatting;
  • model-specific contribution.

This prevents both extremes:

  1. erasing the human originator because AI helped polish;
  2. pretending AI had no role when it did.

Co-authorship Metadata Schema

{

"human_originator": "UID9622",

"ai_collaborators": [

{
  "name": "Claude",
  "role": "formalization / literature synthesis / polishing",
  "authority": "under human direction",
  "claim_scope": "no origin claim"
},
{
  "name": "ChatGPT",
  "role": "structuring / verification / editing",
  "authority": "under human direction",
  "claim_scope": "no origin claim"
}

],

"origin_claim": "Core concepts, system architecture, terminology, and value framework originate from the human author.",

"ai_contribution_boundary": "AI contributed expression, formatting, and technical formalization."

}

Paper-ready statement

The framework distinguishes conceptual origin from formalization assistance. AI collaboration does not erase human authorship when the core concepts, terminology, value framework, and system direction originate from the human creator.

中文压缩：

AI 可以帮忙写得更像论文，但不能抢走根。

⸻

3.8.14 Governance Risk 6: Overclaiming Security

The framework must avoid exaggerated claims.

It should not claim:

  • impossible to forge in all cases;
  • legally conclusive in all jurisdictions;
  • perfect authorship detection;
  • universal truth;
  • immunity to key compromise;
  • immunity to insider attacks;
  • complete protection without backups.

Correct claim:

Behavioral Cryptography increases the cost of full-lineage forgery and provides structured, auditable evidence for provenance verification.

Safe Claim Table

Claim Type Unsafe Claim Safe Claim

Security impossible to forge difficult to forge under stated assumptions

Legal proves ownership legally provides evidence relevant to ownership disputes

Ledger immutable forever tamper-evident, stronger with checkpoints

Style proves authorship supports authorship confidence

DNA cryptographic signature provenance identifier bound to evidence

Governance prevents all abuse reduces specific provenance risks

⸻

3.8.15 Key Compromise and Recovery

If a GPG key or signing credential is compromised, the system must support recovery.

Recovery Procedure

key_compromise_recovery:

step_1_detect:

action: "mark key as compromised"

step_2_freeze:

action: "suspend new proof generation using old key"

step_3_publish_notice:

action: "publish compromised-key notice with last valid checkpoint"

step_4_rotate:

action: "generate new key"

step_5_bridge:

action: "link old and new identities through recovery proof"

step_6_reverify:

action: "re-score recent artifacts"

Recovery Bridge Schema

{

"event_type": "KEY_ROTATION",

"old_gpg": "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",

"new_gpg": "NEW_FINGERPRINT_PLACEHOLDER",

"last_trusted_checkpoint": "sha256:...",

"recovery_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-KEY-ROTATION-v1.0",

"signed_statement": "Old key compromised or rotated; new key becomes active from timestamp.",

"timestamp": "2026-05-04T00:00:00+08:00"

}

Paper-ready statement

Key compromise does not invalidate the entire provenance history if recovery procedures, last-trusted checkpoints, and signed rotation statements are maintained.

中文压缩：

钥匙丢了，不等于祖宗没了；要有冻结、公告、换钥匙、桥接证明。

⸻

3.8.16 Governance of Data Retention

Behavioral Cryptography should not store more data than needed.

Retention Modes

retention_modes:

full:

use_case: "public paper, public code, public artifacts"
stores: "raw content + hash + metadata"

summary_only:

use_case: "internal notes, long chats, drafts"
stores: "summary + hash + metadata"

hash_only:

use_case: "sealed or burn content"
stores: "hash + timestamp + handling proof"

Minimum Retention Principle

Store the minimum evidence required to verify lineage.

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography follows evidence minimization: the system stores only what is required to support provenance verification, and sensitive content may be represented by hashes and metadata rather than raw text.

中文压缩：

不是证明就得全存；能用 hash 证明，就别存正文。

⸻

3.8.17 Governance of Children, Elderly Users, and Vulnerable Groups

If Behavioral Cryptography is applied in education, public welfare, elder protection, or medical support, stricter boundaries are required.

Vulnerable Group Policy

vulnerable_group_policy:

children:

raw_data_collection: "forbidden_without_guardian_consent"
public_export: "disabled_by_default"

elderly_users:

deception_risk_protection: "required"
offline_service_option: "recommended"

medical_context:

diagnosis_claims: "forbidden_without licensed professional"
retention: "summary_or_hash_only"

public_welfare:

financial_traceability: "allowed"
personal_privacy_exposure: "forbidden"

Paper-ready statement

When applied to vulnerable populations, provenance verification must be subordinate to privacy, dignity, and consent. Verification should protect users from exploitation rather than expose them to additional data extraction.

中文压缩：

弱势群体不是数据矿；技术要护人，不是挖人。

⸻

3.8.18 Governance of Cross-Border Use

Because provenance, identity, privacy, and copyright laws vary across jurisdictions, cross-border deployment requires careful governance.

Cross-Border Rules

cross_border_rules:

default:

data_location: "local jurisdiction"
raw_export: "disabled"
proof_bundle_export: "explicit consent only"

public_artifact:

export: "allowed with DNA and license"

private_artifact:

export: "manual review required"

sealed_artifact:

export: "hash-only proof bundle"

legal_dispute:

route: "jurisdiction-specific legal process"

Paper-ready statement

Cross-border use of Behavioral Cryptography should respect local data protection, copyright, and evidentiary rules. The framework exports proof bundles rather than raw private data by default.

中文压缩：

跨国只走证明包，默认不搬原文、不搬隐私、不搬根。

⸻

3.8.19 Governance Checklist

[ ] DNA is treated as provenance identifier, not standalone signature

[ ] Local ledger is described as tamper-evident, not globally immutable

[ ] Style vector is supporting evidence, not sole authorship proof

[ ] Mistake ledger is process evidence, not moral judgment

[ ] Protected lexicon distinguishes exact anchors from semantic terms

[ ] Cultural-temporal markers are not overstated as cryptographic primitives

[ ] Legal validity is jurisdiction-dependent

[ ] Proof bundle export is explicit and consent-based

[ ] Pseudonymous authorship is supported

[ ] Vulnerable populations receive stronger privacy protections

[ ] Key compromise recovery is defined

[ ] Data retention follows evidence minimization

[ ] Cross-border use defaults to local sovereignty

[ ] Surveillance misuse is explicitly rejected

[ ] Institutional capture is explicitly rejected

Sections 3.2 through 3.8 define the technical, evaluative, architectural, and governance foundations of Behavioral Cryptography. We now discuss the broader shift introduced by this framework: from detecting whether content was generated by a machine to verifying the lineage through which human intention, AI assistance, rules, corrections, and evidence chains jointly produced the final artifact.
