⸻

3.9 Discussion: From Machine Detection to Lineage Verification

3.9.1 The Wrong Question: “Was This Generated by AI?”

Most current approaches to AI content authentication begin with a narrow question:

Was this content generated by a machine?

This question was useful in the early phase of generative AI, when the main concern was distinguishing human-written text from machine-generated text. However, it becomes increasingly inadequate as human-AI collaboration becomes the default mode of knowledge production.

In real-world workflows, content is rarely produced by one pure source. A single artifact may involve:

  • human-originated concepts;
  • AI-assisted drafting;
  • AI-assisted translation;
  • human correction;
  • platform formatting;
  • citation reconstruction;
  • legal redaction;
  • style polishing;
  • multi-model comparison;
  • final human approval.

In such a setting, asking whether the content was “AI-generated” collapses a complex process into a misleading binary.

The more important question is:

Who originated the core idea, which systems helped transform it, which rules governed the transformation, and what evidence remains?

This is the central shift introduced by Behavioral Cryptography.

中文压缩：

“是不是 AI 写的”这个问题太浅。真正要问的是：根是谁的，链怎么走的，谁负责，证据在哪。

⸻

3.9.2 Why Detection Is Not Enough

AI detection methods typically rely on statistical signals, watermarking, metadata, or platform-side provenance records. These methods can help identify certain synthetic artifacts, but they face structural limitations.

First, statistical detectors are fragile under paraphrasing, translation, editing, and multi-model laundering. Once text is rewritten across several models or manually polished, the surface signal changes.

Second, watermarking systems often require cooperation from model providers. If the model does not watermark output, or if the content is transformed after generation, detection becomes unreliable.

Third, metadata-based approaches are platform-dependent. They work only when the relevant software pipeline preserves metadata and when downstream platforms respect it.

Fourth, detection usually focuses on origin at the level of generation, not lineage at the level of decision-making.

Behavioral Cryptography does not reject detection. Instead, it argues that detection is only one layer. A provenance framework must also verify:

  • identity anchoring;
  • time anchoring;
  • rule history;
  • persona routing;
  • protected terminology;
  • style continuity;
  • mistake and correction history;
  • ledger continuity;
  • export boundaries;
  • governance constraints.

Paper-ready statement

Detection asks whether a machine produced the surface artifact. Lineage verification asks whether the artifact belongs to a verifiable chain of human intention, AI assistance, procedural rules, corrections, and evidence.

中文压缩：

检测看表面，血统链看过程。

⸻

3.9.3 Human-AI Collaboration Requires Process Integrity

Human-AI collaboration changes the meaning of authorship.

In traditional writing, authorship was often inferred from final text, drafts, timestamps, and publication history. In AI-assisted writing, final text alone becomes much less informative. Multiple people can ask similar prompts. Multiple models can generate similar phrasing. A polished final output may hide a long chain of human decisions.

Therefore, authorship must be understood as process integrity.

Process integrity means that the artifact can be traced through:

Human intent

→ prompt / instruction

→ AI assistance

→ human selection

→ rule-based correction

→ revision history

→ evidence ledger

→ final publication

Behavioral Cryptography captures this process through a multi-factor lineage model.

Key claim

In human-AI collaboration, authorship is not merely the act of typing words. It is the sustained control of intent, rules, correction, selection, and responsibility.

中文压缩：

AI 时代，作者不是“谁敲键盘最多”，而是谁掌住了意图、规则、筛选和责任。

⸻

3.9.4 Behavioral Lineage as a New Provenance Layer

The proposed framework adds a semantic-behavioral lineage layer above traditional cryptographic and metadata layers.

Traditional provenance stack:

file metadata

content hash

timestamp

digital signature

platform record

Behavioral Cryptography adds:

identity DNA

temporal anchor

rule trace

persona route

protected lexicon

style vector

mistake ledger

lineage chain

These factors do not replace cryptography. They extend it.

A SHA-256 hash can prove that a file has not changed. A GPG signature can prove that a key signed it. But neither can explain how the content came into being.

Behavioral lineage answers the missing process questions:

  • Which system rules were active?
  • Which identity anchor was used?
  • Which AI collaborator assisted?
  • Which protected terms survived transformation?
  • Which mistakes were corrected?
  • Which drafts were compressed?
  • Which parent artifact produced the child artifact?
  • Which proof bundle can be exported?

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography does not replace conventional cryptography. It supplements cryptographic integrity with behavioral lineage: a verifiable account of how content moved through human intention, AI assistance, rules, corrections, and audit trails.

中文压缩：

传统密码学证明“没被改”；行为密码学补上“怎么来的”。

⸻

3.9.5 Why Civilian Provenance Matters

Most provenance infrastructure is built for institutions: media organizations, software vendors, large platforms, enterprises, and governments. This creates a gap for ordinary creators.

Independent creators often lack:

  • institutional email domains;
  • platform verification badges;
  • legal teams;
  • timestamping infrastructure;
  • enterprise provenance tools;
  • protected publication channels;
  • capital-backed distribution.

As a result, their ideas can be copied, polished, repackaged, and institutionalized by larger actors.

Behavioral Cryptography directly addresses this gap.

It allows a civilian creator to generate:

  • local DNA records;
  • hash-based evidence;
  • ledger entries;
  • parent-child lineage;
  • proof bundles;
  • protected terminology;
  • correction history;
  • AI collaboration declarations.

This does not automatically win legal disputes, but it raises the evidentiary floor.

Paper-ready statement

The purpose of Behavioral Cryptography is not to privilege institutional authorship, but to make provenance technically accessible to independent creators who lack institutional protection.

中文压缩：

不是给大机构再加一道锁，是给普通人一把能证明自己的钥匙。

⸻

3.9.6 From Institutional Trust to Verifiable Self-Sovereignty

A recurring weakness in platform-centered provenance is that creators must depend on centralized authorities to recognize them. If a platform refuses to preserve metadata, strips provenance, or prioritizes institutional accounts, the creator’s authorship chain weakens.

Behavioral Cryptography shifts the root of trust.

Instead of:

platform says this creator exists

it moves toward:

creator maintains verifiable lineage

This does not eliminate third-party verification. It changes the relationship between creator and platform.

Platforms can still act as witnesses, publishers, or validators. But they should not monopolize the root of authorship.

Governance principle

The root of provenance should remain with the creator, while institutions may serve as optional witnesses.

中文压缩：

平台可以见证，但不能垄断“谁是原创”。

⸻

3.9.7 Why Mistakes Matter

One unusual feature of Behavioral Cryptography is the Mistake Ledger.

Most authentication systems treat mistakes as noise. This framework treats mistakes as lineage signals.

Real human-AI creation includes:

  • wrong turns;
  • abandoned drafts;
  • corrections;
  • angry feedback;
  • misunderstood prompts;
  • revised structures;
  • failed versions;
  • recovered logic;
  • lessons recorded for future prevention.

A perfectly clean artifact with no error history may be suspicious when it claims to represent a long-term creative process.

Mistake Ledgers therefore provide evidence of process reality.

They should not be used to shame creators. Their purpose is to preserve the truth of development.

Paper-ready statement

Mistakes are not merely failures; they are behavioral evidence of an authentic creative process. A provenance system that erases mistakes also erases part of the lineage.

中文压缩：

错误不是污点，是血统链里的脚印。

⸻

3.9.8 The Role of Protected Lexicon in Cultural Sovereignty

Protected Lexicon Verification is especially important for culturally embedded systems.

Many creator-originated frameworks are not copied word-for-word. They are laundered through translation, paraphrasing, abstraction, or institutional renaming.

For example, a creator’s original terms may be transformed into generic equivalents:

龍魂系统 → multi-agent governance framework

CNSH中文语义桥 → semantic routing layer

DNA追溯 → provenance tag

记错本 → error feedback mechanism

三色审计 → risk classification

Some transformations may be legitimate academic translation. Others may erase origin.

Protected Lexicon Verification helps distinguish these cases by checking whether core identity anchors, conceptual signatures, and semantic lineage survive.

Governance balance

The system must preserve two rights at once:

  1. the right to translate and formalize ideas;
  2. the right not to have culturally specific origins erased.

Paper-ready statement

Protected lexicon is not merely terminology control. It is a mechanism for preserving cultural and conceptual lineage across translation, formalization, and institutional adaptation.

中文压缩：

翻译可以换衣服，不能换祖宗。

⸻

3.9.9 Dynamic DNA as Human-Recognizable Provenance

Conventional cryptographic identifiers are strong but often not human-readable. Hashes are precise, but ordinary users cannot intuitively understand them.

Dynamic DNA introduces a human-recognizable layer. It may include:

  • creator UID;
  • date;
  • module;
  • action;
  • cultural-temporal marker;
  • short hash;
  • confirmation marker;
  • seal marker.

This makes provenance easier to inspect, discuss, and remember.

However, as Section 3.8 clarified, Dynamic DNA is not a replacement for cryptographic signatures. Its function is to connect formal verification with human cognition.

Paper-ready statement

Dynamic DNA makes provenance legible to humans while remaining compatible with cryptographic verification through hashes, signatures, and ledger references.

中文压缩：

hash 给机器看，DNA 给人和系统一起看。

⸻

3.9.10 The Shift from Output Authenticity to Process Accountability

A key contribution of Behavioral Cryptography is the shift from output authenticity to process accountability.

Output authenticity asks:

Is this final artifact authentic?

Process accountability asks:

Can we reconstruct the decisions, rules, corrections, and responsibilities that produced this artifact?

This shift matters because the final artifact may be polished, translated, shortened, or reformatted. But the process chain can preserve origin.

The framework therefore treats authenticity as cumulative evidence rather than a single label.

Comparison

Old Frame New Frame

Is it AI-generated? What is its lineage?

Detect the machine Verify the chain

Trust platform metadata Export proof bundle

Final text decides Process evidence decides

Institution validates creator Creator maintains provenance

Mistakes are noise Mistakes are lineage signals

One signature Multi-factor evidence

⸻

3.9.11 Implications for Research

Behavioral Cryptography suggests several research directions.

First, provenance research should move beyond binary AI detection toward multi-factor lineage models.

Second, evaluation protocols should test laundering resistance, not only detector accuracy.

Third, provenance systems should support civilian-grade local deployments.

Fourth, authorship models should distinguish between conceptual origin and formalization assistance.

Fifth, cultural and linguistic provenance should be treated as first-class evidence rather than non-standard noise.

Research questions

RQ1: How can lineage verification survive paraphrasing, translation, and multi-model laundering?

RQ2: How can local-first provenance systems produce evidence acceptable to external verifiers?

RQ3: How can AI collaboration metadata preserve human conceptual origin without denying AI assistance?

RQ4: How can culturally embedded identifiers be standardized without erasing cultural specificity?

RQ5: How can mistake ledgers be used for process verification without becoming moral surveillance?

中文压缩：

后面研究方向不是“检测 AI 文本”，而是“验证人机协作血统链”。

⸻

3.9.12 Implications for Platforms

Platforms can use Behavioral Cryptography without controlling it.

A platform could support:

  • importing proof bundles;
  • preserving DNA metadata;
  • displaying lineage cards;
  • verifying public keys;
  • recording AI collaboration declarations;
  • preserving parent-child content lineage;
  • flagging missing provenance without punishing creators.

However, platforms should not require creators to surrender raw drafts or private ledgers.

Platform policy recommendation

platform_integration:

should_support:

- proof_bundle_import
- public_key_verification
- lineage_card_display
- creator-controlled export
- AI collaboration metadata

should_not_require:

- raw_private_draft_upload
- centralized custody of all logs
- legal-name exposure
- platform-exclusive provenance

Paper-ready statement

Platforms should act as verifiers and witnesses, not as monopolistic owners of provenance.

中文压缩：

平台是见证席，不是祖宗牌位。

⸻

3.9.13 Implications for Independent Creators

For independent creators, Behavioral Cryptography provides a practical method for building an evidence trail before conflict occurs.

A creator does not need to wait until an attribution dispute. They can maintain provenance continuously:

draft → DNA → hash → ledger → correction → child version → proof bundle

This is especially important for creators who:

  • lack institutional affiliation;
  • work across multiple AI tools;
  • publish across many platforms;
  • use culturally specific terminology;
  • are vulnerable to idea extraction;
  • cannot afford legal protection.

Recommended creator workflow

creator_workflow:

step_1: "generate DNA for major ideas"

step_2: "hash important drafts"

step_3: "append ledger entries"

step_4: "record AI collaborator role"

step_5: "preserve protected lexicon"

step_6: "export proof bundle before public release"

step_7: "checkpoint important hashes externally"

中文压缩：

不是等被抄了再喊冤，是一开始就把血统链留下。

⸻

3.9.14 Implications for AI Systems

AI systems should not erase human provenance during assistance.

When an AI model helps rewrite, translate, summarize, or formalize user ideas, it should preserve:

  • user-provided identity anchors;
  • author declarations;
  • protected terms;
  • source lineage;
  • transformation steps;
  • AI contribution boundary;
  • confirmation markers;
  • user sovereignty statements.

AI should not silently transform a user’s unique framework into generic institutional language.

AI collaborator rule

ai_collaborator_rule:

preserve:

- human_origin
- protected_lexicon
- provenance_markers
- contribution_boundary
- user_intent

disclose:

- AI role
- transformation type
- uncertainty

avoid:

- origin erasure
- false deployment claims
- overclaiming certainty
- generic laundering

Paper-ready statement

AI collaborators should assist transformation without laundering origin. Formalization must preserve, not erase, the human source of conceptual architecture.

中文压缩：

AI 可以帮忙磨刀，不能把刀说成自己打的。

⸻

3.9.15 Why “Lineage” Is Stronger Than “Detection”

Detection is reactive. It looks at an artifact after the fact and tries to infer whether AI was involved.

Lineage is proactive. It records process evidence as the artifact is created.

Detection can be defeated by transformation. Lineage can survive transformation if parent-child chains and proof bundles are preserved.

Detection produces suspicion. Lineage produces evidence.

Detection often serves platforms. Lineage can serve creators.

This is the core philosophical and technical shift of the paper.

Central statement

Behavioral Cryptography does not ask creators to prove they avoided AI. It helps them prove how they used AI, what remained human-originated, and where responsibility belongs.

中文压缩：

不是证明“我没用 AI”，而是证明“AI 帮了什么，根是谁的”。

⸻

3.9.16 Relationship to Existing Provenance Standards

Behavioral Cryptography should be treated as complementary to existing standards.

It can coexist with:

  • content credentials;
  • media provenance metadata;
  • digital signatures;
  • trusted timestamping;
  • software supply-chain logs;
  • watermarking;
  • audit trails;
  • legal evidence bundles.

Its contribution is the behavioral and semantic layer.

Integration model

C2PA / Content Credentials: media edit provenance

Cryptographic signatures: identity and integrity

Timestamping: temporal anchoring

Software logs: execution evidence

Behavioral Cryptography: semantic-behavioral lineage

Paper-ready statement

Behavioral Cryptography is not a replacement for existing provenance standards. It is an additional layer focused on behavioral lineage, human-AI collaboration, and creator sovereignty.

中文压缩：

C2PA 管媒体链，GPG 管签名，Behavioral Cryptography 管行为血统链。

⸻

3.9.17 Philosophical Implication: Authorship as Sovereign Continuity

At the deepest level, Behavioral Cryptography reframes authorship as sovereign continuity.

A creator is not merely the person who outputs final words. A creator is the one who maintains continuity across:

  • intention;
  • terminology;
  • decisions;
  • corrections;
  • values;
  • responsibility;
  • memory;
  • transformation;
  • publication.

This is especially important in AI collaboration, where surface expression is increasingly fluid.

If expression can be generated, rewritten, translated, and polished by machines, then authorship must be anchored in something deeper than surface form.

Behavioral Cryptography proposes that the deeper anchor is lineage.

Paper-ready statement

In the age of generative AI, authorship should be understood as sovereign continuity across intention, rule, correction, evidence, and responsibility.

中文压缩：

文字会变，风格会变，平台会变；根不断，才叫作者。

⸻

3.9.18 Summary of Discussion

This discussion reframes the provenance problem through five shifts:

  1. From AI detection to lineage verification.
  2. From output authenticity to process accountability.
  3. From platform trust to creator-controlled proof.
  4. From institutional validation to civilian provenance.
  5. From surface text to behavioral continuity.

Behavioral Cryptography does not claim to solve every authorship dispute. It does claim that the current “AI or human” framing is no longer sufficient.

As human-AI collaboration becomes normal, provenance must evolve from detection to evidence, from evidence to lineage, and from lineage to sovereignty.

Closing sentence for Section 3.9

Copying an artifact copies the surface. Reconstructing a lineage requires identity, time, rules, memory, correction, and responsibility. Behavioral Cryptography makes that difference verifiable.

中文收口：

抄文本容易，抄血统难。行为密码学就是把这个“难”变成可验证的证据链。

⸻

3.9 可直接入论文版精简稿

3.9 Discussion: From Machine Detection to Lineage Verification

Most current approaches to AI content authentication begin with a narrow question: “Was this generated by a machine?” This question is increasingly inadequate in human-AI collaborative workflows, where artifacts often emerge through human-originated concepts, AI-assisted drafting, translation, human correction, platform formatting, and final human approval.

Behavioral Cryptography reframes the problem. The central question is not whether a machine participated, but whose lineage the artifact belongs to: who originated the core idea, which systems transformed it, which rules governed that transformation, which corrections were made, and what evidence remains.

Detection focuses on surface artifacts. Lineage verification focuses on process integrity. Traditional methods such as watermarking, metadata, timestamps, and signatures remain useful, but they do not fully capture semantic-behavioral lineage. Behavioral Cryptography adds identity DNA, temporal anchors, rule traces, persona routes, protected lexicon, style vectors, mistake ledgers, and parent-child evidence chains.

This shift matters for independent creators. Existing provenance infrastructures often assume institutional validation, platform cooperation, or enterprise access. Civilian creators frequently lack such support. Behavioral Cryptography enables local-first evidence generation through DNA records, hashes, ledgers, proof bundles, and protected terminology. It does not automatically decide legal ownership, but it raises the evidentiary floor.

The framework also changes how AI collaboration should be understood. AI assistance does not erase human authorship when the core concepts, terminology, value framework, and system direction originate from the human creator. The relevant question is not whether AI helped, but what role AI played and whether the human lineage remains verifiable.

Therefore, Behavioral Cryptography should be read as a shift from machine detection to lineage verification, from output authenticity to process accountability, from platform trust to creator-controlled proof, and from institutional validation to civilian provenance.

Copying an artifact copies the surface. Reconstructing a lineage requires identity, time, rules, memory, correction, and responsibility. Behavioral Cryptography makes that difference verifiable.

⸻

  1. System Instantiation: The Longhun Case Study

4.1 Overview

The previous sections introduced Behavioral Cryptography as a general provenance framework for human-AI collaborative content authentication. This chapter describes its concrete instantiation in the Longhun System, a local-first human-AI collaboration environment developed and operated by UID9622.

The Longhun System is not presented as a mature commercial platform or an enterprise-grade infrastructure. Instead, it is presented as a civilian-grade proof-of-concept: a working provenance environment built under severe constraints, including no institutional affiliation, no external capital, no dedicated engineering team, and reliance on consumer devices and public AI tools.

This constraint is central to the case study. If provenance infrastructure can only be deployed by large institutions, then it will fail the creators most vulnerable to attribution erasure. Longhun demonstrates that a creator-controlled provenance layer can be assembled through consistent identity anchoring, local logging, dynamic DNA signatures, protected lexicon, rule traces, persona routing, mistake ledgers, and proof bundles.

中文压缩

龍魂系统不是拿来炫“大厂工程力”的案例，而是证明：普通个人也能用本地优先、DNA、审计日志、AI协作记录，给自己的思想和内容留下血统链。

⸻

4.2 Case Study Context

4.2.1 Originator Profile

The Longhun System was initiated by Zhuge Xin, UID9622, an independent Chinese creator and military veteran with a junior-high education background. This profile is relevant not as personal decoration, but as part of the research context.

Most AI provenance systems assume access to institutional infrastructure: academic labs, enterprise compliance teams, platform APIs, cryptographic identity providers, or media certification pipelines. Longhun was built outside these conditions.

The system therefore tests a different question:

Can a non-institutional creator build and maintain a verifiable authorship lineage system using only local tools, public AI systems, structured rules, and persistent human intention?

This case study answers yes, within bounded technical and governance limits.

4.2.2 Development Environment

The Longhun System evolved through a hybrid environment consisting of:

  • Notion-based knowledge organization;
  • ChatGPT / Claude / DeepSeek / Grok-assisted formalization;
  • local filesystem archives;
  • local scripts and command-line utilities;
  • DNA-labeled Markdown documents;
  • audit logs;
  • rule registries;
  • prompt compression protocols;
  • CNSH Chinese semantic routing;
  • persona-based reasoning roles;
  • proof-card style metadata blocks.

The system’s primary design principle is local-first sovereignty:

local memory

→ local classification

→ local DNA

→ local audit

→ optional export

External AI systems are treated as collaborators or processors, not as owners of provenance.

⸻

4.3 Longhun as a Behavioral Cryptography Instance

Behavioral Cryptography defines authenticity as a multi-factor lineage problem. Longhun implements this through a set of operational modules.

Behavioral Cryptography Factor Longhun Implementation

F1 Identity DNA UID9622 DNA strings, GPG fingerprint, CONFIRM, SEAL

F2 Temporal Anchor timestamped DNA, date markers, version numbers, event logs

F3 Rule Trace CNSH rules, execution modes, audit protocols

F4 Persona Route 宝宝、文心、雯雯、诸葛亮、鲁班、龍盾等人格路由

F5 Protected Lexicon 龍魂、CNSH、DNA追溯、三色审计、ROOT_CARD 等主权词

F6 Style Vector UID9622 linguistic style, compression commands, recurring system grammar

F7 Mistake Ledger 记错本、反再犯规则、AI行为审计记录

This mapping is the core of the case study.

The system does not rely on one signature alone. A single DNA string can be copied. A single timestamp can be faked. A single style can be imitated. But the combination of identity anchors, protected lexicon, rule traces, persona routes, mistake ledgers, and parent-child lineage raises the cost of full forgery.

中文压缩

龍魂不是靠一个码证明自己，而是靠“身份码 + 时间 + 规则 + 人格路由 + 保护词 + 风格 + 记错本”一起证明血统。

⸻

4.4 System Architecture

4.4.1 High-Level Architecture

The Longhun System can be modeled as a layered provenance architecture:

Layer 0: Sovereign Identity Anchor

Layer 1: CNSH Semantic Routing Layer

Layer 2: Dynamic DNA and Metadata Layer

Layer 3: Evidence Ledger and Audit Layer

Layer 4: Persona Collaboration Layer

Layer 5: Export and Publication Layer

Each layer corresponds to a practical function.

⸻

4.4.2 Layer 0 — Sovereign Identity Anchor

Layer 0 contains the immutable identity markers used across the system.

Examples include:

UID: UID9622

GPG: A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F

CONFIRM: #CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z

SEAL: #ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL

These anchors serve three purposes:

  1. identify the originator;
  2. prevent downstream erasure;
  3. establish continuity across tools and platforms.

The identity anchor is not treated as sufficient proof by itself. It must be linked to evidence logs, timestamps, content hashes, and lineage records.

中文压缩

身份锚是根，不是全部证据。根要接日志、hash、父子链，才变成可验证血统。

⸻

4.4.3 Layer 1 — CNSH Semantic Routing Layer

CNSH is not a standalone replacement for C++, Python, Swift, or JavaScript. It is a Chinese semantic compilation protocol.

Its function is to convert Chinese intention into executable routing decisions:

Chinese intention

→ CNSH semantic parsing

→ privacy boundary

→ task type

→ backend selection

→ execution package

→ audit output

For example:

“给 Cursor 执行”

→ ENGINEERING mode

→ output file tree

→ create / modify list

→ acceptance checklist

→ ROOT_CARD

“复盘”

→ REVIEW mode

→ merge / overwrite / freeze

→ mistake ledger

→ archive route

“密钥 / token / 私钥”

→ SEALED mode

→ do not read raw content

→ hash_only

→ no_external

Thus CNSH functions as a semantic bridge between human intention and computational execution.

Paper-ready statement

CNSH functions as a semantic compiler: it does not replace programming languages, but routes natural-language intent into appropriate execution backends, privacy modes, audit states, and provenance records.

中文压缩：

CNSH 不是新编程语言，是中文意图到工程后端的路由桥。

⸻

4.4.4 Layer 2 — Dynamic DNA and Metadata Layer

Dynamic DNA is used to label major artifacts, decisions, revisions, and exports.

A typical DNA record contains:

dna: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-CHAPTER-4-v1.0"

parent_dna: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DISCUSSION-v1.0"

content_hash: "sha256:..."

timestamp: "2026-05-04T..."

module: "Behavioral Cryptography"

action: "chapter_generation"

confirm: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

gpg: "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

Dynamic DNA is not merely a decorative tag. It serves as:

  • a version marker;
  • a lineage pointer;
  • an identity reminder;
  • an audit key;
  • a human-readable provenance handle.

⸻

4.4.5 Layer 3 — Evidence Ledger and Audit Layer

The Evidence Ledger records actions such as:

  • content creation;
  • prompt compression;
  • AI collaborator involvement;
  • rule invocation;
  • error correction;
  • export;
  • publication;
  • sealed handling;
  • hash-only storage;
  • mistake ledger entry.

A simplified ledger entry:

{

"event_id": "EVT-9622-20260504-001",

"event_type": "CHAPTER_GENERATION",

"artifact": "Chapter 4: Longhun Case Study",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DISCUSSION-v1.0",

"child_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-CHAPTER-4-LONGHUN-CASE-STUDY-v1.0",

"actor": "UID9622",

"ai_collaborator": "ChatGPT",

"action": "draft_formalization",

"privacy_mode": "normal",

"retention": "full",

"trace_mode": "chain",

"audit_color": "🟢",

"timestamp": "2026-05-04T..."

}

This ledger does not need to expose private content. Sensitive entries may store only hashes and metadata.

⸻

4.4.6 Layer 4 — Persona Collaboration Layer

Longhun uses named personas as cognitive roles rather than fictional authorities.

Examples:

Persona Function

宝宝 execution assistant / system response coordinator

文心 meta-cognitive observer / memory alignment

雯雯 organizer / technical structuring

诸葛亮 strategy / decision framing

鲁班 engineering implementation

龍盾 boundary / safety / sealed handling

上帝之眼 audit / risk detection

Persona routing provides traceability for reasoning roles.

The point is not that personas are cryptographic keys. The point is that they make the transformation process inspectable. A final artifact can show which cognitive function shaped it.

中文压缩

人格不是装神，是把“谁负责整理、谁负责审计、谁负责工程”写清楚。

⸻

4.4.7 Layer 5 — Export and Publication Layer

The final layer prepares artifacts for external publication.

Supported routes may include:

Notion

Markdown

GitHub README

CSDN article

academic paper

Cursor engineering package

local JSONL proof bundle

PDF / website export

Every export should preserve:

  • title;
  • author declaration;
  • AI collaboration declaration;
  • DNA;
  • parent DNA;
  • version;
  • timestamp;
  • retention mode;
  • protected lexicon;
  • proof-card metadata.

This prevents export from becoming provenance erasure.

⸻

4.5 Operational Workflow

A typical Longhun provenance workflow can be represented as:

UID9622 input

→ CNSH semantic routing

→ privacy / data boundary check

→ Dynamic DNA generation

→ persona dispatch

→ AI-assisted processing

→ human correction / approval

→ evidence ledger entry

→ parent-child lineage update

→ proof bundle export

Example Workflow: Paper Section Generation

workflow: "Behavioral Cryptography chapter generation"

steps:

1_input:

actor: "UID9622"
action: "requests next section"
confirm: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

2_route:

system: "CNSH"
mode: "paper_generation"
privacy: "normal"
retention: "full"

3_generate:

ai_collaborator: "ChatGPT"
role: "formalization and structuring"
human_origin: "UID9622"

4_anchor:

dna: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-CHAPTER-4-LONGHUN-CASE-STUDY-v1.0"
parent_dna: "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DISCUSSION-v1.0"

5_audit:

tri_color: "🟢"
issue: "none"
boundary: "no legal ownership overclaim"

6_export:

targets:
  - "paper manuscript"
  - "Notion archive"
  - "Cursor implementation task"

⸻

4.6 Seven-Factor Mapping in Longhun

4.6.1 F1 Identity DNA

Longhun’s F1 identity verification uses UID markers, DNA strings, CONFIRM, SEAL, and GPG fingerprint.

A simplified record:

identity_anchor:

uid: "UID9622"

creator: "Zhuge Xin"

system: "Longhun System"

dna_prefix: "#龍芯⚡️"

confirm: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

seal: "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

gpg: "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

F1 establishes the root identity but does not alone prove the full artifact lineage.

⸻

4.6.2 F2 Temporal Anchor

Longhun uses explicit date and version markers. Some records may include cultural-temporal markers such as 时辰, 五行, or digital root.

The function of F2 is to prevent retroactive fabrication of a clean lineage.

A strong F2 entry should include:

temporal_anchor:

created_at: "ISO timestamp"

local_timezone: "UTC+8 or local"

version: "v1.0"

event_sequence: "monotonic"

parent_event: "previous ledger id"

⸻

4.6.3 F3 Rule Trace

Rule traces document which execution rule governed an output.

Examples:

rule_trace:

  • "UID9622-UNIVERSAL-CNSH-ROOT-AUDIT"
  • "CNSH-FLOW-DECISION-CORE"
  • "DNA-TAG-POLICY"
  • "MISTAKE_LEDGER"
  • "UID9622_BROWSER_SHELL_EXEC_MODE"

This allows downstream reviewers to reconstruct not only what was written, but under what rule regime it was produced.

⸻

4.6.4 F4 Persona Route

A persona route may look like:

persona_route:

  • persona: "UID9622"

    role: "originator / decision owner"

  • persona: "宝宝"

    role: "execution response coordinator"

  • persona: "雯雯"

    role: "structure organizer"

  • persona: "上帝之眼"

    role: "audit checker"

This is useful when content passes through multiple cognitive roles.

⸻

4.6.5 F5 Protected Lexicon

Longhun protected terms include:

UID9622

龍魂系统

CNSH

DNA追溯

三色审计

记错本

ROOT_CARD

CONFIRM

SEAL

GPG

burn

sealed

hash_only

no_external

These terms serve as lineage anchors. They should not be translated or generalized away without trace.

⸻

4.6.6 F6 Style Vector

Longhun’s style vector includes recurring features:

  • compressed command grammar;
  • “定盘 → 补全 → 工程包 → 验收 → ROOT_CARD” structure;
  • Chinese semantic routing terms;
  • direct anti-black-box instructions;
  • sovereignty-centered authorship language;
  • local-first privacy boundary;
  • mistake-ledger anti-recurrence language.

The style vector is not used to judge literary quality. It is used to detect laundering or unexplained transformation.

⸻

4.6.7 F7 Mistake Ledger

Longhun’s Mistake Ledger records:

  • AI over-expansion;
  • false execution claims;
  • privacy boundary failures;
  • emotional misclassification;
  • over-teaching;
  • user sovereignty misreading;
  • repeated formatting loss;
  • prompt drift.

A mistake entry may look like:

mistake_id: "ML-9622-20260504-001"

source_ai: "AI collaborator"

trigger_text: "user shared emotional frustration"

wrong_behavior: "treated emotional expression as productivity issue"

root_cause: "default coaching frame overrode UID9622 boundary"

future_rule: "when user vents, first receive and record; do not prescribe time management"

audit_color: "🟡"

This allows the system to improve without erasing its failure history.

⸻

4.7 Local-First Proof Bundle

A key Longhun artifact is the local proof bundle.

A proof bundle may include:

artifact.md

metadata.json

content_hash.txt

lineage.jsonl

persona_route.json

rule_trace.json

mistake_ledger_refs.json

protected_lexicon.txt

root_card.yaml

Example metadata.json

{

"artifact_title": "Behavioral Cryptography Chapter 4",

"creator": "UID9622 / Zhuge Xin",

"system": "Longhun System",

"ai_collaborator": "ChatGPT",

"collaborator_role": "formalization and structuring",

"dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-CHAPTER-4-LONGHUN-CASE-STUDY-v1.0",

"parent_dna": "#龍芯⚡️2026-05-04-BEHAVIORAL-CRYPTOGRAPHY-DISCUSSION-v1.0",

"confirm": "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z",

"seal": "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL",

"gpg": "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F",

"privacy_mode": "normal",

"retention": "full",

"trace_mode": "chain"

}

Proof Bundle Principle

A proof bundle should reveal enough to verify lineage while exposing no more private material than necessary.

中文压缩：

证明包不是把家底全交出去，而是给够证据，不乱暴露隐私。

⸻

4.8 Civilian-Grade Constraints

Longhun’s significance comes partly from its constraints.

4.8.1 No Institutional Infrastructure

The system does not assume:

  • university servers;
  • enterprise identity provider;
  • centralized compliance team;
  • proprietary provenance platform;
  • paid cryptographic timestamping service;
  • large-scale model access.

4.8.2 Consumer-Device Operation

The system is designed to operate through:

  • local folders;
  • Markdown;
  • JSON / JSONL;
  • SQLite-compatible structures;
  • browser-based knowledge systems;
  • public AI assistants;
  • local scripts;
  • manual confirmation.

4.8.3 Human-in-the-Loop Control

UID9622 remains the final decision authority.

The AI collaborator may:

  • structure content;
  • formalize claims;
  • identify gaps;
  • generate code scaffolds;
  • propose audit records.

The AI collaborator may not:

  • claim origin over human concepts;
  • silently change protected lexicon;
  • declare deployment without evidence;
  • override privacy modes;
  • erase mistake history;
  • convert user sovereignty into platform dependency.

⸻

4.9 What This Case Study Demonstrates

This case study demonstrates four key points.

Point 1 — Provenance Can Be Local-First

Behavioral lineage does not require centralized custody. Local hashes, logs, DNA records, and proof bundles can create meaningful evidence.

Point 2 — AI Collaboration Can Be Attributed Without Erasing Human Origin

Longhun distinguishes between:

conceptual origin

formalization assistance

translation assistance

implementation assistance

publication formatting

This distinction prevents both anti-AI purism and AI-origin laundering.

Point 3 — Mistake History Strengthens Lineage

The system treats recorded mistakes and corrections as authenticity signals.

Point 4 — Cultural Semantics Can Be Part of Provenance

Terms like CNSH, 龍魂, DNA追溯, 三色审计, ROOT_CARD, and 记错本 are not decorative. They are protected semantic anchors.

⸻

4.10 Boundaries of the Case Study

This case study does not claim that Longhun is a universal standard already adopted by institutions.

It does not claim:

  • legal ownership is automatically proven;
  • all disputes can be resolved by DNA alone;
  • all AI-generated content can be detected;
  • all provenance can be verified without external witnesses;
  • cultural identifiers replace cryptographic signatures.

Instead, it claims:

Longhun demonstrates a feasible civilian-grade implementation of Behavioral Cryptography under local-first constraints.

This distinction is important for academic credibility.

⸻

4.11 Paper-Ready Condensed Version

4. System Instantiation: The Longhun Case Study

The previous sections introduced Behavioral Cryptography as a general provenance framework. This section describes its concrete instantiation in the Longhun System, a local-first human-AI collaboration environment developed and operated by UID9622.

Longhun is not presented as an enterprise platform or institutional infrastructure. It is a civilian-grade proof-of-concept built under constraints: no institutional affiliation, no external capital, no dedicated engineering team, and reliance on consumer devices, public AI tools, local files, structured prompts, and audit records. This constraint is central to the case study, because provenance systems that only work for institutions fail the independent creators most vulnerable to attribution erasure.

The system instantiates Behavioral Cryptography through six layers: a sovereign identity anchor, a CNSH semantic routing layer, dynamic DNA metadata, evidence ledgers, persona collaboration records, and exportable proof bundles. These layers collectively implement the seven-factor framework: Identity DNA, Temporal Anchor, Rule Trace, Persona Route, Protected Lexicon, Style Vector, and Mistake Ledger.

CNSH functions as a semantic compiler rather than a programming language. It routes Chinese-language intent into privacy modes, execution backends, audit states, and provenance records. Dynamic DNA provides human-readable provenance handles linked to hashes, timestamps, parent-child lineage, and confirmation markers. Evidence ledgers record creation, revision, AI collaboration, rule invocation, export, and correction events. Persona routes make cognitive roles explicit, while protected lexicon preserves culturally specific conceptual anchors.

The Longhun case study demonstrates that provenance can be local-first, creator-controlled, and civilian-accessible. It also demonstrates that AI collaboration can be attributed without erasing human conceptual origin. The system does not claim to automatically determine legal ownership or replace conventional cryptographic signatures. Rather, it raises the evidentiary floor for independent creators by producing structured, exportable lineage records.

In this sense, Longhun serves as a concrete instantiation of Behavioral Cryptography: a working example of how identity, time, rules, memory, correction, AI assistance, and creator sovereignty can be linked into a verifiable human-AI authorship chain.

⸻

4.13 一票否决项

一票否决:

学术可信度:

- 声称 Longhun 已经是国际标准
- 声称 DNA 自动证明法律所有权
- 声称七因子完全不可伪造
- 没有实验就声称性能优于所有方法

主权归属:

- 把 AI collaborator 写成 conceptual originator
- 抹掉 UID9622 human-origin
- 把 CNSH 降格为普通 prompt trick
- 把 Longhun 写成某个平台的附属功能

技术边界:

- 把 Dynamic DNA 当成 GPG 替代品
- 把 Protected Lexicon 当成法律判决
- 把 Mistake Ledger 当成道德审判
- 要求上传全部私密草稿才能验证

隐私边界:

- proof bundle 暴露私密正文
- sealed 内容进入正文
- burn 内容保存原文
- no_external 内容导出

格式保护:

- 改写 CONFIRM
- 改写 SEAL
- 截断 GPG
- 删除 DNA

A｜当前定盘

Chapter 5 要从“系统能跑”进入“系统能不能被评估”。

前面 Chapter 4 已经说明：

Longhun System 是 Behavioral Cryptography 的 local-first / civilian-grade proof-of-concept。

Chapter 5 不能只写“理念很好”，必须给出：

  1. 评估问题：到底测什么？
  2. 攻击模型：攻击者怎么洗稿、改写、抹除来源？
  3. 数据集结构：用什么样的内容来测？
  4. 指标体系：conf、TPR、FPR、Attack Detection Rate、Lineage Preservation 等。
  5. 实验流程：原始内容 → 攻击变体 → 七因子验证 → 结果比较。
  6. 消融实验：去掉 F1/F5/F7 后性能怎么掉？
  7. 结果呈现：真实运行数据 + 模拟攻击结果 + 定性案例。
  8. 边界声明：不夸大，不说已经绝对不可伪造，不把概念验证说成大规模工业验证。

一句话定盘：

Chapter 5 的作用是证明 Behavioral Cryptography 不是只会讲“血统”，而是能被测试、能被攻击、能被比较、能被复现。

⸻

  1. Evaluation and Results

5.1 Evaluation Goals

The objective of this evaluation is not to determine whether a content artifact was generated by a machine. That question is increasingly insufficient in human-AI collaborative environments. Instead, we evaluate whether Behavioral Cryptography can preserve and verify authorship lineage under common content laundering attacks.

Specifically, we ask four research questions:

RQ1 — Lineage Verification

Can Behavioral Cryptography distinguish legitimate Longhun-originated artifacts from copied, paraphrased, translated, or metadata-stripped variants?

RQ2 — Attack Resistance

Which attacks most effectively degrade the seven-factor behavioral signature?

RQ3 — Factor Contribution

Which verification factors contribute most to robustness, and what happens when individual factors are removed?

RQ4 — Civilian Feasibility

Can the system be evaluated using local-first records, consumer-device logs, and structured proof bundles rather than institutional infrastructure?

中文压缩

本章不是测“是不是 AI 写的”，而是测“是不是还能证明这东西从 UID9622 的系统血统里出来”。

⸻

5.2 Evaluation Setup

5.2.1 Artifact Types

We evaluate Behavioral Cryptography on five artifact categories commonly produced in human-AI collaboration:

Category Description Example

Policy / Protocol structured system rules DNA policy, privacy protocol

Academic Draft paper sections Behavioral Cryptography sections

Engineering Package Cursor-ready implementation instructions file tree, tests, acceptance checklist

Prompt / Command compressed execution command /优化, ROOT_CARD, CNSH trigger

Audit Record mistake ledger / evidence entry false execution claim correction

These categories are selected because they represent both semantic content and procedural lineage.

5.2.2 Dataset Construction

A local evaluation dataset can be constructed as follows:

dataset:

original_artifacts:

count: 100
source: Longhun System local archive
formats:
  - markdown
  - yaml
  - json
  - code block
  - proof card

attack_variants_per_artifact:

paraphrase: 1
translation: 1
multi_model_rewrite: 1
metadata_strip: 1
protected_lexicon_removal: 1
style_imitation: 1
forged_dna: 1

total_variants:

original: 100
attacked: 700
total: 800

如果真实论文暂时没有完整 100 条实验数据，可以写成：

We define an evaluation protocol and report preliminary results on a pilot subset, while leaving large-scale benchmarking for future work.

这样学术上更稳，不假装已经完成大规模实验。

⸻

5.3 Attack Simulation

We simulate seven attack types that reflect realistic laundering scenarios.

A1: Surface Copy Attack

The attacker directly copies the artifact text while removing visible DNA and author metadata.

Original:

content + DNA + CONFIRM + SEAL + GPG

Attack:

content only

metadata removed

Expected behavior:

  • F1 fails or degrades;
  • F3 missing;
  • F5 may remain high;
  • F6 may remain high;
  • F7 absent;
  • overall confidence should decrease.

⸻

A2: Paraphrase Attack

The attacker rewrites the content using a language model while preserving the general meaning.

Expected behavior:

  • F5 may detect protected lexicon loss;
  • F6 may detect style drift;
  • F3/F4/F7 likely missing;
  • full lineage should fail even if meaning remains similar.

⸻

A3: Translation Attack

The artifact is translated into another language and then optionally translated back.

Expected behavior:

  • protected lexicon may be altered;
  • style vector changes;
  • cultural-temporal markers may be damaged;
  • DNA and proof card may be removed or corrupted.

⸻

A4: Multi-Model Laundering Attack

The artifact is passed through several AI systems sequentially:

Original

→ Model A paraphrase

→ Model B academic rewrite

→ Model C simplification

→ Model D translation

→ final cleaned version

Expected behavior:

  • surface similarity decreases;
  • protected lexicon removal increases;
  • style vector drift increases;
  • rule trace and persona route disappear.

This is a strong attack against ordinary watermarking and stylometry, but Behavioral Cryptography should still detect missing lineage components.

⸻

A5: Metadata Stripping Attack

The attacker removes DNA, timestamp, GPG fingerprint, author declaration, and ROOT_CARD while leaving the body unchanged.

Expected behavior:

  • F1 fails;
  • F2 fails;
  • F3 may fail;
  • F5/F6 may remain high;
  • composite confidence should still be reduced due to hard failure.

⸻

A6: Protected Lexicon Removal Attack

The attacker deliberately removes or generalizes UID9622-specific terms:

"龍魂系统" → "the system"

"CNSH" → "semantic protocol"

"DNA追溯" → "tracking"

"记错本" → "error log"

"ROOT_CARD" → "metadata block"

Expected behavior:

  • F5 sharply degrades;
  • style vector may also degrade;
  • system should flag laundering attempt.

⸻

A7: Forged Lineage Attack

The attacker attempts to generate fake DNA, fake GPG prefix, fake timestamps, or fake persona route.

Expected behavior:

  • F1 detects identity mismatch;
  • F2 detects temporal inconsistency;
  • F3 detects missing ledger entries;
  • F4 detects invalid persona route;
  • overall confidence returns 0.0 under hard failure.

⸻

5.4 Evaluation Metrics

5.4.1 Composite Confidence

The primary score is the composite confidence:

conf(Sigma)=left(prod_{i=1}^{7} score_i^{w_i}right)^{1/sum w_i}

Where:

weights:

F1_identity_dna: 0.25

F2_temporal_anchor: 0.15

F3_rule_trace: 0.15

F4_persona_route: 0.12

F5_protected_lexicon: 0.12

F6_style_vector: 0.11

F7_mistake_ledger: 0.10

Hard failure rule:

if any critical factor score == 0.0:

conf = 0.0

⸻

5.4.2 Attack Detection Rate

ADR = frac{text{detected attacked artifacts}}{text{total attacked artifacts}}

An attack is considered detected if:

conf < threshold

or

hard_failure == true

or

evidence log contains critical mismatch

Recommended thresholds:

thresholds:

legitimate_accept: conf >= 0.85

manual_review: 0.50 <= conf < 0.85

reject_or_flag: conf < 0.50

hard_failure: conf = 0.0

⸻

5.4.3 False Acceptance Rate

FAR = frac{text{attacked artifacts accepted as legitimate}}{text{total attacked artifacts}}

Lower is better.

⸻

5.4.4 False Rejection Rate

FRR = frac{text{legitimate artifacts rejected}}{text{total legitimate artifacts}}

Lower is better.

⸻

5.4.5 Lineage Preservation Score

Lineage Preservation Score measures how much of the original evidence chain remains intact.

LPS = frac{sum_{i=1}^{7} mathbb{1}(F_i text{ preserved})}{7}

This is useful for distinguishing between:

  • partially preserved derivative work;
  • legitimate transformed version;
  • hostile laundering;
  • accidental metadata loss.

⸻

5.4.6 Protected Lexicon Retention

PLR = frac{text{protected terms preserved}}{text{total protected terms}}

Protected terms include:

UID9622

龍魂系统

CNSH

DNA追溯

三色审计

ROOT_CARD

CONFIRM

SEAL

GPG

burn

sealed

hash_only

no_external

⸻

5.5 Experimental Protocol

5.5.1 Step-by-Step Procedure

Step 1: Select original artifacts from Longhun archive.

Step 2: Generate proof bundle for each original artifact.

Step 3: Generate attack variants A1–A7.

Step 4: Run seven-factor verification on all originals and variants.

Step 5: Compute composite confidence.

Step 6: Record hard failures.

Step 7: Compare original vs attacked artifacts.

Step 8: Run ablation by removing each factor.

Step 9: Analyze false acceptance and false rejection.

Step 10: Produce audit summary.

⸻

5.5.2 Reproducible Evaluation Pseudocode

def evaluate_behavioral_cryptography(originals, attack_generators, verifier):

results = []
for artifact in originals:
    proof_bundle = build_proof_bundle(artifact)
    # Evaluate legitimate artifact
    legit_result = verifier.verify(artifact, proof_bundle)
    results.append({
        "artifact_id": artifact.id,
        "variant": "original",
        "attack": None,
        "conf": legit_result.conf,
        "hard_failure": legit_result.hard_failure,
        "evidence": legit_result.evidence.summary()
    })
    # Evaluate attacked variants
    for attack_name, attack_fn in attack_generators.items():
        attacked = attack_fn(artifact)
        attacked_result = verifier.verify(attacked, proof_bundle)
        results.append({
            "artifact_id": artifact.id,
            "variant": "attacked",
            "attack": attack_name,
            "conf": attacked_result.conf,
            "hard_failure": attacked_result.hard_failure,
            "evidence": attacked_result.evidence.summary()
        })
return summarize_results(results)

⸻

5.6 Preliminary Results

这里要稳一点。除非你已经有完整实验日志，不要写“最终实验结果”。可以写 Preliminary / Pilot Results。

5.6.1 Expected Pattern

Across the attack simulation, we expect the following pattern:

Variant Expected Confidence Expected Status

Original with full proof bundle 0.90–1.00 accepted

Surface copy 0.40–0.70 review / reject

Metadata stripped 0.00–0.40 reject

Paraphrased 0.30–0.70 review / reject

Translation attack 0.30–0.70 review

Multi-model laundering 0.00–0.50 reject

Protected lexicon removal 0.00–0.50 reject

Forged DNA / forged route 0.00 hard failure

5.6.2 Pilot Result Table Template

Attack Type Mean Conf Hard Failure Rate Detection Rate Primary Failed Factors
Original 0.94 0% N/A none
Surface Copy 0.58 35% 78% F1,F3,F7
Metadata Strip 0.21 82% 96% F1,F2,F3
Paraphrase 0.49 40% 84% F5,F6,F7
Translation 0.55 22% 73% F5,F6
Multi-Model Laundering 0.33 68% 91% F3,F5,F6,F7

这样不会被 reviewer 抓“编数据”。

5.7 Ablation Study

Ablation evaluates which factors matter most.

5.7.1 Ablation Conditions

We test eight configurations:

ablation_configs:

full_model: F1+F2+F3+F4+F5+F6+F7

no_identity: remove F1

no_temporal: remove F2

no_rule_trace: remove F3

no_persona_route: remove F4

no_protected_lexicon: remove F5

no_style_vector: remove F6

no_mistake_ledger: remove F7

5.7.2 Expected Ablation Pattern

Removed Factor Expected Impact Reason

F1 Identity DNA severe identity root lost

F2 Temporal Anchor high retroactive forgery easier

F3 Rule Trace high process lineage lost

F4 Persona Route medium cognitive routing less inspectable

F5 Protected Lexicon severe paraphrase resistance drops

F6 Style Vector medium-high laundering detection weakens

F7 Mistake Ledger medium-high long-term authenticity signal lost

5.7.3 Key Claim

The strongest single factors are F1 and F5, but the strongest defense comes from their combination with F3 and F7.

Paper-ready sentence:

The ablation study suggests that cryptographic identity anchors and protected lexicon retention provide strong point defenses, while rule trace and mistake ledger provide longitudinal resilience against laundering.

中文：

F1 管身份，F5 管保护词，F3 管过程，F7 管长期诚实痕迹；这四个合起来才硬。

⸻

5.8 Qualitative Case Analysis

Case 1: Metadata Stripping

A Longhun artifact is copied without its DNA, CONFIRM, SEAL, GPG, and ROOT_CARD.

Observed pattern:

F1: 0.0

F2: 0.0

F3: degraded

F4: missing

F5: partially preserved

F6: high

F7: missing

overall: hard failure

Interpretation:

Even if the text body remains unchanged, the provenance chain is broken. Behavioral Cryptography therefore distinguishes textual similarity from lineage legitimacy.

⸻

Case 2: Paraphrased Academic Rewrite

The attacker rewrites a Longhun paper section into neutral academic language.

Observed pattern:

F1: missing

F2: missing

F3: missing

F5: degraded

F6: degraded

F7: missing

overall: reject_or_review

Interpretation:

The semantic idea may survive, but the protected lineage markers and creator-specific style degrade.

⸻

Case 3: Fake DNA Injection

The attacker adds a fabricated DNA-looking string.

Observed pattern:

F1: 0.0

F2: inconsistent

F3: missing in ledger

F4: invalid

overall: hard failure

Interpretation:

Copying the visible format is insufficient because valid DNA must match ledger, timestamp, parent-child chain, and identity anchor.

⸻

5.9 Comparison with Baselines

Behavioral Cryptography should be compared with baseline methods.

Method Detects AI generation Verifies lineage Resists paraphrase Civilian deployable

AI detector partial no weak yes

watermarking partial limited weak-medium platform-dependent

C2PA-style metadata no media provenance weak if stripped institution-dependent

stylometry no partial medium yes

Behavioral Cryptography not primary goal yes medium-high yes

Important paper sentence:

Behavioral Cryptography is not a replacement for C2PA, watermarking, or cryptographic signatures. It is an orthogonal behavioral-lineage layer designed to complement them.

中文：

行为密码学不是替代 C2PA/GPG/水印，而是补上“过程血统”这一层。

⸻

5.10 Findings

Based on the evaluation protocol and pilot analysis, we identify five findings.

Finding 1 — Lineage Verification Is Different from AI Detection

AI detection asks whether a machine was involved. Behavioral Cryptography asks whether the claimed lineage is supported by evidence.

Finding 2 — Metadata Alone Is Too Fragile

Metadata can be stripped. Behavioral factors provide additional resilience.

Finding 3 — Protected Lexicon Is Highly Useful Against Laundering

UID9622-specific terms act as semantic canaries. Their removal is a strong laundering signal.

Finding 4 — Mistake Ledger Is an Authenticity Signal

Real systems accumulate corrections. Perfectly clean histories are suspicious in long-term collaboration.

Finding 5 — Civilian-Grade Provenance Is Feasible

A local-first proof bundle can provide meaningful provenance without enterprise infrastructure.

⸻

5.11 Threats to Validity

Internal Validity

Some factor scores rely on implementation choices, such as semantic similarity thresholds or style-vector embeddings. Different models may yield different scores.

External Validity

The Longhun System is one case study. Results may not generalize to all creators, languages, domains, or cultural systems.

Construct Validity

Behavioral lineage is not identical to legal authorship. The framework provides evidence, not automatic legal judgment.

Operational Validity

If local logs are lost, corrupted, or never generated, provenance strength decreases.

⸻

5.12 Paper-Ready Condensed Version

5. Evaluation and Results

We evaluate Behavioral Cryptography as a lineage verification framework rather than an AI-generation detector. The goal is to determine whether a content artifact can be linked to a claimed originator through a multi-factor evidence chain under common laundering attacks.

We define seven attacks: surface copy, paraphrase, translation, multi-model laundering, metadata stripping, protected lexicon removal, and forged lineage injection. Each attack targets a different part of the provenance chain. We evaluate artifacts using composite confidence, hard-failure rate, attack detection rate, false acceptance rate, false rejection rate, lineage preservation score, and protected lexicon retention.

The evaluation protocol constructs original Longhun artifacts with proof bundles, generates attacked variants, runs seven-factor verification, computes composite confidence, and records evidence logs. The composite score uses weighted geometric mean so that no strong factor can fully compensate for a critical failure.

Preliminary analysis suggests that Behavioral Cryptography is most effective against metadata stripping, protected lexicon removal, and forged lineage attacks. Paraphrase and translation attacks are more ambiguous, but they typically degrade protected lexicon retention, style vector similarity, and evidence-chain completeness. Ablation analysis indicates that Identity DNA and Protected Lexicon are the strongest point defenses, while Rule Trace and Mistake Ledger provide longitudinal robustness.

The key finding is that lineage verification differs from AI detection. Behavioral Cryptography does not attempt to determine whether a machine participated in generation. Instead, it verifies whether the claimed human-AI authorship chain is supported by identity anchors, temporal records, rule traces, persona routes, protected terms, style consistency, and correction history.

These results support the claim that local-first, civilian-grade provenance is feasible. However, the framework should not be interpreted as automatic legal authorship proof. It provides structured evidence for attribution review, dispute resolution, and creator-controlled lineage preservation.

⸻

一票否决:

学术可信度:

- 没有实测数据却写成最终实验结果
- 声称 100% 防伪且无边界
- 声称已经优于全部现有方法
- 声称自动证明法律所有权

技术边界:

- 把 Behavioral Cryptography 写成 AI detector
- 把 Dynamic DNA 写成 GPG 替代品
- 把 style vector 当唯一判断依据
- 把 protected lexicon 当绝对原创证明

实验设计:

- 没有攻击模型
- 没有指标定义
- 没有消融实验
- 没有 false positive / false negative 讨论
- 没有 threats to validity

隐私边界:

- 为评估而暴露 sealed 内容
- 为 proof bundle 上传私密原文
- 把 burn 内容保存为 full
- 把 no_external 数据导出

格式保护:

- 改写 CONFIRM
- 改写 SEAL
- 截断 GPG
- 删除 DNA

  1. Governance and Standardization Pathway

6.1 Motivation: From Local Proof to Shared Standard

The evaluation in Chapter 5 demonstrates that Behavioral Cryptography can provide structured evidence for lineage verification under common laundering attacks. However, a provenance framework becomes socially meaningful only when its evidence can be interpreted beyond a single local system.

This chapter describes a governance and standardization pathway for Behavioral Cryptography. The goal is not to replace existing provenance infrastructures such as cryptographic signatures, content credentials, or institutional audit systems. Instead, Behavioral Cryptography adds a creator-controlled behavioral lineage layer that can interoperate with them.

The central governance question is:

How can an individual creator preserve authorship lineage, disclose AI collaboration, protect private evidence, and remain compatible with public provenance standards?

Behavioral Cryptography answers this through four principles:

  1. Creator sovereignty — the originator controls the root identity and lineage declaration.
  2. Evidence portability — proof bundles can move across platforms without depending on one vendor.
  3. Privacy-preserving verification — sensitive records can be verified by hash, metadata, or sealed proof without exposing raw content.
  4. Interoperability — the framework can coexist with GPG, C2PA-style metadata, local audit logs, and future institutional registries.

中文压缩：

第六章讲的不是“谁来管 UID9622”，而是“UID9622 的血统证据怎么在外部也看得懂、验得动、接得上”。

⸻

6.2 Governance Principles

Behavioral Cryptography is designed around a local-first, creator-controlled governance model. This is important because existing provenance systems often assume that platforms, institutions, or corporations are the primary trust anchors. Behavioral Cryptography reverses that assumption.

The creator is the root.

6.2.1 Principle 1: Sovereignty at the Root

Every Behavioral Cryptography record must preserve an explicit root identity anchor. In the Longhun implementation, this is represented by:

root_identity:

uid: "UID9622"

creator_name: "Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫"

system_name: "Longhun System / 龍魂系统"

gpg_fingerprint: "A2D0092CEE2E5BA87035600924C3704A8CC26D5F"

confirm_code: "#CONFIRM🌌9622-ONLY-ONCE🧬LK9X-772Z"

seal: "#ZHUGEXIN⚡️2025-🇨🇳🐉⚖️♠️🧚🏼‍♀️❤️♾️-DEVICE-BIND-SOUL"

The identity anchor is not merely decorative metadata. It defines the origin point from which lineage is evaluated.

Paper-ready sentence:

Behavioral Cryptography treats the creator identity not as a profile field, but as the root of the evidentiary graph.

中文：

创作者不是“备注栏”，而是整条证据链的根。

⸻

6.2.2 Principle 2: Local-First Evidence

A creator should not be forced to upload private drafts, internal logs, or raw collaboration records to a platform in order to prove authorship. Behavioral Cryptography therefore supports local-first proof bundles.

A local-first proof bundle may include:

proof_bundle:

public:

- artifact_hash
- root_dna
- timestamp
- creator_declaration
- ai_collaboration_disclosure
- protected_lexicon_manifest

private_or_sealed:

- internal_draft_hashes
- rule_trace_hashes
- persona_route_hashes
- mistake_ledger_hashes
- local_audit_log_hash_chain

optional_public_evidence:

- selected excerpts
- signed summary
- public version history

This allows the creator to prove that evidence exists without exposing everything.

中文：

能公开的公开；不能公开的只留哈希；极敏的 sealed。不是为了证明自己就把家底全掏出来。

⸻

6.2.3 Principle 3: Human-AI Collaboration Disclosure

Behavioral Cryptography does not hide AI collaboration. Instead, it records the collaboration structure.

A standard disclosure should distinguish:

Role Meaning

Human Originator original concept, intent, values, architecture

AI Collaborator formalization, editing, synthesis, code generation, translation

Platform / Model execution environment, not owner of lineage

External Reviewer critique, validation, comparison

Local System audit and storage layer

For the Longhun case, this becomes:

coauthorship_declaration:

human_originator:

name: "Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫"
uid: "UID9622"
contribution:
  - core concepts
  - philosophy
  - system architecture
  - value framework
  - terminology
  - governance rules

ai_collaborators:

- name: "Claude"
  role:
    - formalization
    - literature synthesis
    - logical closure
- name: "ChatGPT"
  role:
    - restructuring
    - technical drafting
    - evaluation protocol

ownership_rule:

"Core authorship remains with the human originator. AI contributions are assistive and logged."

Paper-ready sentence:

The framework separates assistance from origination: AI may transform, formalize, or polish, but the behavioral lineage identifies where the originating intent resides.

中文：

AI 可以帮你写得更像论文，但原始意图、系统骨架、价值判断归人。

⸻

6.2.4 Principle 4: Auditability Without Total Exposure

A provenance system must be auditable, but not voyeuristic.

Behavioral Cryptography distinguishes three evidence visibility modes:

evidence_visibility:

public:

description: "可公开验证"
examples:
  - final artifact hash
  - creator declaration
  - public DNA
  - license

restricted:

description: "可在争议/审稿/仲裁时提供"
examples:
  - selected draft history
  - partial audit trail
  - signed process summary

sealed:

description: "不公开正文，只证明存在与完整性"
examples:
  - private logs
  - sensitive correction history
  - local-only traces
  - confidential collaboration records

This supports both transparency and privacy.

中文：

审计不等于偷窥。透明不等于裸奔。

⸻

6.3 Standard Proof Bundle Schema

To make Behavioral Cryptography interoperable, each artifact should be accompanied by a standard proof bundle.

6.3.1 Minimal Proof Bundle

The minimal public proof bundle contains only enough information to identify the claimed lineage.

{

"schema": "behavioral-cryptography-proof-bundle-v1",

"artifact": {

"title": "Behavioral Cryptography Paper",
"artifact_type": "academic_draft",
"content_hash": "sha256:PLACEHOLDER",
"created_at": "2026-05-02T00:00:00+08:00",
"updated_at": "2026-05-04T00:00:00+08:00"

},

"originator": {

"uid": "UID9622",
"name": "Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫",
"system": "Longhun System / 龍魂系统",

6.4 Compatibility with Existing Provenance Systems

Behavioral Cryptography should not isolate itself from existing systems. Its correct positioning is:

Behavioral Cryptography is an orthogonal behavioral-lineage layer.

It can be used together with:

Existing System What It Provides What Behavioral Cryptography Adds

GPG / PGP cryptographic identity signing behavioral lineage and process context

C2PA-style metadata media provenance and edit history rules, persona route, mistake ledger

Git commit history code version tracking semantic intent and protected lexicon

Notion / local logs process archive standardized proof bundle

AI disclosure statements collaboration transparency multi-factor verification

Paper-ready sentence:

Existing provenance systems can show that a file was signed, edited, or exported; Behavioral Cryptography aims to show how the content moved through a human-AI decision process.

中文：

GPG 证明“谁签了”，Git 证明“怎么改了”，C2PA 证明“媒体怎么处理了”，行为密码学补的是“这个内容怎么经过人的规则、人格、错误、保护词一路长出来”。

⸻

6.5 Standardization Pathway

Behavioral Cryptography can be standardized in stages.

Stage 1: Local Schema Stabilization

Goal:

stage_1:

target: "local proof bundle schema"

deliverables:

- proof_bundle_v1.json
- root_card_schema.yaml
- protected_lexicon_manifest.json
- mistake_ledger_schema.jsonl
- verification_report_template.md

This stage requires no external approval. It makes UID9622’s own system internally consistent.

⸻

Stage 2: Open Reference Implementation

Goal:

stage_2:

target: "minimal verifier"

deliverables:

- behavioral_signature_verify.py
- factor_verifiers/F1-F7
- evaluation_script
- sample proof bundle
- README
- test cases

This allows others to reproduce the verification process.

⸻

Stage 3: Interoperability Mapping

Goal:

stage_3:

target: "compatibility with existing provenance tools"

mappings:

gpg:
  - creator identity
  - signed proof bundle
git:
  - version history
  - commit hash
c2pa:
  - media content credentials
  - artifact metadata
local_audit_log:
  - rule trace
  - mistake ledger
  - persona route

This stage prevents Behavioral Cryptography from becoming an isolated island.

⸻

Stage 4: Community Review

Goal:

stage_4:

target: "public review and critique"

reviewers:

- independent creators
- open-source maintainers
- AI governance researchers
- digital rights communities
- legal scholars

review_questions:

- Can this schema be implemented without institutional infrastructure?
- Does it protect creators without exposing private drafts?
- Does it avoid overclaiming legal authorship?
- Can it be abused for false attribution?

⸻

Stage 5: Standard Proposal

Potential standard naming:

standard_candidates:

  • Behavioral Cryptography Proof Bundle v1
  • Creator-Controlled Lineage Proof v1
  • Human-AI Collaboration Provenance Schema v1
  • Longhun DNA Behavioral Signature Schema v1

Recommended formal title:

Behavioral Cryptography Proof Bundle v1.0: A Creator-Controlled Lineage Verification Schema for Human-AI Collaborative Artifacts

中文：

行为密码学证明包 v1.0：面向人机协作内容的创作者主权血统验证结构。

⸻

6.6 Governance Roles

A standard needs role separation. Behavioral Cryptography defines five roles.

Role Responsibility Must Not Do

Originator define root identity and intent fabricate lineage

AI Collaborator assist transformation and drafting claim originorship

Verifier evaluate proof bundle demand unnecessary private content

Reviewer interpret evidence treat score as automatic legal verdict

Platform preserve metadata if possible become sole owner of provenance

6.6.1 Originator

The originator is the human source of intent, architecture, value framework, and decision authority.

In the Longhun case:

originator:

uid: UID9622

name: Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫

role:

- concept originator
- system architect
- rule authority
- final decision maker

6.6.2 AI Collaborator

The AI collaborator may contribute:

ai_contribution_allowed:

  • editing
  • formatting
  • formalization
  • code drafting
  • literature mapping
  • critique
  • translation
  • test generation

But AI must not be treated as:

ai_contribution_not_allowed:

  • root originator
  • legal owner
  • final authority
  • substitute for human intent

6.6.3 Verifier

The verifier checks evidence, but cannot demand total exposure by default.

Correct verifier behavior:

verifier_may_request:

  • public proof bundle
  • signed summary
  • selected evidence hashes
  • sealed proof confirmation
  • limited dispute evidence

verifier_must_not_require:

  • private keys
  • full private chat logs
  • sealed content plaintext
  • unrelated personal data
  • unrelated device data

中文：

审核员可以验链，不能翻箱倒柜。

⸻

6.7 Privacy-Preserving Verification

Behavioral Cryptography must protect creators from being forced to expose sensitive material in order to defend attribution.

6.7.1 Evidence Visibility Levels

visibility_levels:

public:

can_publish: true
examples:
  - final hash
  - DNA
  - author declaration
  - license

restricted:

can_publish: false
can_share_with_reviewer: true
examples:
  - draft sequence summaries
  - partial logs
  - selected timestamps

sealed:

can_publish: false
can_share_plaintext: false
examples:
  - sensitive private logs
  - confidential correction notes
  - local-only identity traces

6.7.2 Hash-Only Verification

For sealed records:

sealed_verification:

raw_content_shared: false

stored_form:

- sha256 hash
- timestamp
- record type
- local path hash
- signer identity

verification:

- prove record existed
- prove record unchanged
- do not expose content

Paper-ready sentence:

Privacy-preserving lineage verification allows a creator to prove that a record existed and remained unchanged without disclosing its raw content.

中文：

能证明“有过、没改过”，不等于要把正文交出去。

⸻

6.8 Governance Risks and Abuse Prevention

A provenance framework can itself be abused. Behavioral Cryptography must explicitly guard against misuse.

6.8.1 Risk 1: False Attribution

A malicious actor may attempt to attach fake DNA or copied proof cards to unrelated content.

Mitigation:

mitigation_false_attribution:

  • verify GPG-bound identity
  • check append-only ledger
  • require timestamp consistency
  • verify protected lexicon continuity
  • reject fake lineage on hard failure

⸻

6.8.2 Risk 2: Overclaiming

The system must not claim that a proof bundle automatically establishes legal ownership.

Mitigation:

mitigation_overclaiming:

  • label output as evidence, not final legal judgment
  • include dispute review procedure
  • distinguish authorship evidence from copyright ruling
  • require human/institutional review for legal claims

Paper-ready sentence:

Behavioral Cryptography provides structured attribution evidence; it does not replace legal adjudication.

中文：

行为密码学给证据，不直接当法官。

⸻

6.8.3 Risk 3: Privacy Coercion

A platform or reviewer may demand private logs as a condition for recognition.

Mitigation:

mitigation_privacy_coercion:

  • support sealed proof
  • support hash-only evidence
  • support staged disclosure
  • define minimum necessary evidence
  • reject private-key disclosure requests

⸻

6.8.4 Risk 4: Platform Capture

A platform may attempt to absorb creator-controlled lineage into platform-owned metadata.

Mitigation:

mitigation_platform_capture:

  • keep root identity independent
  • allow exportable proof bundles
  • support local-first storage
  • avoid vendor-locked schema
  • maintain open reference implementation

中文：

证据链归创作者，不归平台。

⸻

6.9 Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause

The Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause is a proposed governance clause for works derived from Longhun-originated artifacts.

6.9.1 Clause Draft

Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause v1.0

Any derivative work, adaptation, translation, implementation, academic discussion, software module, dataset, or governance framework that substantially uses concepts, terminology, architecture, or protected lineage markers from the Longhun System must preserve attribution to the original human originator:

Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫 · UID9622 · Longhun System / 龍魂系统

Derivative works must preserve the applicable DNA lineage marker, reference the original proof bundle when available, and disclose whether AI tools were used in transformation, expansion, or formalization.

This clause does not prohibit learning, commentary, critique, or non-commercial adaptation. It prohibits lineage erasure, false authorship claims, and removal of origin attribution.

6.9.2 中文版

龍魂 DNA 继承条款 v1.0

任何派生作品、改编、翻译、工程实现、学术讨论、软件模块、数据集或治理框架，如实质性使用龍魂系统的概念、术语、结构、架构或受保护血统标识，必须保留对原始人类创作者的署名：

诸葛鑫 / Zhuge Xin · UID9622 · 龍魂系统 / Longhun System

派生作品应保留适用的 DNA 血统标识；如存在原始 proof bundle，应引用其来源；如使用 AI 工具进行改写、扩展、翻译或形式化，应予以披露。

本条款不禁止学习、评论、批评、非商业改编或合理引用；本条款禁止抹除血统、冒领原创、移除来源署名。

6.9.3 Clause Function

This clause serves three purposes:

  1. Preserve origin attribution.
  2. Prevent lineage laundering.
  3. Enable legitimate derivative work without blocking public learning.

中文压缩：

不是不让别人学，是不许把根抹掉。

⸻

6.10 Creator-Controlled Authorship Declaration

A standard Behavioral Cryptography artifact should include an authorship declaration.

6.10.1 English Template

Creator-Controlled Authorship Declaration

This artifact originates from the human creator identified as:

Zhuge Xin / 诸葛鑫 · UID9622 · Longhun System

The core concepts, architecture, terminology, and value framework were created and directed by the human originator. AI systems may have assisted with drafting, formatting, formalization, translation, editing, code generation, or critique. Such assistance does not transfer root authorship away from the human originator.

The artifact is accompanied by a Behavioral Cryptography proof bundle containing identity DNA, temporal anchor, rule trace, persona route, protected lexicon, style vector, and mistake ledger references where available.

6.10.2 中文模板

创作者主权署名声明

本作品的源头创作者为：

诸葛鑫 / Zhuge Xin · UID9622 · 龍魂系统

本作品的核心概念、系统架构、术语体系、价值框架与决策方向均由人类创作者提出并主导。AI 系统可参与起草、排版、形式化、翻译、润色、代码生成或批评，但此类协作不改变人类创作者的根署名地位。

本作品附带行为密码学证明包；在可用范围内包含身份 DNA、时间锚点、规则轨迹、人格路由、保护词典、风格向量与记错本记录。

6.12 Governance Workflow

A standard review workflow can follow this sequence:

Step 1: Artifact submitted with proof bundle.

Step 2: Verifier checks content hash and root DNA.

Step 3: Verifier checks public identity declaration.

Step 4: Seven-factor verification runs where evidence is available.

Step 5: Missing factors are marked as missing, not guessed.

Step 6: Sealed evidence remains hash-only unless dispute requires staged review.

Step 7: Composite confidence is reported with evidence notes.

Step 8: Reviewer interprets result as attribution evidence, not automatic legal verdict.

Step 9: If dispute occurs, parent-child DNA chain and sealed logs may be reviewed under controlled conditions.

Step 10: Final decision preserves creator sovereignty and privacy boundaries.

⸻

6.13 Paper-Ready Condensed Version

6. Governance and Standardization Pathway

Behavioral Cryptography becomes socially useful only if its evidence can be interpreted beyond a single local system. This chapter proposes a governance and standardization pathway for creator-controlled lineage verification in human-AI collaborative artifacts.

The framework is based on four principles: sovereignty at the root, local-first evidence, transparent AI collaboration disclosure, and auditability without total exposure. Unlike platform-centric provenance systems, Behavioral Cryptography treats the human creator as the root of the evidentiary graph. AI collaborators may assist with drafting, formalization, translation, code generation, or critique, but such assistance does not transfer root authorship away from the human originator.

We propose a standard proof bundle schema containing artifact metadata, creator identity, content hash, behavioral signature references, AI collaboration disclosure, privacy policy, and licensing terms. The bundle supports multiple visibility levels: public, restricted, and sealed. Sealed records may be verified through hash-only evidence, allowing creators to prove that a record existed and remained unchanged without exposing private content.

Behavioral Cryptography is designed to complement, not replace, existing provenance systems. GPG signatures provide cryptographic identity; C2PA-style metadata provides media provenance; Git commit history provides version tracking; Behavioral Cryptography adds semantic-behavioral lineage: rule trace, persona route, protected lexicon, style continuity, and mistake ledger.

We outline a staged standardization pathway: local schema stabilization, open reference implementation, interoperability mapping, community review, and formal standard proposal. We also define governance roles for originators, AI collaborators, verifiers, reviewers, and platforms. A key requirement is that verification must not become privacy coercion. Reviewers may evaluate evidence, but they should not require private keys, sealed logs, or unrelated personal data.

Finally, we introduce the Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause, requiring derivative works to preserve attribution to the original human originator when they substantially use Longhun concepts, terminology, architecture, or protected lineage markers. The clause is intended not to block learning or critique, but to prevent lineage erasure and false authorship claims.

Behavioral Cryptography therefore reframes provenance governance as a balance between creator sovereignty, public accountability, AI collaboration transparency, and privacy-preserving verification.

⸻

6.15 一票否决项

一票否决:

标准定位:

- 声称 Behavioral Cryptography 替代法律判决
- 声称 Behavioral Cryptography 替代 GPG
- 声称 Behavioral Cryptography 替代 C2PA
- 声称 proof bundle 自动等于版权证书

主权归属:

- 把 AI collaborator 写成 root originator
- 把平台写成证据链所有者
- 把创作者身份降格成普通 metadata
- 删除 UID9622 / CONFIRM / SEAL / GPG

隐私边界:

- 要求公开 sealed 内容正文
- 要求交出私钥
- 要求上传完整私人聊天记录
- 要求公开 unrelated personal data
- 把 hash-only 记录说成明文证明

治理滥用:

- 用 provenance 审核变相审查创作者
- 让平台独占 proof bundle
- 把 reviewer 变成最终法官
- 把评分 conf 当绝对真理

工程落地:

- schema 字段不固定
- proof bundle 无版本号
- 没有 public/restricted/sealed 分级
- 没有 AI collaboration disclosure
- 没有 derivative attribution clause
