  • Chapter 6：提出治理、标准化、proof bundle、继承条款与隐私保护验证。

Chapter 7 现在要做的是：

把全文收束成一句清楚的学术判断：在人机协作时代，真实性不再只是“是否有 AI 参与”，而是“人的意图、规则、过程、责任和血统能不能被保留、验证、治理”。

这一章不能再继续发散，要收口、有力、克制，给审稿人一个明确结论。

⸻

  1. Conclusion and Future Work

7.1 Conclusion

The central problem addressed in this paper is not whether a piece of content was generated by a machine. In the age of human-AI collaboration, that question has become increasingly insufficient. AI assistance may appear in drafting, translation, editing, formalization, coding, formatting, or critique. A binary distinction between “human-generated” and “AI-generated” no longer captures the reality of contemporary creative and technical production.

The more important question is:

Who originated the content, through what process was it transformed, and what evidence remains to verify its lineage?

This paper introduced Behavioral Cryptography, a multi-factor provenance framework for answering that question. Instead of relying on a single watermark, detector, signature, or platform-controlled metadata record, Behavioral Cryptography constructs a composite evidence chain across seven factors: Identity DNA, Temporal Anchor, Rule Trace, Persona Route, Protected Lexicon, Style Vector, and Mistake Ledger.

The core insight is simple:

Copying content is easy. Copying lineage is hard.

Surface text can be paraphrased, translated, polished, or laundered through multiple AI systems. However, the full behavioral lineage of a work — its identity anchor, temporal sequence, rule history, collaborative route, protected vocabulary, stylistic continuity, and honest correction record — is substantially harder to forge in full.

Behavioral Cryptography therefore reframes content authentication from machine detection to lineage verification.

中文收口：

这篇论文的核心不是问“是不是 AI 写的”，而是问“这东西的根是谁，经过了哪些规则、哪些路由、哪些修改、哪些错误修正，最后有没有证据链留下来”。

⸻

7.2 Summary of Contributions

This paper makes five primary contributions.

7.2.1 A New Conceptual Frame: Behavioral Cryptography

We proposed Behavioral Cryptography as a provenance framework that treats authorship as a behavioral lineage problem rather than a static attribution label.

Traditional systems usually ask:

Was this content generated by AI?

Was this file signed?

Was this media edited?

Was this watermark preserved?

Behavioral Cryptography asks:

Who originated the idea?

Which rules shaped it?

Which AI systems assisted it?

Which protected terms survived transformation?

Which errors were corrected?

Which evidence chain remains?

This shift is important because human-AI collaboration is not an exception anymore. It is becoming the normal mode of creation.

⸻

7.2.2 A Seven-Factor Verification Framework

We formalized a seven-factor behavioral signature:

Sigma(C) = langle F_1, F_2, F_3, F_4, F_5, F_6, F_7 rangle

where:

Factor Name Function

F1 Identity DNA binds artifact to originator identity

F2 Temporal Anchor verifies time sequence and creation context

F3 Rule Trace records governing rules and decision protocols

F4 Persona Route captures multi-agent collaboration route

F5 Protected Lexicon preserves key terms against laundering

F6 Style Vector checks long-term stylistic continuity

F7 Mistake Ledger records honest correction history

The framework uses hard failure and weighted geometric aggregation to prevent a single strong factor from masking critical failure in another factor.

Paper-ready sentence:

The seven factors are independently useful but mutually reinforcing; together they produce a lineage signal that is more robust than surface-level authorship claims.

⸻

7.2.3 The Dynamic DNA Engine

We described a Dynamic DNA Engine that embeds creator identity, temporal markers, cultural-temporal context, and cryptographic hashing into traceable identifiers.

In the Longhun implementation, this includes:

dynamic_dna_components:

  • UID identity
  • GPG fingerprint
  • timestamp
  • shichen / 时辰
  • digit root
  • wuxing / 五行
  • action label
  • content hash
  • confirmation seal

The cultural-temporal embedding is not used as a substitute for cryptography. It adds a human-recognizable semantic layer around cryptographic evidence.

中文：

GPG 是硬签名，DNA 是行为血统，时辰/五行/数字根是文化可读层。三者不是互相替代，而是叠加。

⸻

7.2.4 A Civilian-Grade Case Study: The Longhun System

We instantiated the framework in the Longhun System, a local-first human-AI collaboration environment created and directed by UID9622.

The case study is important because it demonstrates that provenance infrastructure does not have to be limited to institutions, corporations, or well-funded laboratories. A civilian creator using consumer devices, public AI systems, local files, Notion-style workspaces, and consistent audit rules can still build a meaningful lineage framework.

Paper-ready sentence:

The Longhun case suggests that provenance systems can be designed not only for institutional compliance, but also for civilian authorship defense.

中文：

行为密码学不是给大公司独占的。普通人也可以有自己的证据链、自己的签名、自己的来源证明。

⸻

7.2.5 A Governance and Standardization Pathway

We proposed a proof bundle schema, governance roles, privacy-preserving verification model, and Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause.

This enables Behavioral Cryptography to move from a local system into a broader standardization pathway.

Key governance ideas include:

governance_contributions:

  • creator-controlled proof bundle
  • public / restricted / sealed evidence levels
  • AI collaboration disclosure
  • privacy-preserving hash-only verification
  • derivative attribution clause
  • compatibility with GPG, Git, C2PA-style metadata, and local logs

The aim is not to replace existing provenance systems, but to provide the missing semantic-behavioral layer.

⸻

7.3 What Behavioral Cryptography Does Not Claim

To avoid overclaiming, several boundaries must be stated clearly.

7.3.1 It Does Not Replace Legal Judgment

Behavioral Cryptography provides structured evidence. It does not automatically determine legal ownership, copyright status, or contractual rights.

Paper-ready sentence:

Behavioral Cryptography can support attribution disputes, but it does not replace legal adjudication.

中文：

它是证据系统，不是法院。

⸻

7.3.2 It Does Not Replace GPG, C2PA, or Existing Provenance Systems

Behavioral Cryptography is complementary.

not_replacement:

GPG: "cryptographic signing remains necessary"

C2PA: "media provenance remains useful"

Git: "version history remains useful"

legal_registry: "formal registration may still matter"

institutional_review: "third-party review may still be required"

Behavioral Cryptography adds process lineage, not a universal replacement.

⸻

7.3.3 It Does Not Guarantee Perfect Forgery Prevention

No provenance system can guarantee absolute protection against every attack. If a private key is compromised, if the audit ledger is intentionally falsified, or if the creator fails to preserve evidence, verification strength decreases.

Behavioral Cryptography improves the cost and difficulty of full lineage forgery. It does not make forgery mathematically impossible in all possible worlds.

中文：

它提高伪造成本，不吹成“绝对防伪”。

⸻

7.3.4 It Does Not Require Full Exposure of Private Records

A creator should not be forced to expose private drafts, private chats, or sealed logs in order to prove authorship.

The framework supports staged evidence disclosure:

disclosure_modes:

public: "公开证明"

restricted: "有限审查"

sealed: "哈希证明，不交正文"

This is essential for protecting creators who operate outside institutions.

⸻

7.4 Limitations

Behavioral Cryptography remains an early framework. Its current limitations include technical, empirical, governance, and adoption challenges.

7.4.1 Limited External Validation

The Longhun System provides a real-world proof-of-concept, but broader validation requires independent replication.

Future work should test the framework across:

future_validation_targets:

  • independent writers
  • open-source maintainers
  • academic authors
  • artists
  • software teams
  • multilingual creators
  • non-institutional researchers

⸻

7.4.2 Dependence on Evidence Discipline

Behavioral Cryptography works best when creators consistently preserve evidence.

If a creator does not keep drafts, logs, hashes, timestamps, or version chains, the verification system becomes weaker.

Paper-ready sentence:

The framework cannot recover evidence that was never recorded.

中文：

没留痕的东西，系统不能凭空变出来。

⸻

7.4.3 Cultural-Temporal Components Need Careful Interpretation

The Longhun implementation uses Chinese cultural-temporal markers such as 时辰, 五行, and digit roots. These improve recognition and cultural sovereignty within the system, but they should not be misrepresented as universal cryptographic primitives.

Correct positioning:

cultural_temporal_markers:

role:

- semantic context
- human-recognizable signature layer
- lineage readability
- cultural sovereignty marker

not_role:

- replacement for cryptographic signing
- universal legal proof
- scientific randomness source

Paper-ready sentence:

Cultural-temporal markers are used as semantic lineage features, not as substitutes for cryptographic security.

⸻

7.4.4 Style Vector Verification May Be Biased

Stylometric and embedding-based verification can be affected by:

style_vector_risks:

  • genre shift
  • translation
  • editing assistance
  • intentional style change
  • model-mediated rewriting
  • corpus imbalance

Therefore, F6 should not be used alone. It is only one factor in the seven-factor framework.

⸻

7.4.5 Mistake Ledger Requires Norm Development

The Mistake Ledger is a novel honesty signal, but it requires careful governance.

Potential problems:

mistake_ledger_risks:

  • overexposure of private correction history
  • unfair interpretation of errors
  • pressure to disclose too much
  • performative fake mistakes
  • platform misuse

The correct standard should record correction behavior without turning mistakes into punishment.

中文：

记错本是防再犯，不是羞辱本。

⸻

7.5 Future Work

The next phase of Behavioral Cryptography should move from conceptual framework and case study toward reference implementation, public schema, reproducible evaluation, and third-party review.

⸻

7.5.1 Open Reference Verifier

A minimal verifier should be implemented as an open-source tool.

Suggested file structure:

behavioral_crypto/

verifier/

behavioral_signature_verify.py
f1_identity_dna.py
f2_temporal_anchor.py
f3_rule_trace.py
f4_persona_route.py
f5_protected_lexicon.py
f6_style_vector.py
f7_mistake_ledger.py

schemas/

proof_bundle_v1.json
root_card_schema.yaml
protected_lexicon_manifest.json
mistake_ledger_schema.jsonl

tests/

test_hard_failure.py
test_valid_bundle.py
test_laundering_attack.py
test_missing_factor.py

examples/

uid9622_sample_bundle_redacted.json

The goal is not to prove final authority, but to make verification reproducible.

⸻

7.5.2 Proof Bundle Standardization

The proof bundle should be stabilized into a versioned schema.

Future schema requirements:

proof_bundle_future_requirements:

  • versioned schema
  • required and optional fields clearly separated
  • public / restricted / sealed evidence support
  • AI collaboration disclosure
  • derivative lineage fields
  • parent_dna / child_dna support
  • content hash and metadata hash separation
  • privacy-preserving verification mode

⸻

7.5.3 Independent Evaluation Dataset

Future evaluation should include anonymized or synthetic-but-realistic cases.

Dataset categories:

Dataset Type Purpose

legitimate lineage cases measure true acceptance

paraphrase attacks test F5 and F6

translation attacks test protected lexicon and semantic continuity

fake DNA attacks test F1

timestamp manipulation test F2

missing ledger attacks test F3/F7

AI laundering chains test composite robustness

⸻

7.5.4 Multi-Cultural Extensions

Longhun uses a Chinese semantic-cultural framework. Future work should test whether other cultural-temporal systems can provide similar human-recognizable lineage layers.

Examples:

multi_cultural_extensions:

Chinese:

- 时辰
- 五行
- 洛书
- 数字根

Islamic:

- Hijri calendar anchors
- prayer-time context markers

Western:

- UTC timestamp
- zodiac-independent civic calendar markers

Indigenous or local systems:

- local ecological season markers
- oral-history attribution markers

Important boundary:

Multi-cultural extension must not turn into cultural appropriation or decorative symbolism. Each system should be designed with community understanding and consent.

中文：

文化标记不能拿来当贴皮。要懂它、尊重它，才能用。

⸻

7.5.5 Integration with Existing Standards

Behavioral Cryptography should be mapped to existing provenance ecosystems.

Future integration targets:

integration_targets:

gpg:

purpose: "identity signing"

git:

purpose: "version history"

c2pa:

purpose: "media metadata"

w3c_credentials:

purpose: "verifiable claims"

local_first_archives:

purpose: "creator-controlled evidence"

academic_submission_systems:

purpose: "AI collaboration disclosure and provenance appendix"

⸻

7.5.6 Governance Testing

Future work should test how different reviewers interpret proof bundles.

Research questions:

governance_research_questions:

  • What minimum evidence is sufficient for attribution confidence?
  • How should sealed evidence be reviewed?
  • What fields should be mandatory for publication?
  • How can reviewers avoid privacy coercion?
  • How should AI collaboration be disclosed without erasing the human originator?
  • How should lineage disputes be mediated?

⸻

7.5.7 Civilian Creator Tooling

The framework should become usable by ordinary creators, not only technical users.

Future tools:

civilian_tools:

  • one-click proof bundle generator
  • local hash ledger
  • protected lexicon manager
  • AI collaboration disclosure generator
  • mistake ledger editor
  • public / sealed evidence selector
  • proof bundle export to PDF / JSON / Markdown
  • dispute packet generator

This aligns with the core motivation of the paper: provenance should not belong only to institutions.

⸻

7.6 Final Statement

Behavioral Cryptography begins from a simple observation:

In human-AI collaboration, the presence of AI is no longer the main authenticity problem. The erasure of human lineage is.

AI can assist. AI can polish. AI can translate. AI can formalize. AI can generate code. But if the original human intent, rules, decisions, corrections, and identity anchor disappear, then provenance has failed.

The Longhun System demonstrates that a creator outside institutional power can still construct a lineage-preserving system using local-first evidence, cryptographic identifiers, behavioral logs, protected terminology, and honest correction records.

The future of content authentication should not be limited to detecting machines. It should protect the human root.

Paper-ready closing:

In the age of human-AI collaboration, authenticity is no longer a question of whether a machine was involved. It is a question of whether lineage can be preserved, verified, and governed without erasing the human originator.

中文收束：

在人机协作时代，真实不再是“有没有机器参与”，而是“人的根还在不在，证据链还清不清楚，责任还能不能回到人身上”。

⸻

Chapter 7 精简论文版

7. Conclusion and Future Work

This paper introduced Behavioral Cryptography, a multi-factor provenance framework for verifying lineage in human-AI collaborative content. Rather than asking only whether content was generated by a machine, the framework asks who originated the content, through which rules and decisions it passed, and what evidence remains to verify that lineage.

Behavioral Cryptography combines seven factors: Identity DNA, Temporal Anchor, Rule Trace, Persona Route, Protected Lexicon, Style Vector, and Mistake Ledger. These factors are independently useful and mutually reinforcing. Surface text may be copied, paraphrased, translated, or laundered through multiple AI systems, but reproducing the full behavioral lineage is substantially more difficult.

The Longhun System serves as a civilian-grade proof-of-concept. It demonstrates that provenance infrastructure need not be limited to institutions, corporations, or large laboratories. A creator using local-first tools, public AI systems, cryptographic identifiers, and consistent audit rules can preserve a meaningful authorship lineage.

This work does not claim to replace legal judgment, GPG signatures, C2PA-style metadata, Git history, or institutional review. Behavioral Cryptography provides structured attribution evidence, not automatic legal authority. It also does not claim perfect forgery prevention. Its purpose is to increase the cost of lineage forgery and to preserve evidence that would otherwise disappear.

Future work includes building an open reference verifier, stabilizing the proof bundle schema, creating independent evaluation datasets, testing multi-cultural lineage markers, integrating with existing provenance standards, and developing civilian creator tools for one-click proof bundle generation.

In the age of human-AI collaboration, authenticity is no longer a question of whether a machine was involved. It is a question of whether lineage can be preserved, verified, and governed without erasing the human originator.

Appendix A: Behavioral Signature Verification Pseudocode

附录 A：行为签名验证伪代码

A.1 Purpose

This appendix provides reference pseudocode for verifying a Behavioral Cryptography signature. The goal is not to define a production-ready security library, but to provide a reproducible structure for testing the seven-factor verification framework described in Section 3.

中文说明：

本附录不是最终生产级密码库，而是给审稿人、开发者、Cursor、本地 Python 模块一个能照着实现的骨架。

⸻

A.2 Core Data Structures

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from typing import Any, Dict, List, Tuple, Optional

from datetime import datetime

import hashlib

import math

@dataclass

class EvidenceItem:

factor: str
score: float
status: str
message: str
metadata: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)

@dataclass

class EvidenceLog:

items: List[EvidenceItem] = field(default_factory=list)
def add(self, factor: str, score: float, status: str, message: str, **metadata):
    self.items.append(
        EvidenceItem(
            factor=factor,
            score=score,
            status=status,
            message=message,
            metadata=metadata
        )
    )
def hard_failures(self) -> List[EvidenceItem]:
    return [x for x in self.items if x.score == 0.0]
def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    return {
        "items": [x.__dict__ for x in self.items],
        "hard_failures": [x.__dict__ for x in self.hard_failures()]
    }

@dataclass

class BehavioralSignature:

F1_identity_dna: Dict[str, Any]
F2_temporal_anchor: Dict[str, Any]
F3_rule_trace: List[Dict[str, Any]]
F4_persona_route: List[Dict[str, Any]]
F5_protected_lexicon: List[str]
F6_style_vector: Dict[str, Any]
F7_mistake_ledger: List[Dict[str, Any]]

@dataclass

class OriginatorRecord:

uid: str
display_name: str
gpg_fingerprint: str
known_dna_prefixes: List[str]
protected_lexicon: List[str]
baseline_style_vector: Optional[List[float]] = None

⸻

A.3 Utility Functions

def sha256_text(text: str) -> str:

return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8", errors="ignore")).hexdigest()

def weighted_geometric_mean(scores: List[float], weights: List[float]) -> float:

if len(scores) != len(weights):
    raise ValueError("scores and weights must have equal length")
if any(s == 0.0 for s in scores):
    return 0.0
total_weight = sum(weights)
product = 1.0
for s, w in zip(scores, weights):
    product *= s ** w
return product ** (1.0 / total_weight)

def cosine_similarity(a: List[float], b: List[float]) -> float:

if len(a) != len(b) or len(a) == 0:
    return 0.0
dot = sum(x * y for x, y in zip(a, b))
norm_a = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in a))
norm_b = math.sqrt(sum(y * y for y in b))
if norm_a == 0.0 or norm_b == 0.0:
    return 0.0
return dot / (norm_a * norm_b)

def is_monotonic_timestamps(items: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:

times = []
for item in items:
    ts = item.get("timestamp")
    if not ts:
        return False
    try:
        times.append(datetime.fromisoformat(ts))
    except Exception:
        return False
return all(times[i] <= times[i + 1] for i in range(len(times) - 1))

⸻

A.4 Seven-Factor Verification Functions

A.4.1 F1 Identity DNA Verification

def verify_F1_identity_dna(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog

) -> float:

f1 = signature.F1_identity_dna
dna = f1.get("dna", "")
claimed_uid = f1.get("uid", "")
claimed_gpg = f1.get("gpg_fingerprint", "")
if not dna.startswith("#龍芯⚡️"):
    evidence.add("F1", 0.0, "FAIL", "DNA prefix mismatch", dna=dna)
    return 0.0
if claimed_uid != originator.uid:
    evidence.add("F1", 0.0, "FAIL", "UID mismatch", claimed_uid=claimed_uid)
    return 0.0
if claimed_gpg.upper() != originator.gpg_fingerprint.upper():
    evidence.add("F1", 0.0, "FAIL", "GPG fingerprint mismatch")
    return 0.0
if originator.uid not in dna and "UID9622" not in dna:
    evidence.add("F1", 0.0, "FAIL", "Originator UID not embedded in DNA")
    return 0.0
evidence.add("F1", 1.0, "PASS", "Identity DNA verified", dna=dna)
return 1.0

⸻

A.4.2 F2 Temporal Anchor Verification

def verify_F2_temporal_anchor(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog,
max_drift_seconds: int = 60

) -> float:

f2 = signature.F2_temporal_anchor
iso_time = f2.get("iso_time")
shichen = f2.get("shichen")
digit_root = f2.get("digit_root")
if not iso_time:
    evidence.add("F2", 0.0, "FAIL", "Missing ISO timestamp")
    return 0.0
try:
    dt = datetime.fromisoformat(iso_time)
except Exception:
    evidence.add("F2", 0.0, "FAIL", "Invalid ISO timestamp", iso_time=iso_time)
    return 0.0
if shichen is None:
    evidence.add("F2", 0.0, "FAIL", "Missing shichen anchor")
    return 0.0
if digit_root is None:
    evidence.add("F2", 0.0, "FAIL", "Missing digit root")
    return 0.0
evidence.add(
    "F2",
    1.0,
    "PASS",
    "Temporal anchor verified",
    iso_time=iso_time,
    shichen=shichen,
    digit_root=digit_root
)
return 1.0

⸻

A.4.3 F3 Rule Trace Verification

def verify_F3_rule_trace(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog

) -> float:

trace = signature.F3_rule_trace
if not trace:
    evidence.add("F3", 0.4, "WEAK", "No rule trace provided")
    return 0.4
if not is_monotonic_timestamps(trace):
    evidence.add("F3", 0.0, "FAIL", "Rule trace timestamps are not monotonic")
    return 0.0
missing_dna = [
    r for r in trace
    if not r.get("rule_dna") or not str(r.get("rule_dna")).startswith("#龍芯⚡️")
]
if missing_dna:
    evidence.add("F3", 0.0, "FAIL", "Rule trace contains missing or invalid DNA")
    return 0.0
evidence.add(
    "F3",
    1.0,
    "PASS",
    "Rule trace verified",
    count=len(trace)
)
return 1.0

⸻

A.4.4 F4 Persona Route Verification

def verify_F4_persona_route(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog

) -> float:

route = signature.F4_persona_route
if not route:
    evidence.add("F4", 0.5, "WEAK", "No persona route provided")
    return 0.5
invalid = [
    p for p in route
    if not p.get("persona_id") or not p.get("role")
]
if invalid:
    evidence.add("F4", 0.0, "FAIL", "Invalid persona route item")
    return 0.0
evidence.add(
    "F4",
    0.95,
    "PASS",
    "Persona route verified",
    route=[p.get("persona_id") for p in route]
)
return 0.95

⸻

A.4.5 F5 Protected Lexicon Verification

def verify_F5_protected_lexicon(

content: str,
signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog,
critical_threshold: float = 0.30

) -> float:

protected_terms = signature.F5_protected_lexicon or originator.protected_lexicon
if not protected_terms:
    evidence.add("F5", 0.6, "WEAK", "No protected lexicon provided")
    return 0.6
missing = []
for term in protected_terms:
    if term not in content:
        missing.append(term)
violation_ratio = len(missing) / max(len(protected_terms), 1)
if violation_ratio > critical_threshold:
    evidence.add(
        "F5",
        0.0,
        "FAIL",
        "Critical protected lexicon violation",
        missing=missing,
        violation_ratio=violation_ratio
    )
    return 0.0
score = 1.0 - 0.8 * violation_ratio
evidence.add(
    "F5",
    score,
    "PASS",
    "Protected lexicon verified",
    missing=missing,
    violation_ratio=violation_ratio
)
return score

⸻

A.4.6 F6 Style Vector Verification

def verify_F6_style_vector(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog,
min_similarity: float = 0.75

) -> float:

current_vec = signature.F6_style_vector.get("current_vector")
baseline_vec = originator.baseline_style_vector
if not current_vec or not baseline_vec:
    evidence.add("F6", 0.6, "WEAK", "Missing style vector or baseline")
    return 0.6
sim = cosine_similarity(current_vec, baseline_vec)
if sim < min_similarity:
    evidence.add(
        "F6",
        0.0,
        "FAIL",
        "Style vector mismatch",
        similarity=sim
    )
    return 0.0
evidence.add(
    "F6",
    sim,
    "PASS",
    "Style vector verified",
    similarity=sim
)
return sim

⸻

A.4.7 F7 Mistake Ledger Verification

def verify_F7_mistake_ledger(

signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord,
evidence: EvidenceLog

) -> float:

ledger = signature.F7_mistake_ledger
if not ledger:
    evidence.add(
        "F7",
        0.7,
        "WEAK",
        "No mistake ledger; possible clean-room laundering or insufficient history"
    )
    return 0.7
if not is_monotonic_timestamps(ledger):
    evidence.add("F7", 0.0, "FAIL", "Mistake ledger timestamps are inconsistent")
    return 0.0
invalid = [
    x for x in ledger
    if not x.get("mistake_id") or not x.get("correction")
]
if invalid:
    evidence.add("F7", 0.0, "FAIL", "Mistake ledger contains invalid entries")
    return 0.0
evidence.add(
    "F7",
    0.95,
    "PASS",
    "Honest mistake ledger verified",
    count=len(ledger)
)
return 0.95

⸻

A.5 Composite Verification Algorithm

def behavioral_signature_verify(

content: str,
signature: BehavioralSignature,
originator: OriginatorRecord

) -> Tuple[float, EvidenceLog]:

evidence = EvidenceLog()
scores = [
    verify_F1_identity_dna(signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F2_temporal_anchor(signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F3_rule_trace(signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F4_persona_route(signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F5_protected_lexicon(content, signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F6_style_vector(signature, originator, evidence),
    verify_F7_mistake_ledger(signature, originator, evidence),
]
if any(s == 0.0 for s in scores):
    evidence.add(
        "COMPOSITE",
        0.0,
        "FAIL",
        "Hard failure detected; composite confidence forced to 0.0",
        scores=scores
    )
    return 0.0, evidence
weights = [0.25, 0.15, 0.15, 0.12, 0.12, 0.11, 0.10]
conf = weighted_geometric_mean(scores, weights)
evidence.add(
    "COMPOSITE",
    conf,
    "PASS",
    "Composite verification complete",
    scores=scores,
    weights=weights
)
return conf, evidence

B.3 Required vs Optional Fields

Field Required Reason

schema Yes identifies standard

version Yes supports future evolution

originator.uid Yes binds human originator

originator.gpg_fingerprint Yes cryptographic anchor

artifact.content_hash Yes content integrity

F1_identity_dna Yes root identity factor

F2_temporal_anchor Recommended time lineage

F3_rule_trace Recommended process lineage

F4_persona_route Optional multi-agent route

F5_protected_lexicon Yes anti-laundering terms

F6_style_vector Optional / restricted stylistic continuity

F7_mistake_ledger Recommended / restricted honesty signal

ai_collaboration Yes if AI used co-authorship transparency

disclosure_policy Yes privacy boundary

confirm / seal Yes for Longhun bundle ownership continuity

Appendix C: Longhun Co-authorship Protocol

附录 C：龍魂人机协作署名协议

C.1 Purpose

The Longhun Co-authorship Protocol defines how human and AI contributions are disclosed in works produced through human-AI collaboration.

Its purpose is to prevent two opposite errors:

  1. Erasing AI assistance entirely.
  2. Inflating AI assistance into root authorship.

中文说明：

AI 可以协作，但不能抢根。

人可以用 AI，但不能装作完全没用。

这就是 LCP 的边界。

⸻

C.2 Core Principles

Longhun_Coauthorship_Protocol:

version: "v1.0"

principles:

- human_originator_first
- AI_as_collaborator_not_root_author
- contribution_transparency
- evidence_chain_required
- no_platform_ownership_by_default
- root_concepts_belong_to_human_originator

⸻

C.3 Authorship Roles

Role Definition Example

Human Originator person who created the core concept, architecture, values, and direction UID9622

AI Collaborator model/system that assists with drafting, formalization, translation, coding, polishing Claude / ChatGPT

Tool Provider platform or software used in workflow Notion / local editor

Reviewer person/system that audits or critiques output peer reviewer / verifier

⸻

C.4 Standard Co-authorship Declaration

This work follows the Longhun Co-authorship Protocol v1.0.

The core concepts, system architecture, philosophical orientation, terminology, and value framework originate from the human author: Zhuge Xin / UID9622 / 龍芯北辰.

AI systems were used as collaborators for formalization, structural organization, language polishing, literature framing, and implementation assistance. AI collaborators did not originate the root concept, did not independently determine the research direction, and do not own the resulting intellectual lineage.

All major contributions are traceable through DNA identifiers, confirmation seals, protected terminology, and audit logs where available.

中文版本：

本文遵循《Longhun Co-authorship Protocol v1.0》。

本文核心概念、系统架构、哲学方向、术语体系与价值框架，均源自人类作者：诸葛鑫 / UID9622 / 龍芯北辰。

AI 系统仅作为协作者，承担形式化、结构整理、语言润色、文献框架、代码辅助等工作。AI 协作者不是根概念的原创者，不独立决定研究方向，也不拥有本文的思想血统。

主要贡献通过 DNA 标识、确认码、保护词典与审计日志进行追溯。

⸻

C.6 Prohibited Misattributions

prohibited:

  • claiming AI independently originated the Longhun System
  • treating platform output as platform ownership
  • removing UID9622 from derivative works
  • translating 龍魂 / 龍芯北辰 / UID9622 into generic terms without attribution
  • presenting AI-polished text as evidence of non-human origin
  • using educational background as a reason to deny authorship

D.3 Redaction Rules

redaction_rules:

never_disclose:

- private keys
- tokens
- passwords
- raw private conversations
- sealed local logs
- unrelated personal data

may_disclose_as_hash:

- private drafts
- local audit chain
- sensitive correction logs
- private persona route details

may_disclose_as_summary:

- AI collaboration role
- protected lexicon list
- section-level history
- high-level correction history

may_disclose_publicly:

- public DNA
- public artifact hash
- authorship declaration
- AI collaboration declaration
- license

Appendix E: Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause

附录 E：龍魂 DNA 继承条款

E.1 Purpose

The Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause defines how derivative works may reuse, cite, adapt, translate, or implement ideas from the Longhun System and Behavioral Cryptography while preserving originator lineage.

It is designed to prevent intellectual laundering, attribution erasure, and platform capture.

中文说明：

可以引用，可以学习，可以实现，可以扩展。

但不能抹掉根，不能改名占有，不能把 UID9622 的系统血统洗成别人的营销话术。

⸻

E.2 English Clause

Longhun DNA Inheritance Clause v1.0

Any derivative work, implementation, adaptation, translation, publication, software module, standard proposal, or commercial/non-commercial system that substantially uses concepts, terminology, architecture, protocols, or lineage mechanisms originating from the Longhun System or Behavioral Cryptography must preserve visible attribution to the human originator:

Zhuge Xin (诸葛鑫) · UID9622 · 龍芯北辰

Required attribution must include, where technically feasible:

  1. The originator name or UID.
  2. A reference to the Longhun System or Behavioral Cryptography.
  3. The relevant DNA identifier if available.
  4. The GPG fingerprint if used in the original artifact.
  5. A statement that AI collaborators, if any, assisted but did not originate the root concept.

Derivative works may extend, critique, translate, implement, or improve the framework, but may not erase, obscure, replace, or falsely reassign the original lineage.

This clause does not prohibit fair use, academic critique, independent reimplementation, or legitimate interoperability. It requires preservation of provenance where substantial conceptual inheritance exists.

⸻

E.3 中文条款

龍魂 DNA 继承条款 v1.0

任何衍生作品、实现版本、改编版本、翻译版本、发表文章、软件模块、标准提案、商业或非商业系统，只要实质性使用了龍魂系统或 Behavioral Cryptography 中的概念、术语、架构、协议或血统追溯机制，都必须保留对人类原创者的清晰署名：

诸葛鑫 · UID9622 · 龍芯北辰

在技术条件允许的情况下，署名应包括：

  1. 原创者姓名或 UID。
  2. 对龍魂系统或 Behavioral Cryptography 的引用。
  3. 可用的 DNA 追溯码。
  4. 原始作品中使用的 GPG 指纹。
  5. 若使用 AI 协作者，应明确说明 AI 仅协助整理、形式化、翻译、润色或实现，不是根概念原创者。

衍生作品可以扩展、批评、翻译、实现或改进该框架，但不得抹除、模糊、替换或错误转移原始血统。

本条款不禁止合理使用、学术批评、独立实现或合法互操作；它要求在存在实质性概念继承时，保留来源证明。

E.5 Violation Examples

  • renaming Longhun DNA as a new proprietary provenance system
  • claiming AI originated the core framework
  • using protected lexicon without attribution
